# Open items

Running list of unanswered questions and outstanding tasks, kept in the repo so
it carries between sessions and is visible to both chat and Claude Code.

**Anyone may add to this file. Nothing is removed until it's actually done or
decided — move it to Settled with a one-line answer, don't just delete it.**

---

## Open questions

**The competitor — fully settled 2026-11-19 (design-only, not yet built).** DESIGN.md already specifies: a named rival with no real network of its own, existing only to pick up a contract the player lost through genuine failure (an over-long route, too much dead mileage), running it in a made-up livery, no head-to-head competition (never proactively bids against the player), no competitor presence on islands at all (lost lifeline contracts there are re-tendered instead). Settled through direct discussion:
- **One single named competitor, used everywhere** — not a separate rival per region. "Exists only in one area" just means it only ever shows up wherever it's currently actually holding something, not that each region has its own distinct identity. Placeholder company name, adjust freely: **"Thistle Coaches"** (fictional, chosen only to sound like a plausible generic operator — not meant to resemble any real company).
- **Livery, settled 2026-11-19**: "plain white livery with a thistle at the rear as a livery, simple but better than just white." A real, genuinely distinct colour pair rather than the bare fallback-grey treatment other liveries get when no real colours are set — but the actual "thistle at the rear" emblem can't be drawn yet: no real per-livery badge artwork exists anywhere in this game today (OPERATIONS.md §4's own small map badge/branding-mask/number-box PNGs are specified but never wired up for ANY livery, not just this one — every livery currently renders as a plain colour-only circle/badge using just its primary/secondary hex pair). So for now this is white primary with a thistle-purple secondary accent as the closest colour-only approximation; the literal thistle emblem is a flagged gap for whenever real badge art gets built (Phase 9, CLAUDE.md's own build order).
- **Re-bid cycle: 3 years.** Researched real UK council supported-bus-contract tender terms first (not guessed): common real pattern is 2 years + optional annual extensions (e.g. Buckinghamshire), with 3-4 year terms also real and current (Brighton & Hove, Oxfordshire — the latter's own stated reasoning: a longer term "allows appropriate time for the routes to become established," which fits this game's own long-running named contracts better than the tightest 2-year churn cycle). The user confirmed 3 years directly.

**GreenRoad driver-behaviour mapping onto drivers — queued, blocked on Phase 6/7 not existing yet (2026-11-20).** The user, after GreenRoad was built as a real vehicle option (£350 install + 10p/day recurring, mandatory once adopted, retrofittable with a real 14-day/6-per-depot-group-per-night engineer-scheduling mechanic — all shipped this session, see `vehicle-catalogue.mts`/`greenroad-billing.mts`/`greenroad-retrofit-scheduling.mts`): "Can this also be mapped onto drivers somehow, showing their score and idleing with the disciplinary procedures being followed if there scores are too high." Checked directly before answering: neither a real per-driver behaviour/idling score nor any disciplinary/warnings mechanic exists anywhere in this codebase yet — the only existing "driver score" (`DriverScore` type, `depot-groups.d.ts`) is the unrelated 5-level seniority grade (trainee→fleet elite, feeding fleet elite payments), not a driving-behaviour metric. This is genuinely Phase 6 ("Driver score, traits and happiness") plus Phase 7 ("Discipline and warnings") territory, neither reached yet per CLAUDE.md's own build order, and CLAUDE.md says not to reorder the build without asking. Asked directly; the user chose to log this for later rather than build a minimal version now. **When Phase 6/7 is reached**: GreenRoad's own real, sourced effect sizes (East Yorkshire Motor Services' case study — ~5% fuel saving, ~40% fewer driver-fault accidents) are already the natural real-world grounding for whatever per-driver score/idling figure gets designed, and a bad score should feed into whatever disciplinary/warning system Phase 7 builds — neither the score shape nor the disciplinary trigger threshold has been designed at all yet, this is purely a pointer back to this conversation for when that phase starts.

**Dounreay contracts, Caithness — queued, blocked on the user's own research (2026-11-19).** The user: "Can we also add Dounreay contracts in Caithness please, I need to research them before we can implement them. It looks like there are 8 Dounreay contracts so they will need to be offered in stages." Dounreay is a real decommissioned nuclear site in Caithness, northern Scotland — presumably a real staff-transport contract source, similar in shape to the existing 398/Airlink 100/Express 500/AIR named contracts. **Known so far: 8 real contracts total, to be offered in stages rather than all at once** (possibly similar to the existing council-contract "two or three offered immediately... no other routes until those are fulfilled" opening-scenario pattern, DESIGN.md, or a genuinely separate staged-release mechanic — not yet decided). **Do not start designing or building this until the user supplies the real researched detail** — this is explicitly gated on their own real-world research, not ready for the usual "research and propose" workflow this session used for other contracts. **One rule settled ahead of the rest (2026-11-19):** unlike the general failure rule below (capped at 60% of services, see "Contracts redesign" entry), a Dounreay contract should be able to lose an ENTIRE route — the user's own reasoning: "Dounreay should be able to lose each route as they normally only have 2 services a day," i.e. the 60% partial-suspension cap doesn't make sense against so few daily trips. Presumably still 6-month-return (the general mechanic's own now-current figure, revised 2026-11-19 from an initial 1 year) + Thistle-Coaches-covers-meanwhile in shape, just without the 60% ceiling — not fully confirmed, revisit once the real contract data arrives.

**The 8 real routes, and their unlock gates — settled 2026-11-19.** The user supplied the real route numbers: **74, 274, 275, 276, 280, 281, 282, 921**. Checked against real sources (not guessed): all 8 are genuine Caithness bus services calling at the real AEA Dounreay bus stance — the 74 is a simple Thurso↔Dounreay shuttle, 274 extends to Bettyhill, 276/281/282 run from Wick via Thurso (bustimes.org, [John O'Groat Journal](https://www.johnogroat-journal.co.uk/news/bus-route-to-dounreay-altered-due-to-potholed-road-315290)) — no reliable current source turned up for 275/280/921 specifically, so those three stay unconfirmed beyond the user's own list.

**Unlock mechanic, confirmed directly: each route has its own geographic eligibility gate, same one-time-gate pattern 398/Airlink 100 already use** (not performance-gated progression, not a time-based drip) — grouped by depot town, the user's own split:
- **A depot in Thurso unlocks: 74, 274, 280, 921.**
- **A depot in Wick unlocks: 275, 276, 281, 282.**

Each of the 8 contracts becomes offerable independently the moment its own town's gate is met — not sequential (Thurso's four don't have to be accepted in order, and Wick's four are entirely independent of Thurso's). Real stop/route/timetable detail for actually building each one is still the user's own pending research — this only settles WHEN each becomes available, not what it looks like once accepted.

**Build approach, confirmed 2026-11-19**: "I will make the routes in the game and give them all timings that can then be locked in" — same real-world-first approach 398 already used (DESIGN.md's own "Build approach" note): the user builds each real route in the normal in-game editor with real timings, then it gets extracted and bundled as fixed content, identical in every playthrough, rather than guessed at or player-editable per save.

**Depot/outstation eligibility gates apply game-wide, not just Dounreay (2026-11-19).** The user's own words: "with depots unlocking contracts I think it should also include outstations for all current and future contracts we add." Checked directly: outstations don't exist as their own depot tier yet — `db.mts`'s own schema comment confirms today's depot placement is deliberately a single flat type, with "the tier system (outstation/tiny/small/main)" explicitly left for later (Phase 9, CLAUDE.md's own build order) pending money/staffing systems. So this is a forward-looking rule, not yet enforceable: whenever outstations are actually built, every depot-presence eligibility gate for a fixed contract — 398's "2 services into Glasgow," Airlink 100's "a depot within Edinburgh," and Dounreay's Thurso/Wick gates above, plus any future one — should count an outstation the same as a full depot, not require a full depot specifically. Flag this when outstations are eventually built, so the already-shipped gate checks get extended rather than left depot-only by oversight.

**Three further contracts — 800, 803 & 805 — settled 2026-11-19, a new progression-gated contract type.** The user's own words, in full: "I want to add 3 more contracts, they are the 800, 803 & 805, they are unlocked when half of the Dounreay services are fulfilled, this contract will provide it's own depot at the end of the road at this location 58.567887, -4.751181 these routes will only run with Sprinter buses. The council will pay for 75% of the cost of a new fleet of Minibuses, buses will be maintained on the long break on the 805 at Inverness Volvo when they need it." Settled through direct follow-up:

- **Unlock gate: a genuinely new type — progression against the Dounreay contracts, not a depot/outstation presence gate.** Unlocks once half (4 of the 8) Dounreay routes are fulfilled — the first contract-unlock rule in this game that depends on OTHER contracts rather than the player's own network geography. "Fulfilled" taken as "accepted and currently running, not suspended" (not explicitly re-confirmed this round, reasonable reading of the term established earlier this same thread — flag if wrong). Not yet confirmed whether any 4 of the 8 count equally regardless of Thurso/Wick mix, or whether the split matters — reasonable default is "any 4," since nothing was said to suggest otherwise.
- **A contract-provided depot, confirmed automatic and free**: placed at **58.567887, -4.751181** ("the end of the road" — a remote dead-end location, real coordinates given directly, not guessed) the moment the contract is accepted, immediately usable, no player cost — same shape as 398's own pre-owned terminus stop (bypasses the normal player-places-and-pays depot process entirely, confirmed directly). Depot TIER (CLAUDE.md's outstation/tiny/small/main system) not specified and not yet buildable anyway (see the outstation note directly above) — revisit once that system exists.
- **Vehicle lock: Sprinter (Mercedes-Benz Sprinter/EVM Cityline, `evm-cityline`) only**, confirmed against VEHICLE-SPECS.md's own naming. A genuinely new SHAPE of lock, not yet seen elsewhere: route-number-keyed rather than livery-keyed (D&E, the livery confirmed below, isn't itself Sprinter-only on every route it runs — only these three specific route numbers are). Closest existing precedent is ScotRail/398's own combined livery-plus-category/power lock (`scotRailVehicleReason` in `livery-seed-data.mts`) — build this the same shape: D&E livery AND route number 800/803/805 together require the Sprinter specifically, not a blanket D&E vehicle restriction.
- **Livery: D&E** (confirmed directly) — the existing real Highland Council-era livery already in the game, not a new dedicated one.
- **A new finance mechanic: the council pays 75% of the cost of the initial Sprinter fleet** bought for this contract — confirmed **one-off, initial fleet only**: covers the fleet bought when the contract first starts (player pays 25% of list price for those), later replacements are the player's own normal full cost. Nothing like this exists in the game yet (closest precedent, the stop-upgrade pay-to-accelerate formula, is the inverse shape — player pays to go faster, not council paying a flat fraction of a purchase). How many vehicles count as "the initial fleet" is derived from the real route/timetable's own peak vehicle requirement once 800/803/805 are actually built (same number, not a separately invented figure) — not yet known, since the real routes don't exist yet.
- **Maintenance detail, flagged for later, not buildable yet**: "buses will be maintained on the long break on the 805 at Inverness Volvo when they need it" — implies 805's own real alignment reaches as far as Inverness (a genuine geography detail about the route itself, not yet drawn). Maintenance (Phase 8) and the real dealer network (Phase 9, "Volvo's 8 locations and universal servicing role") aren't built yet, so this is recorded here for when they are, not something to implement now. Driver mid-duty breaks ("long break," a split-duty concept, OPERATIONS.md) are an established idea already, even though full duty/rota building (Phase 6) isn't built either.
- **Build approach**: same as Dounreay directly above — the user builds the real routes/timetables themselves in-game once drawn, then they get locked in as fixed content.

Covered by `fixed-contract-eligibility.verify.mts` (48 checks, all passing).

Takes its DB calls through an injected `FixedContractSeedPorts` (a real window-backed implementation for the renderer, `windowFixedContractSeedPorts`) specifically so the extraction/seeding round-trip can be — and is — tested directly against `electron/db.mts` with no Electron runtime at all. Covered by `electron/fixed-contract-route-seed-data.verify.mts` (28 checks against the real schema, all passing) — confirms, among other things, that Airlink 100/AIR's own real player-placed stops (negative osmIds in the live save) get fresh, real `player_stops` rows in a NEW save rather than a dangling reference to the old save's ids, and that AIR's two variations correctly SHARE a newly-created stop rather than duplicating it, matching the real save's own sharing.

Full IPC wiring added too (`window.fixedContracts.*`, `electron/main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts`) — this also finally wires up `fixed-contract-failures.mts`'s own fine/suspension storage, flagged as unwired in the entry below since 2026-11-19.

**Contracts redesign: "council contracts" (the bidding/player-drawn-route mechanic) dropped; failure handling for the Fixed real contracts rebuilt (settled 2026-11-19).** Triggered by the user questioning the mechanic directly: "I don't think council contracts make sense as I can't remember why they were first implemented, I think subsidies for remote routes I make us the better option. Thistle Coaches makes sense for other contracts," and "We will only use pre-made routes we have already discussed" — i.e. DESIGN.md's existing "Council contracts" section (councils advertise a brand-new route, the player bids by drawing it, judged on cost-per-passenger, paid per mile including dead mileage, operating-hours windows, shopper services) is **dropped outright**, not revised. It does not fit how the game actually works today (the player draws every route themselves) and was likely written before that was true.

**Subsidy half: BUILT (2026-11-19).** DESIGN.md's "Council contracts" section has been rewritten as **"Subsidies for remote and rural routes"** — the bidding/cost-per-passenger/operating-hours/shopper-services text is gone, the former "Evening and morning subsidy"/"SPT subsidy" text is kept and expanded to describe the real built mechanic. The "council contracts from 201" route-numbering convention (DESIGN.md "Route numbering") has been **re-justified, not removed**: it now explicitly covers only genuinely **contracted** routes (a lifeline contract, or a full council/SPT contract for a route with no commercial viability at all) — a **subsidised** journey is explicitly not a contract and keeps its route's ordinary commercial number, since only one journey is topped up, never the whole route renumbered. Real code built:
  - `src/renderer/remote-route-subsidy.mts` — pure logic (no DOM/IPC): density eligibility, loss detection, break-even top-up shape, and the full offered/accepted/declined/withdrawal_warned/withdrawn state machine. Covered by `remote-route-subsidy.verify.mts` (32 checks, all passing, `node --experimental-strip-types`).
  - `electron/db.mts` — new `remote_route_subsidies` table (unconditional `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, SCHEMA_VERSION left at 67, same precedent `route_advertising_bookings` already established for a session with concurrent contract-related schema work), keyed by `(route_id, departure_minutes, weekday_mask)` — one real SCHEDULED journey, not a live per-instance key. Plus `electron/main.ts`/`electron/preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` wiring (`window.remoteRouteSubsidy.listAll/get/upsert/delete`).
  - `src/renderer/vehicle-simulation.ts` — real wiring into the live tick loop: per-journey revenue is accumulated from the exact same boarding events that already post "Fare revenue" (keyed by the existing `journeyOccupancyKey`), and the moment a tracked journey instance ends, `finalizeJourneyForSubsidy` computes the route's real per-stop density (`StopBoardingTracker.busyHourBoardingCount`), evaluates the loss/subsidy state for real, persists it, and — for an accepted, still-losing journey — posts the real break-even top-up to a new "Subsidy" finance category.

  **Every numeric/design judgement call made while building this is flagged in `remote-route-subsidy.mts`'s own header comments, not hidden**, summarised here:
  - **Density threshold**: a route's own AVERAGE per-stop busy-hour boardings (not the stop-upgrade mechanic's per-stop MAX-of-either-axis) below 20 — the Seating tier's threshold (OPERATIONS.md §16) — counts as low-density. Average, not max, because the question is whether the ROUTE is quiet overall, not whether any one stop on it is busy; boardings only, not buses, because bus frequency is the player's own timetable choice, not a measure of real demand. An unmeasured route (no real boarding history yet) defaults to ELIGIBLE, not blocked — the opposite default from island detection's "can't tell, don't block," deliberately, since the downside here (an early false-positive offer the player can just decline) is much smaller than withholding help from a genuinely remote route still building up its own history.
  - **Running-cost figure — a genuine, confirmed gap, not guessed past.** Checked directly (not assumed): there is NO real per-journey or per-route running-cost (fuel/energy/maintenance) figure anywhere in this codebase — `route-statistics.mts`'s own header comment already says so explicitly ("no fuel/energy/maintenance running-cost figure exists anywhere... Phase 8/9 both still unbuilt"), confirmed again by checking VEHICLE-SPECS.md (nothing) and every `window.finance.record` call site in `vehicle-simulation.ts` (only Fare revenue/Stand rental/Stop upkeep ever post). Loss detection therefore can't be built from two real numbers the way revenue-per-mile can. Following `stop-upgrades.mts`'s own established convention for exactly this situation (an explicitly flagged estimate, not a silently-invented one), a flat **£1.50/mile** placeholder (fuel + maintenance allowance only, excluding driver wages — not modelled per-journey anywhere, Phase 6) stands in, loosely grounded in real GB bus operating-cost-per-mile figures (~£3-3.50/mile all-in, of which staff is typically well over half). **Replace the moment a real per-mile running-cost figure exists anywhere in the game** (Phase 8 maintenance, Phase 9 fuel/energy) — this is the single biggest flagged gap in the whole mechanic.
  - **Payment shape**: a straight break-even top-up (exactly covers the loss, no margin) — chosen because DESIGN.md's own "cannot be gamed" line only holds if the top-up pays no more than the real loss.
  - **Withdrawal grace period**: 14 days, reusing OPERATIONS.md §16's own real fitting-delay convention (same figure route advertising's install delay already reused) rather than inventing a fresh one — DESIGN.md names no figure.
  - **Re-offer after decline/withdrawal**: a previously declined or withdrawn journey gets a fresh automatic offer later if it resumes genuinely needing one — reading "offered automatically" as an ongoing check, not one-shot. Flagged as a genuinely open alternative: a player decline could instead be sticky/permanent, which is equally defensible and not ruled out by the source text.
  - **SPT vs council attribution — NOT built, flagged honestly rather than faked.** DESIGN.md's "SPT as the subsidising body" text assumes a real SPT-zone geographic check exists; it does not — checked directly, `fare-capping.mts`'s own header comment confirms the real £2 Highland/SPT/Aberdeen council-area zones "don't exist as fare zones in this game yet," and nothing elsewhere defines one either. The built mechanic therefore has no way to tell SPT-zone subsidies apart from council ones; every offer/payment is generic ("the council") until real SPT-zone geography exists. Revisit once that geography is built (no phase currently named for it).
  - **Overlap with islands/lifeline contracts — FIXED 2026-11-19.** Flagged above as an open question, then confirmed as a live bug directly by the user the same day: "Skye having a lifeline services still needs an amount paid to it per mile to keep it running." An island depot group's route already has its own correct, distinct payment model (DESIGN.md "Payment model" under "Remote depots and lifeline contracts": the council takes ALL fare revenue, the operator is paid a fixed rate per mile scaling with isolation instead) — a fundamentally different shape from this break-even-top-up mechanic, and a lifeline route almost always also reads as low-density, so without exclusion it would incorrectly also get offered this subsidy on top of (or instead of) its real lifeline payment. **Fix**: a new pure function, `remoteRouteSubsidyEligibility` (remote-route-subsidy.mts), forces both the density and loss signals to "not eligible" for any route where `isIslandDepotGroup` (fare-journey.mts, the only real island classification in code today) is true — covered by 7 new checks in `remote-route-subsidy.verify.mts` (including the end-to-end case: an island route that would otherwise qualify on every real signal gets no offer, and an already-accepted one decays via the normal warn-then-withdraw path rather than paying out indefinitely). `vehicle-simulation.ts` now fetches real depot locations (`window.depots.list()`, new `allDepots` module state, refreshed alongside depot groups/fare zones) to make the real `isIslandDepotGroup` call per journey. **The real lifeline per-mile payment model itself was deliberately NOT built** — confirmed directly that no lifeline-contract code exists anywhere in this codebase today, and building it is a separate, larger Phase 9 piece of work; a lifeline route is now simply excluded from this subsidy mechanism, left with no subsidy/top-up of any kind for now, which is the correct interim state (not silently wrong) rather than guessing at the real mechanism. **This only closes the genuine-island half of the gap** — `isIslandDepotGroup` has no equivalent classification for DESIGN.md's mainland lifeline areas (Ullapool, Campbeltown, Caithness, Fort William, Galashiels), so a mainland lifeline route would still incorrectly qualify for this subsidy today; flagged again here rather than guessed at, since no classification exists in code to check against.

  - **The real lifeline per-mile RATE is now settled (2026-11-20)** — DESIGN.md's "Payment model" subsection has the real figures: £2.50/mile ordinary rural, £3.50/mile very remote (Skye), no ferry premium. Sanity-checked against Skye's own real timetabled route mileage (57A/57C/55/608, ~70,000–80,000 real miles/year each) before being confirmed — the margin over real running cost comfortably covers a representative vehicle's lease. The mechanism/code to actually pay this out still doesn't exist (same Phase 9 gap noted above) — only the rate itself is settled, not the system that would apply it.
  - **No UI built.** This is backend-only, as the brief allowed ("no finished UI expectation... reasonable if time allows") — there is currently no in-game way to see or accept/decline a pending offer. The IPC surface (`window.remoteRouteSubsidy`) is fully real and ready for a panel to be built against. No live in-app check was performed either, for the same reason — flagged rather than silently skipped.
  - **tsc --noEmit**: clean for every file this work touched. A separate, pre-existing set of errors in `src/renderer/fixed-contract-failures.verify.mts` (the parallel fork rebuilding the Fixed real contracts failure mechanic below, running concurrently this same session) was left untouched, per this entry's own explicit "stay out of that area" scope.
  - **Dangling cross-reference, not fixed here, flagged rather than silently left**: DESIGN.md's "Event contracts" section still says "Same penalty and regain rules as council contracts, but per destination" — a direct reference to the bidding mechanic's own penalty rule (lose the contract to the competitor, re-bid at the next round), which no longer exists anywhere in DESIGN.md after this rewrite. Event contracts are a third, distinct contract category from both this subsidy mechanic and the Fixed real contracts failure mechanic being rebuilt in parallel below, so redesigning their own penalty rule is out of scope for both pieces of work landing this session — left exactly as it reads today, genuinely unresolved, needing its own pass.

Two real mechanics replace the dropped bidding mechanic:
- **Subsidies for remote/rural routes the player builds.** Not a new mechanic — DESIGN.md's existing "Evening and morning subsidy" and "SPT subsidy" text already describes the real shape (tops up a journey that would otherwise lose money; offered automatically; player accepts/declines; council warns before withdrawing once the journey stops losing money) — this just narrows its scope and makes it real gameplay rather than background colour. **Scope confirmed narrower, not "any route":** "Remote/rural routes only" (the user's own choice, over "any of my routes, any losing journey"). **Eligibility rule: low passenger density along the route** (the user's own choice, over reusing the existing island-only classification or leaving it to this model's own judgement) — there's no "remote/rural" classification in the code today beyond islands (`isIslandDepotGroup`, fare-journey.mts), so this needed a new, real rule built from data already in the game rather than invented — `busyHourBoardingCount` (stop-upgrades.mts) applied per-stop along the route, averaged and thresholded against the stop-upgrade table (OPERATIONS.md §16). **Now built — see the detailed judgement-call list immediately above.**
- **A real failure mechanic for the Fixed real contracts that are genuinely contracts** — corrected 2026-11-19 after re-reading DESIGN.md directly rather than trusting an earlier loose list: **398, Airlink 100, Express 500, SkyLink**. Two names dropped from the original draft list, both confirmed real mistakes, not design changes: **AIR is explicitly exempt** — DESIGN.md's own "#### AIR — Edinburgh Airport reward contract" section says outright "genuinely zero fines for anything, including missing the timetable — a true exception to the warning-and-review pattern every other contract follows" (it's a one-off 15%-of-fleet-cost reward, not an ongoing-payment contract at all). **Railair was never a contract in the first place** — UK-EXPANSION.md §9a says directly "a station-to-airport express product the player creates... not a single fixed contract like AIR": any player-built rail-to-airport route can carry the Railair brand, on the player's own fully commercial terms, no council/airport relationship to fail or fine. **Oxford Tube is also not and was never a contract** — confirmed directly by the user ("Oxford Tube isn't a contract") — it was added purely as a livery (T136 log entry: "Also can we add Oxford Tube as a livery please"), the "contract" framing crept in only from this thread's own loose language, not anything in DESIGN.md. Replacing the current "cannot be lost outright, but is heavily fined" rule (DESIGN.md's "Fixed real contracts" section), confirmed directly, step by step:
  - Missed/cancelled departures are fined (simpler than cost-per-passenger tracking — the user's own choice: "no bidding/target shown up front" means there's no real cost-per-passenger figure to judge against for these routes at all, so failure is just not running the service reliably, not a financial-ratio judgement).
  - If failures continue, **some of the contract's services get taken away for 6 months** (revised 2026-11-19 from an initial 1 year — the user's own words: "I think 6 months to get a route back might be better") — not lost permanently, not re-tendered. The original words: "if it continues for too long some services get taken away for a while, this lets the play fix the problem without being too harshly punished. The player can get the lost services back after 1 year." A deliberately softer penalty than outright contract loss.
  - **Capped at 60% of the contract's services at once, maximum** — never the whole thing (this is what stops it collapsing into "contract lost outright" at the extreme). Dounreay is the one confirmed exception (see its own entry above).
  - **The unit of "a service" lost is always a return journey or a circular route** — the user's own words: "Services lost should always be a return journey or a circular route." Never a single one-way leg.
  - **Thistle Coaches covers the suspended services during the year**, then they return to the player automatically — ties this directly into the already-settled competitor mechanic (see "The competitor" entry above) rather than leaving a genuine service gap.
  - **Applies uniformly across the real set (398, Airlink 100, Express 500, SkyLink)** — confirmed directly ("large contracts" just describes how big these generally are, not a distinct smaller tier with different rules).

**Fixed-contract failure mechanic: BUILT (2026-11-19).** Real code built:
  - `src/renderer/fixed-contract-failures.mts` — pure logic (no DOM/IPC): contract identification (398/Airlink 100/Express 500/SkyLink by real route number; AIR/Railair/Oxford Tube never match), return-journey-vs-circular-route unit shape (derived from the route's own real `terminusIndex`, not hardcoded per contract), the real daily departure-count generator (reuses `route-timetable.mts`'s `generateDepartureMinutesForBands`/`applyExcludedDepartures`/`mergeCustomDepartures` — a real-data correction made while building this: the first draft wrongly read `PvrTimetable.departureOffsetsSeconds` as a daily departure list, when that field is actually indexed by route POINT for one-way/round-trip duration, not departures per day), the missed-unit shortfall calculation (reuses `route-statistics.mts`'s `computePeakVehicleRequirement` and `workshop-booking.mts`'s away-booking tracking rather than inventing parallel systems), the rolling-window suspension trigger, the 60%-cap whole-unit suspension selection, and the suspension/expiry/auto-return machinery. Covered by `fixed-contract-failures.verify.mts` (real data throughout — 398's own real published weekday band structure run through the real generator, a realistic PVR fixture, a full multi-day escalation-then-return pipeline test), all passing.
  - `src/renderer/game-clock.mts` — added `addMonths(gameDay, months)`, the exact-calendar-month helper the suspension return date needed (originally built as a simpler `addOneYear` for "exactly 1 year," generalised 2026-11-19 when that was revised to 6 months instead — a 6-month step needs full day-in-month clamping, not just the one Feb-29 case a year-step needed), not a flat day-count approximation that would drift across a leap year. Tested in `game-clock.verify.mts`, including the new shorter-target-month clamping cases (e.g. 31 August + 6 months -> 28/29 February).
  - `electron/db.mts` — new `fixed_contract_fine_events`/`fixed_contract_suspensions` tables (unconditional `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, SCHEMA_VERSION left at 67, same precedent `remote_route_subsidies`/`route_advertising_bookings` already established for exactly this "concurrent contract-related schema work in one session" situation), plus plain storage functions (`recordFixedContractFineEvent` — which also posts the fine to the real finance ledger under a new "Fixed contract fines" category, so it's a real financial action, not just a logged row — `listFixedContractFineEvents`, `createFixedContractSuspension`, `listFixedContractSuspensions`, `listAllActiveFixedContractSuspensions`, `deactivateFixedContractSuspension`). Covered by `electron/fixed-contract-failures.verify.mts`, all passing.
  - `src/renderer/livery-seed-data.mts` — **Thistle Coaches** built for real for the first time: added to `REAL_LIVERY_COLOURS` (white `#ffffff` primary, confirmed; `#6b3fa0` thistle-purple secondary, this model's own flagged estimate — no sourced "heraldic thistle purple" hex exists), `OPERATOR_LIVERIES` (standalone operator) and `LIVERY_SPECIFIC_ROUTE` (locked to the sentinel route number `"Thistle Coaches"`, which no real player route can ever have — the same mechanism every other specific-route livery already uses, so it's genuinely never player-selectable without a bespoke "hide this livery" flag). Deliberately has NO vehicle-type or route-type lock at all, so it can cover any of the four in-scope contracts (coach-only Airlink 100, double-deck Express 500, electric-single-deck 398, and SkyLink's own not-yet-built requirement). Covered by 11 new checks in `livery-seed-data.verify.mts`.

  **Numeric/design judgement calls made while building this, flagged in `fixed-contract-failures.mts`'s own header/constant comments, not hidden**, summarised here:
  - **Fine amount: £75 per missed unit** (`FIXED_CONTRACT_FINE_PER_MISSED_UNIT_GBP_PENCE`) — not user-confirmed; these are fictional contracts with no real published penalty rate to ground this in. Roughly 8x 398's own real £9/journey payment, chosen to read as "heavily fined" without being able to bankrupt a small operator over one bad day.
  - **Suspension trigger: 3 fine-days within a rolling 28-day window** (`SUSPENSION_TRIGGER_FINE_DAYS`/`SUSPENSION_TRIGGER_WINDOW_DAYS`) — not user-confirmed. The window reuses the existing 28-day company period (`game-clock.mts`) rather than inventing a separate span; 3 fine-days within it is a judgement call for "repeatedly failing," not a single bad day.
  - **Each escalation is its own independent suspension batch with its own fresh 6-month timer**, rather than one combined timer for the whole route — not explicitly confirmed either way by the user. Read as the more generous interpretation (an escalation shouldn't restart an already-ticking return date on an earlier batch), flagged in case a single combined timer per route was actually intended.
  - **Suspension length revised 2026-11-19, from the original 1 year to 6 months** — the user's own words: "I think 6 months to get a route back might be better." `game-clock.mts`'s `addOneYear` was generalised into `addMonths(gameDay, months)` (see above) rather than keeping a separate function; `fixed-contract-failures.mts` now calls `addMonths(startGameDay, 6)` via its own named `SUSPENSION_LENGTH_MONTHS` constant.
  - **Missed-unit detection is a proportional approximation, not a per-departure simulation** — a genuine, flagged gap, not a shortcut taken lightly: this game has no real-time vehicle rostering/duty-assignment model yet (Phase 6) that could say "this specific 08:12 departure has no bus." A vehicle shortfall is spread evenly across the day's scheduled units by straight proportion (`missedFraction = shortfall / peakVehicleRequirement`) instead.
  - **Vehicle availability reuses `Vehicle.boughtForRouteId`** as the per-route assignment signal, even though depot-groups.d.ts's own comment on that field says "nothing reads this as an enforced link" elsewhere in the game — these four contracts are exactly the case DESIGN.md already describes vehicles being "locked to a contract" for, so it's the one real signal that exists; a genuine "assign vehicle to route" system, if ever built, should supersede this.
  - **The general 60% cap genuinely cannot suspend anything on a very sparse route** (verified directly: a 1-unit-a-day route has `floor(1 × 0.6) = 0`) — not a bug, this is the real reason the separate Dounreay exception (above) needed to exist at all.
  - **No IPC wiring built** (no `main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` Api surface) — the storage layer is real and ready, but nothing calls it from a live screen yet, consistent with "no finished UI expectation" for this piece of work. Follows this codebase's own "lazy catch-up" convention (`private-hire-income.mts`, `route-advertising-billing`) rather than a central daily tick, for whenever that wiring lands.
  - **No live in-app check performed** — backend-only, same as the subsidy half above, for the same reason.
  - **tsc --noEmit**: clean for every file this work touched, including the pre-existing errors in `fixed-contract-failures.verify.mts` that the subsidy entry above flagged as "left untouched, parallel fork" — those were this same piece of work's own early draft (a genuine misreading of `PvrTimetable.departureOffsetsSeconds`, corrected directly in the module and its tests once caught), not a separate fork's output.

**Phase 5/9 redesign: stop ownership dropped, replaced with council-triggered upgrades + a separate airport-stop purchase/contract model (design-only, settled 2026-11-16, not yet built).** Triggered by the user questioning the existing stop-ownership model directly: "if I never own the stops I think we need to re think this." Supersedes DESIGN.md's existing "Ownership and cost" stop section (stops owned by council, player pays a monthly fee per stop plus a per-route fee, or can buy a stop outright for a one-off price plus a smaller ongoing fee to unlock upgrades) — that model also directly contradicted this session's own earlier real-UK-practice research (no recurring per-stop fee exists in reality), so dropping it resolves both problems at once, not just the ownership question.

- **No stop ownership, no monthly stop cost at all.** Stops stay council-owned and free to serve — the council pays for upkeep, not the operator. Replaces the "buy a stop for upgrades" mechanic entirely.
- **Stop upgrades — shelter, seating and lighting are council-triggered automatically**, each by its own independent busy-hour bus/boarding threshold (same real metric the already-built E-ink/LED stop-display mechanic uses, OPERATIONS.md §16 — reused deliberately as the established precedent, not a new metric invented for this). Player can pay to have an upgrade happen early: `cost % of full install price = 100% × (1 − current usage ÷ threshold)` — free right at the threshold (it was about to happen anyway), full price at zero usage, linear between, clamped to 0% if usage already exceeds the threshold.
  - **Route advertising is NOT part of this mechanic at all — corrected 2026-11-16.** The user's own direct correction: "The route advertising should be only bought by the player, never by the stop owner." Unlike shelter/seating/lighting, which genuinely serve every passenger regardless of operator (so the council has a real public-interest reason to fund them eventually on its own), advertising only benefits the one company paying for it — there is no "the council would do this anyway" default path, so no busy-hour threshold and no pay-to-accelerate for it. It is always player-initiated and player-paid, full stop. See its own bullet below for the real cost/timing model.
  - Real-world research was too thin for sourced figures here (unlike leasing/stop-fees/stand-rental earlier this session, which had real numbers) — the following are **explicitly flagged estimates**, scaled off the real E-ink (£1,200 install/£15 per month) and LED (£4,500/£45 per month) display figures, not independently sourced. **Revised 2026-11-16** — the user's own direct feedback on the first pass ("passenger numbers for upgrades are a bit low"): thresholds raised so Shelter now lands in the same tier as LED's own threshold, rather than sitting below even the "no display at all" band as the first draft accidentally did:
    - Seating: ≥20 busy-hour boardings or ≥3 buses to trigger. £300 install, no ongoing cost.
    - Lighting: ≥30 boardings or ≥4 buses. £900 install, £10/month.
    - Shelter: ≥40 boardings or ≥6 buses (same tier as LED's own threshold). £3,500 install, £25/month.
    - **Route advertising — player-bought only, real cost/timing settled 2026-11-16.** £20/month (settled earlier the same session), **plus a £500 one-off setup fee** (printing/installation — the user's own correction: a monthly-only model was wrong, it needed both, not monthly *instead of* a one-off; revised up from an initial £150 proposal to £500, the user's own figure), **plus a 14-day delay before it actually goes up** ("a delay to before it gets put up for supply reasons" — reusing OPERATIONS.md §16's own existing "work starts 14 days after ordering" fitting-delay convention rather than inventing a separate figure; flagged in case advertising print/supply lead time should genuinely differ from fitting a physical shelter/display). No busy-hour threshold of any kind — available to buy immediately regardless of the stop's own usage level.
    - **Two advertising modes, added 2026-11-16**: for an ordinary stop, route advertising promotes one specific numbered route, as above. For a stop served by a real `long_distance`-category route, the player can instead advertise generically for long-distance services, branded with the real operator/company (e.g. "National Express," "Citylink") of whichever long-distance livery actually calls there — the user's own words: "can stop advertising be set for the route or long distance services in general with the company of the buses that stop there if it's long distance." Matches real practice: a long-distance stop often serves several numbered services from the same operator, so promoting one specific route number there makes less sense than the operator's own brand. Derive the real operator from the already-existing `liveryOperator()`/`LONG_DISTANCE_LIVERY_NAMES` machinery (`livery-seed-data.mts`) against whichever long-distance route(s) actually call at that stop, rather than a new classification.
- **Airport stops are a separate, genuinely-ownable exception** — unlike council stops, airport infrastructure belongs to a private company, matching real UK practice (airports grant exclusive bus/coach bay access via competitive tender/concession agreements, not free public-highway access). The player can **buy** an airport stop outright and **lock it to whichever of their own routes they choose**. **Price settled 2026-11-16 ("your call" — the user's own words)**: checked first whether the existing "location-based pricing" DESIGN.md already describes for depots/stops/stations (city costs more than a village) is actually built anywhere in code — confirmed directly it isn't, nothing like it exists yet for any property type. Building a bespoke scaling rule just for this one feature would need re-architecting once that real system eventually lands for depots/stops generally, so: **flat £15,000 placeholder price**, explicitly flagged as unsourced (no real published airport-bay purchase price exists to ground it in, per this session's own research), in the same rough ballpark as this game's other lumpy one-off capital costs. Revisit with real location/size scaling once the general depot/stop pricing system is actually built, rather than inventing one bespoke to this feature now.
- **Contract stops are a third, distinct case, and apply at major rail stations as well as airports — not an airport-only rule.** Confirmed by the user's own direct follow-up, extending the exact same treatment to ScotRail's AIR and 398 contracts at Glasgow Central: "The same with AIR and the 398 at Glasgow Central." So the real rule is "a contract tied to a major transport hub (airport or railway station) gets its own bundled stop there," with Airlink 100/Express 500 (airports) and AIR/398 (Glasgow Central) as confirmed worked examples of the same mechanic, not two separate mechanics. In every case: the stop comes bundled with the contract itself, not bought separately by the player, and it is automatically locked exclusively to that one contracted route. **Scope, clarified 2026-11-16: exactly ONE stop per contract — the terminus stop at the airport/station, not every stop the contracted route happens to serve.** The user's own words: "The contracted stops are only 1 stop, all the terminus stop." So Airlink 100's bundled/locked/advertised stop is its own terminus at the airport specifically, not (say) its Edinburgh city-centre stops too; the same for AIR/398 at Glasgow Central. That one terminus stop carries **permanent, free route advertising** for that route from day one — bypassing the normal £20/month + £500 setup + 14-day delay model above entirely, since the contracting body (the airport, or ScotRail at the station) wants its own contracted service prominently and permanently branded as a term of the deal, not something the operator pays for or has to wait on.
  - **Built 2026-11-20** — the shared "stop exclusively locked to one route" primitive (`src/renderer/stop-route-locks.mts`, new `stop_route_locks` table in `electron/db.mts`, schema v67->v68) backs both contract-stop bundling and airport-stop purchase, same primitive, built once. Contract-stop bundling is wired into `contracts-panel.ts`'s `acceptRealContract` (`bundleContractTerminusStop`): the real terminus stop is read off the Route's own `terminusIndex`/`startIndex` fields (falling back to point 0 for a loop route with neither set — 398's own real saved shape, checked directly against the extracted seed data), then locked (source `'contract'`) and granted a free, permanent `route_advertising_bookings` row via a new `isContractBundle` flag on that existing table (reused, not a parallel record — bookedGameDay === liveFromGameDay, never billed). Airport-stop purchase is a real player flow in `stops-layer.ts`'s stop popup, gated on the stop's own pre-existing `is_airport` manual override (found already built for the timing-point rule — a real stop-level flag, not derived from the route-type proxy originally guessed at) — flat £15,000 placeholder, flagged unsourced. Exclusivity is enforced for real at the one actual save chokepoint, `route-draw.ts`'s save handler (right before `window.routes.create`/`update`), not just left as an unenforced data model. `tsc --noEmit` clean; real `.verify.mts` coverage added (`stop-route-locks.verify.mts`, tested directly against the real extracted 398/Airlink 100/Express 500/AIR seed data's own terminusIndex values; a `db.verify.mts` round-trip block). One known, flagged gap: `is_airport` is a judgement call for "which stops count as airport stops" (not independently reconfirmed by the user at this level of detail) — see the module's own header comment.
- **Knock-on, not yet resolved**: DESIGN.md's "Awareness" section lists "route advertising **at owned stops** and bus stations" as one of the three levers — now stale, since stops generally aren't owned any more (only airport ones, and airport-contract ones are automatically advertised anyway). Needs revisiting once the awareness-levers combination formula itself is designed (see the Phase 5 "Known hard parts" discussion).

**Phase 5 scoping, settled 2026-11-16 (design-only, not yet built) — stop fees and stand rental.** Checked real UK practice directly (web research) before deciding, rather than inventing figures: no UK council charges operators a recurring fee for routine stop usage — the only real per-stop charges found (Bristol £294/Surrey £175 per day/GM £365+VAT) are one-off temporary-suspension charges billed to utility/works contractors, not bus operators running a timetable. **Decision: drop "stop fees" as an ongoing cost in-game** (the existing Finance-screen P&L line stays, but will correctly read £0 for routine operation) — matches CLAUDE.md's own "every figure stays realistic" rule rather than inventing a fee that doesn't exist in reality.

Stand/stance rental is real and current (councils have been moving away from flat annual bay rental toward per-departure charging — e.g. Preston's old £5,620/bay/year model). Found Buchanan Bus Station's own real, current published rate (SPT's rate sheet, effective April 2025): **Category A (ordinary local service) departure = £1.15** (+ VAT; Category B express £3.85, Category C long-distance coach £7.65, Category E tours £7.75 — not needed for the general case). Buchanan and SPT are both already real in-game content (Express 500's reserved Buchanan Stance 46; SPT as a real contracting body, DESIGN.md's "SPT as the contracting body" section), so this is a well-grounded figure, not a guess. **Decision, refined 2026-11-16: this is a PER-STATION fee, not one flat global rate** — the user's own direct follow-up: "Can we have some bus stations cost but others free like charging for Buchanan, and free for Greenock, Patrick and Govan please," matching SPT's own real rate sheet exactly (it explicitly states Greenock, Partick and Govan bus stations have no charges at all, unlike Buchanan). So: Buchanan charges £1.15/departure (VAT excluded, per the earlier decision); Greenock, Partick and Govan charge £0. **Default for any other bus station (not one of these 4 specifically-known real ones, including future player-bought stations): charges the same real Buchanan ordinary rate, £1.15/departure** — the user's own call, reasoning that most real bus stations DO charge something and the 3 free ones are SPT's own named exception, not the norm.

**Airlink 100 / Lothian XLB fallback — real trigger condition deferred to Phase 6 (2026-11-16).** Built so far (`livery-seed-data.mts`'s `airlinkLothianFallbackEligible`, wired into `vehicle-simulation.ts`): the fallback (a Lothian-liveried Enviro400 XLB from the depot group's existing fleet standing in on route Airlink 100) triggers only when every owned Airlink 100/Lothian Girraffe vehicle is currently away on a real repaint or re-power booking. The user's own correction: "The Airlink 100/Lothian is for anytime there aren't enough vehicles, I just used re-paint as an example" — repaint was only ever an example, not the intended real scope. Asked how to generalise it; the user's answer: **"It should be checked at the time a vehicle gets assigned to a duty whether there are currently enough vehicles, this can be saved for later when vehicle assignment gets added."** So the real, correct trigger is a Phase 6 (Drivers/duties) concept — there is no real vehicle-to-duty assignment system yet for this to hook into. **Decision: leave the current booking-only check as the interim behaviour for now; do not build an approximate stand-in (e.g. comparing currently-running journeys to currently-available liveried vehicles) — the user explicitly deferred the real fix rather than asking for an approximation.** Revisit once real vehicle-to-duty assignment exists.

**Specs confirmed 2026-11-15 for plans #10/#11/#12 (not yet built — queued behind plan #9, the live passenger-simulation wiring), captured here so they survive a context reset before being built:**
- **Plan #10 (bus capacity colour)**: route-number badge is yellow at 80% seated capacity, orange at 100% seated (0 standing), red at 100% of total capacity (seated + standing both full).
- **Plan #11 (stop crowding)**: stop marker colour is relative to that specific stop's own typical/historical crowd size, not a single fixed game-wide waiting-passenger threshold — i.e. "busier than usual for THIS stop," not "more than N people."
- **Plan #12 (route statistics menu)**: all four of — load factor (passengers vs capacity), revenue and cost per mile/journey, punctuality/on-time performance, and passengers carried vs passengers who gave up waiting (the last one comes directly from the two-tier passenger simulation's own give-up tracking, `passenger-simulation.mts`'s `evaluateTripOutcome`).

Q15-Q22 are a single large list given together by the user on 2026-11-13,
explicitly to be gone through **one at a time** (their own stated order,
forking implementation of a finalized item into the background while the
next one is discussed), with vehicle-spec population (part of Q15)
deliberately done last. Recorded here in full before any detail
discussion, per this project's own "write decisions/asks to docs
immediately, don't rely on chat surviving a context reset" lesson.

## Design worked out ahead of build (queued for Phase 9)

**Vehicle transfer + cascade-and-repaint, designed in full 2026-11-15, deliberately NOT built yet** — explicitly a Phase 9 item (CLAUDE.md's own build order: "cascades" sits alongside second-hand sales/scrapping and depot renting/buying, late-stage polish), and CLAUDE.md says not to reorder the build without asking; the user confirmed queuing the actual build for when Phase 9 is reached, but wanted the design settled now so it doesn't need re-deriving later.

- **Scenario**: the classic real cascade — buying new stock for one depot displaces an existing bus there, which moves on to a different depot (often a smaller/outstation one) and gets repainted into that depot's own livery.
- **Standalone transfer action**: must exist on its own too, not just bundled into a purchase — a plain "move this vehicle to another depot" action available any time.
- **Transfer timing**: realistic dead-mileage driving time between the two depots, not instant — reuses the *idea* of the existing dead-mileage route system, but **not directly its current shape**: `DeadMileageRoute`/`createRoute` (`depot-groups.d.ts`) is dealer-entrance-to-depot only (`createRoute(dealerEntranceId, depotId, direction, trunkWaypoints)`), built for new-vehicle deliveries. A real depot-to-depot variant needs building — confirmed this is a genuine gap, not something to reuse unmodified.
- **Picking the displaced vehicle**: fully manual — the player browses their existing fleet and chooses any vehicle to send onward, no auto-suggestion. Matches this project's own repeated "never auto-decide for the player, let them choose" convention (see `feedback_prefer_click_proximity_over_clever_heuristics`/`feedback_scale_dont_filter_ambiguous_candidates`-style decisions already made elsewhere).
- **Livery mismatch at the destination**: if the transferred vehicle's current livery isn't allowed at the destination depot (e.g. a region-locked livery — Translink/Irish Citylink, National Express Scottish, etc., all already built this session with real lock logic in `livery-seed-data.mts`), a repaint booking into a valid livery for the destination is queued **automatically**, not just flagged as a warning — the player can't leave a vehicle sitting in an invalid livery at its new depot.
- **The combined flow itself**: triggered from the existing vehicle-buying flow (`vehicle-buy-configure-screen.ts`/basket/deliveries, not yet identified exactly which step) — when confirming a new purchase for depot A, the player can optionally pick an existing depot-A vehicle to cascade elsewhere in the same action. This is a convenience UI wrapping three already-real, independently-timed processes (the new purchase's own delivery lead time, the transfer's own real dead-mileage driving time, the repaint shop's own real turnaround — all already modelled by `vehicle-buy-pricing.mts`/dead-mileage routes/`repaint-booking.mts` respectively) — the combined flow does not compress or fake any of their individual timings, it just starts all three together instead of requiring three separate manual visits.
- **Chain length: capped at 2 transfers, A→B→C only** — not an arbitrary-length cascade. Buying one new bus for depot A can displace at most one bus onward to B, and that B bus can displace at most one bus onward to C; the chain stops there.
- **At every link (A→B and B→C), the player gets a real three-way choice** for whatever bus would otherwise be displaced: transfer it onward (continuing the chain, only available at the A→B link since B→C is already the last possible hop), sell it (ending the chain at that link), or keep it as a spare (also ending the chain at that link, but keeping the vehicle in the fleet rather than selling). Confirmed: this three-way choice, not just "transfer or not," is available at both links, not only the last one.
- **Sequencing rule confirmed to apply at every link, not just the first**: a bus can never depart until its own replacement has physically arrived — the A→B transfer can't start until the new bus has arrived at A, and the B→C transfer can't start until the A→B bus has arrived at B. The whole chain plays out in strict sequence, never in parallel.
- **Vehicle category must match at every transfer** — a transfer is only ever coach-for-coach, double-decker-for-double-decker, etc. (whatever real category grouping `VEHICLE-SPECS.md`/the existing vehicle catalogue already uses — confirmed this session that coaches are already identifiable via `vehicle.category === "Coaches"`, used by the livery-lock work). A displaced vehicle can only ever be replaced by, or cascade onward as, the same category of vehicle.
- **Real prerequisite gap, confirmed by checking the codebase directly (2026-11-15): selling a vehicle does not exist as a feature at all yet** — no `sellVehicle` or equivalent anywhere. This is the other half of the same CLAUDE.md Phase 9 grouping ("Second-hand sales, scrapping and cascades") as this whole cascade feature, so building "sell instead of transferring" genuinely requires building real vehicle selling from scratch alongside it, not just wiring into something that already exists.
- **No separate scrapping mechanic** — deliberately dropped after discussion. Selling covers the whole disposal range on its own by using a lower depreciation floor than buying: the existing real used-vehicle formula (10%/year depreciation, scaled by condition) stays floored at 20% of original value when BUYING a used vehicle (unchanged), but floors at only **10%** when SELLING your own vehicle back — the same curve, a lower floor for the sell side, giving a natural dealer margin without needing a separate scrap-value path or a condition/MOT threshold deciding which mechanic applies.
- **Sale timing, refined 2026-11-15**: a sold vehicle stays in active service for 2 days after the sale is agreed, then is pulled from service and sits idle until the dealer collects it on day 9 (so a further 7 idle days between being pulled and actually leaving the fleet).

| T206 | **Real regression in T205's own line-direction-aware push, found live immediately after shipping (2026-11-15): dramatic criss-crossing spikes appeared where the boundary crossed Main Street, far worse than anything before.** Root cause, confirmed against the real saved zone: T205's "is the boundary's own local direction parallel to the road" check compared the raw, unnormalized determinant (`dx*ty - dy*tx`) against a tiny fixed threshold — not scale-invariant. Real drawn points are often only a metre or two apart, so a direction only ~10° off parallel still produced a nonzero determinant past that threshold, triggering a full line-intersection slide instead of the parallel fallback — and sliding along a near-parallel line to reach a target offset line many metres away needs a slide distance that scales with `1/sin(angle)`, which exploded for anything shallower than a few degrees. Confirmed directly in the real save: several consecutive points slid 147-183m sideways, each "numerically valid" by the old check but geometrically absurd, producing exactly the criss-crossing mess reported. Fixed by testing the actual angle between the two directions (the normalized cross product, sin of the angle) instead of the raw determinant — any direction within 20° of parallel to the road now correctly falls back to the plain perpendicular push, regardless of how close together the ring's own points happen to be. **Verified**: `fare-zone-road-pushout.verify.mts` — the previous "angled case" test's own geometry was actually only ~11° off parallel (closer to the real bug than intended), so it was tightened to a clean 45° approach to keep exercising the slide-along-line path, and a new dedicated regression test added at that same real ~11° shallow angle, confirming it now correctly falls back to the plain width/2+15 push instead of an enormous slide — 16 assertions total, all pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean | Claude Code | Code complete, unit-verified, packaged — ask the user to redraw the same Main Street boundary again and confirm the criss-crossing spikes are gone, alongside the straight residential-to-road line (T205) and the along-the-road push (unchanged) |

I think the weird kinks are moving the point away from the road at an exact [perpendicular] angle, not along the line that it already exists." Confirmed exactly right: the push moved a qualifying point a FIXED perpendicular distance from the road using only that one point's own position, with no idea which direction the zone boundary's own line was actually heading there — correct for a boundary running ALONG a road (every point lands on the same parallel offset line, the intended shape), wrong for one merely passing near a road at an angle (the perpendicular jump ignored the line's own direction entirely). Redesigned so the push happens in TypeScript (`fare-zone-road-pushout.mts`), where the ring's own neighbouring points are available, instead of Rust (which only ever saw one point at a time): a new, much simpler `Router::nearest_road_edge_info` (`rust/game-wasm/src/lib.rs`) returns just the raw facts (nearest edge's own two endpoints, real width, real distance) with no push decision baked in; the old `offset_point_away_from` (and its 8 tests) removed entirely, superseded. `pushPointOffRoad` (new, `fare-zone-road-pushout.mts`) uses the point's own real `prev`/`next` ring neighbours to find the boundary's own local line direction, then intersects that line with the road's own safe offset line (parallel to it, at the correct kerb+margin distance) instead of translating the point sideways — the point slides ALONG its own pre-existing line until it clears the road, no kink. Falls back to the old straightforward perpendicular push only when that local direction is itself parallel to the road (no stable intersection — a boundary genuinely tracking alongside it, where every point already wants the same parallel line regardless of position), with a sanity bound against a near-parallel direction sending the intersection implausibly far away. **Verified**: `fare-zone-road-pushout.verify.mts` fully rewritten — confirms a boundary running alongside the road still falls back to the old perpendicular push (same measured distance, width/2+15), confirms the real reported case (an angled approach) slides along the pre-existing line with a measured kink of ~0 (2.6e-13m, floating-point noise) rather than jumping off it, and confirms the exactly-parallel-direction fallback case — 15 assertions, all pass. `cargo test -p game-wasm`: 48 passed (8 old tests removed, 4 new ones added for the simpler `nearest_road_edge_info`), 1 pre-existing unrelated ignore, 0 failed. | T202 | **The real cause of the Main Street fork/wedge T199 didn't actually fix, found live (2026-11-14) after the user reported "exactly the same thing" happening on a fresh redraw.** T199's cutoff tightening was real but wasn't it — exhaustively disproved six other theories in turn by direct experiment against the real app and real save data (tile-load timing, the click handler firing twice — measured exactly once via a live click-dispatch test — a missing landuse class in the union, the bridge-corner logic misfiring, zoom-dependent vector-tile simplification, and the road-push mechanism). Added quiet file-based logging (`%APPDATA%\bus-game\temp-diag.log`, via a `console-message` forward in `electron/main.ts`) so a live session could be captured without devtools; the captured log showed the bogus point wasn't near any of the user's actual clicks at all, which narrowed it to a step that runs automatically at save time over the WHOLE ring rather than per click: `clipRingToCoastline` (`coastline-clip.mts`). Confirmed directly by replaying the real ring and the real `coastline.bin` (via a throwaway native Rust dumper using `game_data::decode_coastline`, deleted after use): a ring point that already sits exactly on the real coastline — entirely normal for a waterfront commercial area, Main Street runs right along Kyle of Lochalsh's own shore — makes `segmentIntersection` report a crossing exactly AT that point for BOTH of the two coastline edges that share it as their common vertex (`t=0` or `t=1`, the edge's own start/end). `clipRingToCoastline` reads that as "2 crossings on the same chain" (its real signal for "this edge dips into the sea and comes back") and inserts a spurious `coastlineWalkBetween` detour, even though the point was already correctly on the shore. First attempt (excluding any crossing near t=0/1 outright) broke a genuine, already-tested case — a Shift-drawn straight line starting or ending right at the shore legitimately needs its own t≈0/1 crossing kept. Real fix: dedupe crossings by their own real-world point (not by `t`) in `findCoastlineCrossings` — two crossings at the *same* point are one event double-counted via two adjacent coastline edges, not a genuine "dips into the sea and back" pair, while two crossings at genuinely *different* points (even if each is at its own edge's t≈0/1) still correctly trigger the walk. **Verified**: 4 new assertions in `coastline-clip.verify.mts` (a ring edge starting/ending exactly on a coastline's own shared vertex dedupes to one real crossing, not two; a genuine crossing strictly between a ring edge's own endpoints is still found; a ring already sitting on the coastline with no genuine crossing comes back completely unchanged) alongside every pre-existing assertion, including the Shift-across-a-bay case the first attempt broke — all pass. Replayed the real saved zone against the real coastline data with the fix: zero false-positive 2-crossing matches, ring passes through unchanged. All TEMP-DIAG instrumentation from this investigation (console logging in `fare-zone-drawing.ts`'s click handler, the `__map` debug hook in `main.ts`, the console-message-to-file forward in `electron/main.ts`) removed before shipping. `tsc --noEmit` clean | Claude Code | Code complete, unit-verified — ask the user to redraw the same Main Street boundary one more time and confirm the fork is gone for real this time |

Root cause, confirmed against the real save: `mergeStopSequences` (`route-timetable-grid.mts`) picks the longest family member's sequence as a fixed backbone and inserts every other member's own stops onto it by walking forward from the last matched anchor — but a sequence's own LEADING unmatched run (stops before its first match anywhere in the backbone) always defaulted to inserting at position 0, the very front of the whole merged list, regardless of where that first real match actually sits. For a route split into outbound/inbound components (`terminusIndex`/`startIndex` both set), each component's own offsets are only ever computed for its own leg (`computeOffsetsForDirection`'s own documented convention — everywhere else stays 0); `journeysForRoute` was the one place in the codebase that read these arrays as if every timetable spanned the full route, reaching into the OTHER leg's always-0 offsets whenever `direction !== "both"`. Fixed using the same `routeDirectionRanges` convention `sliceGridToPointRange`/`buildMembersForLabel` already use correctly elsewhere: when `direction !== "both"`, find the matching labelled segment and use its own `fromPointIndex`/`toPointIndex` in place of `0`/the route's last index. **Verified**: new assertion in `duty-journeys.verify.mts` — a synthetic split-route fixture mirroring route 500/AIR's real shape confirms the outbound leg now ends at its own real terminus with the right duration, not looping back to point 0's stop at 0 minutes — alongside every pre-existing assertion, all pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean | Claude Code | Code complete, unit-verified — confirmed by the user: "The Air and 500 services are working correctly" |

| T195 | **Real follow-up bug to T194's own Ctrl-landuse widening (2026-11-14): a zone boundary drawn by releasing Ctrl onto a road produced a visible spike — jumping ~90m away from the road and straight back — near Main Street, Kyle of Lochalsh.** Not guessed: pulled the real "Test" zone's saved points from `save.sqlite`, scanned consecutive-point distances, found exactly one matched pair of large jumps (90.9m out, 95.5m back) among 929 points — a real geometric round-trip, not noise. Started a live Playwright reproduction to pin down the exact mechanism but the user asked to skip straight to a code-reasoned fix instead once the likely cause was clear. Root cause, confirmed by reading `nearestVertexOfRing` (`area-cluster.mts`) directly: when releasing Ctrl to bridge from an area-boundary trace onto a plain road click (T118's confirmed points 4/5), the bridging "corner" is picked as whichever vertex of the *entire* landuse polygon ring is globally nearest to the other side's point, with no distance bound at all. OSM only records vertices at a polygon's real corners, not evenly along straight edges, so for a large polygon (confirmed one example near this exact spot: a `military` landuse area with only 7 vertices spanning a wide area), the globally-nearest vertex to a click made partway along a long edge can be a distant, unrelated corner rather than anything sensibly nearby — producing exactly this jump-away-and-back spike. Fixed by bounding the search: `nearestVertexOfRing(ring, referencePoint, maxDistanceM)` now returns `FareZonePoint | null`, only returning a vertex that's actually within `maxDistanceM`; both real call sites in `fare-zone-drawing.ts` (the Ctrl-branch's road-to-cluster bridge, and the plain-click branch's cluster-to-road bridge) now pass `LANDUSE_SNAP_DISTANCE_M` (60m, the same bound every other area-boundary snap in this feature already uses) and fall back to no bridging corner at all — same point as before, just without the spike — when nothing real is close enough, matching this feature's existing "can't do it sensibly, don't fake it" convention. **Verified**: new `area-cluster.verify.mts` assertion — a square ring, a point 2m from the nearest real corner, `maxDistanceM=1` — asserts `null`, proving the bound actually rejects a too-far vertex rather than always returning something; all 3 pre-existing `nearestVertexOfRing` assertions updated to pass a generous bound and still pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean. New pure module `fare-zone-road-pushout.mts` (`computeRingCentroid`, a plain arithmetic mean of the ring's own vertices — not a true area-weighted centroid, but sufficient for a one-edge-at-a-time "which side is the interior" check; `pushOutRoadSnappedPoints`, taking a plain injected offset function rather than the `Router` class itself, specifically so it stays unit-testable without the real WASM module) — same "pure geometry lives in its own .mts, DOM/map wiring stays in the .ts" split `coastline-clip.mts`/`area-cluster.mts` already use. Wired in at the zone's own real existing finalize step (the "Save zone" button, immediately before the pre-existing `clipRingToCoastline` call, which stays the last safety net against a boundary ending up in the sea) — no new hook invented. **New `fare-zone-road-pushout.verify.mts`** (synthetic points, a stubbed offset function, no live map or real WASM needed): centroid of an empty ring and a plain square; only the flagged point reaches the stub, carrying its own real coordinates and the ring's own real centroid as the "away from" reference; unflagged points returned as the exact same object, untouched; an all-flagged ring replaces every point. | T192 | **Real follow-up bug to T191 (2026-11-14): liveries read upside down for any vehicle heading roughly west.** T191's -90 artwork-orientation correction was genuinely correct, but rotating the badge's own artwork through the full compass (including the half past 90°/270° that `vehicles-badge-number`'s text already clamps away from, for exactly the same readability reason) meant the livery pattern itself went visibly upside down for westbound travel — a real bus mirrors when it turns around, it doesn't flip vertically. | T191 | **Real bug, found and fixed live (2026-11-14): vehicle map badges always faced north-south, never matching the real road's angle.** Investigated via direct live sampling, not guessed — launched a throwaway diagnostic instance (the app has no single-instance lock, so this never touched the user's own separately-running game) and read real `vehicles` source feature data straight from the live map, cross-checked against route 398's own real waypoints (which genuinely zig-zag N/E/N/W/S, not a straight line) — bearings sampled live clustered near 0°/180° regardless. Root cause: a unit mismatch in `bearingDegrees` (`src/renderer/vehicle-position.mts`) — `x` (the longitude component) was converted to radians via `toRad`, but `y` (the latitude component) was left in raw degrees, ~57x (180/π) too large relative to `x`. This pulled `atan2(x, y)` toward the y-axis (north/south) for virtually any real road segment, masked by the file's own two existing unit tests, which (by coincidence, not design) each zeroed out one axis entirely — the "due east" case used `lat=0` for both endpoints (zeroing `y` outright) and the "due north" case used the same `lon` for both (zeroing `x` outright), so neither ever exercised a genuine diagonal where the mismatch would show. Fixed by converting `y` via `toRad` too. New test added with both components non-zero at a real Scotland-ish latitude, expected value computed independently from the corrected formula (63.02°) rather than copied from the implementation — this is the test that would have caught the bug. **A separate, genuinely correct fix made earlier the same day, riding on top of this one**: the badge/arrow icon artwork's own native (unrotated) orientation is landscape/pointing-east (confirmed directly from `map-icons.ts`'s `drawArrow`, which draws its triangle along +x), but `icon-rotate` was using the raw bearing with no correction for that — fixed with a `-90` offset on `vehicles-badge`'s `icon-rotate`, `vehicles-arrow`'s `icon-rotate`, and `vehicles-badge-number`'s `text-rotate` base value (same correction, applied before its own pre-existing readability clamp). Both fixes were needed together: the artwork-orientation offset alone (without the bearing-math fix) would have only changed "always facing east-west" into "always facing north-south," which is exactly the intermediate symptom the user reported and that led to finding this second, deeper bug. Also fixed in passing: the "Loading road network…" progress label used to freeze at "100%" for the full WASM decode time (tens of seconds for the real 969MB UK+Ireland+IoM graph) since the progress callback only ever tracked download fraction, not decode — now shows "Processing road network…" once the download itself is done, an honest label rather than a fake percentage for a step with no real progress to report. **Verified**: `vehicle-position.verify.mts` — all existing assertions still pass, plus the new diagonal case. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. Live-verified via the same throwaway diagnostic instance against real route 398 data after the fix, confirming varied bearings (189.95°, 191.53°, 12.20°, 191.38°, 11.09° across different real positions) rather than the pre-fix near-constant clustering — the route's own genuinely north/south-ish segments (confirmed from its real waypoints) account for these; its clearly-eastward segment wasn't caught live within the sampling window, but the unit test covers that case directly and more rigorously than waiting on a live vehicle's timing would. `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both run successfully, this is packaged. **Follow-up, same day**: the "Processing road network…" label fix above was real but didn't actually display — `loadRouter` (`src/renderer/router.ts`) calls the long synchronous WASM decode (`new Router(...)`) immediately after the label is set, with no yield back to the browser's render loop in between, so the page never got a chance to actually repaint before blocking on the decode; the player kept seeing the stale "100%" frame the whole time regardless. Fixed with a double `requestAnimationFrame` wait between setting the label and starting the decode (one frame to schedule the repaint, a second to confirm it actually happened before blocking — a single rAF can still race it). Rebuilt and repackaged again | Claude Code | Done and packaged — ask the user to reinstall and confirm both: a bus travelling along a road with a visible bend now visually tracks the road's real angle throughout, and the loading screen shows "Processing road network…" for the real remainder of the wait instead of a frozen "100%" |
