| T187 | **Q23 — confirmed root cause and fixed, measured not guessed.** `bridge_nearby_chain_ends` (`rust/pipeline/src/coastline.rs`) was doing a flat O(n²) all-pairs scan over every remaining chain-end **every round**, with no spatial index — exactly what its own doc comment already flagged as a future risk ("a future whole-UK run... would want a spatial grid"). Confirmed by direct measurement, not inference: added a synthetic stress test (`#[ignore]`d, `bridge_nearby_chain_ends_scales_near_linearly_with_many_chains`, generates N short collinear chains each ~1m from the next, forcing N-1 real merges) and ran it against the unmodified code before touching anything — n=500/1000/2000/4000 measured 0.33s/2.85s/21.9s/165.8s, an ~8x slowdown per doubling, the textbook signature of a cubic O(rounds × n²) blowup (rounds scaling with n, each round itself O(n²)). Extrapolating that curve to the tens of thousands of chains a full GB+Ireland+IoM extract produces lands squarely in the hours range — not an infinite loop or a hang, genuinely that slow, matching Q23's own "7+ hours, zero progress" report exactly. **Fix**: added a uniform spatial grid keyed on each chain-end's own coordinate (cell size derived from `tolerance_m`, using a conservative worst-case 0.3 longitude cos-falloff floor so a 3×3 cell neighbourhood search can never miss a real candidate), rebuilt once per round rather than maintained incrementally (simpler, still a large win) — same merge logic, same four-case direction handling, byte-identical, `CoastlineChain`/`CoastlineData`'s public shape untouched. After the fix, the same benchmark measured 0.057s/0.25s/0.95s/3.87s, ~4x per doubling (quadratic, not cubic) — a 43x speedup at n=4000 alone, with the cubic term gone. One honest nuance, not swept under the rug: in the vanishingly rare case of an exact tie between equally-close candidate pairs, which one gets bridged first can now differ from the old fixed scan's i-then-j order — real OSM coordinates don't tie exactly, and it doesn't change which chains end up joined, only (in a tie) which specific pair is processed in which order. **Verified**: all 8 existing `coastline.rs` unit tests (join direction forward/backward/reversed, transitive bridging, a real gap correctly left unbridged, far-apart ends correctly staying separate) pass completely unchanged; full pipeline crate suite (`cargo test --release`) — 77 passed, 0 failed, the new stress benchmark correctly excluded by default via `#[ignore]`; `cargo build --release` clean (one pre-existing unrelated dead-code warning only, not from this change). **Not done**: did not attempt a real full GB+Ireland+IoM `.osm.pbf` rebuild (legitimately multi-hour even for the parts that already work, and the point of the synthetic benchmark was to avoid needing one) — confirmed against representative synthetic scale and the reasoning for why real coastline geometry (geographically spread across a landmass, not globally clustered) should do at least as well as this benchmark's deliberately worst-case uniform adjacency, not against the literal real extract | Claude Code | Fixed and benchmark-verified; **not yet re-run against a real full combined-map pipeline rebuild** — next time a full GB+Ireland+IoM rebuild is genuinely needed, time the coastline step and report back to confirm the real-data win matches the synthetic one |

`routeRestrictionsCompatible(a, b)` for duty-panel's own route-to-route check. **§F — where this runs today**: (1) `vehicle-buy-configure-screen.ts`'s Add-to-basket button, when buying for a specific route — `router` threaded from `main.ts` through `mountVehicleBuyMenu`/`ConfigureContext`, a new `routeOsmLimits(route)` helper replays the route's own points through `find_route_through` (same technique `route-draw.ts` uses) since a route caches no OSM-limit figure of its own; blocks and shows the reason, same treatment as an existing livery lock. (2) `route-draw.ts` gained a new persistent `restrictionInfoEl` (distinct from the existing ephemeral `warningEl`) showing any real road restriction found along the route drawn so far — `rebuildRoute()` **overwrites** the 4 running figures from the getters (it already recomputes the whole route from scratch), `appendLeg()` **folds** (`Math.min`) each new leg's own figures into the running minimum (a single `find_route` call only re-populates the router's `last_edges` with that one leg) — reset to `Infinity` on the first point and on `clear()`. (3) `duty-panel.ts`'s `canFollow` gained a `routes: Route[]` parameter and checks `routeRestrictionsCompatible` first; the separate `cachedCanFollow` closure inside auto-complete (which duplicates `canFollow`'s own logic for caching rather than calling it) got the identical check inlined against its own already-in-scope `routes`. **A real, consequential bug was found and fixed while writing the verify tests, not by inspection**: `listRoutes`'s SQL used an explicit column list that didn't include the 4 new columns, so every route read back got `baseType`/`powerType`/`axleCount` as `undefined` rather than `null` — since `undefined !== null` in JS, this would have made `vehicleRestrictionReason`'s own unset-axis check wrongly fire on EVERY route, blocking every vehicle purchase outright the moment this shipped. Fixed by adding the 4 columns to `listRoutes`'s SELECT; the live-check round-trip (below) was run once against the broken build and once against the fixed one specifically to confirm the fix, not just inferred from reading the diff. **Verified**: Rust — new unit tests for each of the 4 parsers (mirroring `parse_maxspeed_mph_handles_the_common_uk_forms`) and for the 4 router getters (a `None`-tagged edge never corrupts the minimum; `Infinity` when nothing is tagged; each of the 4 finds its own independent true minimum across a multi-edge route) — `cargo test` clean, 43 (game-wasm) + 77 (pipeline) passing. | T178 | **Ctrl-snap for zone drawing switched from individual building footprints to the broader landuse area (2026-11-13, live report: "pressing control... the border snaps to the outside of buildings not the area around them").** T118/T139's confirmed spec and build both targeted the `building` source-layer specifically; the live report asked for the basemap's own separate, broader `landuse` source-layer instead (residential/commercial/retail/industrial — confirmed directly against `basemap-style.json`'s own real layer filters, not guessed) — the visibly shaded area a cluster of buildings sits inside, not each building's own wall. Two vertices far apart on the same polygon fell back to a straight line rather than tracing — plausibly the same tile-fragment-stitching gap T118/T139's own notes already flagged as unproven against real multi-tile data, not something introduced by this change (the underlying trace logic is unchanged, only its data source moved) — not re-investigated further, flagged as still-open. | T177 | **Fixed fare zone boundaries jumping the long way round an island's coast instead of a short arc (2026-11-13, live bug report: "when making a zone around Skye it will jump the entire way around the outside of Skye... instead of just a few hundred meters along").** Root cause confirmed directly against the real data, not guessed: `coastlineWalkBetween` (`coastline-clip.mts`) assumed every coastline chain is a genuinely open polyline with exactly one path between any two points on it — `coastline.rs`'s own doc comment says chain assembly deliberately never closes a ring. But a real island's coastline IS a closed loop in reality, and `bridge_nearby_chain_ends` (the pipeline's own small-gap-closing pass) only ever merges a gap between two DIFFERENT chains, never a chain's own two ends against each other — so a fully (or almost fully) assembled island chain ends up with its own first and last point sitting at, or very close to, the same real place, with nothing in the data model saying so. Confirmed with a throwaway `cargo run --example skye_coastline_seam` (kept in the repo, the project's own "replay real pipeline data" convention, OPEN-ITEMS.md T74) read directly against the real `pipeline-data/coastline.bin`: several real chains around Skye have an **exact 0.0m** gap between their own first/last point (a single OSM way closing an island with first_node==last_node), several more sit within a few tens of metres (real digitisation slop, largest measured ~50m) — exactly the shape that makes a plain index-order walk between two points straddling that seam go almost the entire way around the island instead of the few-hundred-metre short way across it. Fixed in `coastlineWalkBetween`: a new `isEffectivelyClosed` check (chain's own first/last point within 100m, comfortably covering every real gap measured) — for an effectively-closed chain, builds the alternative path that crosses the seam instead of the direct index-order one, and returns whichever is actually shorter by real distance (not point count, which isn't a reliable proxy when segment lengths vary); a genuinely open chain is completely unaffected, still exactly the one path it always had. Both of this function's callers (`coastlineFollowBetween`, used while drawing; `clipRingToCoastline`, used when a finished zone is clipped back to the shore) get the fix automatically, since both delegate to the same function. **Verified**: extended `coastline-clip.verify.mts` with a synthetic near-closed-loop reproduction (two edges straddling a ~55m seam correctly walk the 2-point short way, not the 7-point long way round) and a regression check that a genuinely open chain is byte-for-byte unaffected; all existing checks still pass. **Verified against real data, not just synthetic**: a throwaway Node script (`verify-real-seam.mts`, scratchpad-only, not committed) imported `coastline-clip.mts` directly against a real 127-point chain (extracted via the same cargo example, real ~50m seam) and confirmed `coastlineFollowBetween` now returns a 10-point short path instead of the ~120-point long way round for two points straddling the seam. **Live-verified in the running app**: via the session's own throwaway Playwright driver, flew the map to this exact real chain's coordinates, opened "Fares → Zones → Draw new zone," and simulated two real mouse clicks at the seam's own real screen position (computed via `map.project`) — the resulting draft boundary line came back as an 11-point `LineString` (matching the short-path script result almost exactly), not the 120+ points the old long-way-round bug would have produced; cleared the draft and confirmed no test zone was left in the save. `tsc --noEmit`, the full Rust test suite (40 passing), and every other `*.verify.mts` script all still pass. `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged, verified against real data and live in the running app — ask the user to reinstall, draw a fare zone boundary near Skye's (or any island's) real coast across where it previously jumped the long way round, and confirm it now follows the short real shoreline arc instead |
| T176 | **Fare zone nesting letters (A/B/C...), real zone-count Country/Night Singles (correcting T175), and zone boundaries visible while drawing a route (2026-11-12/13, live discussion).** Started as "let the player see which lettered zone a route is in instead of the game guessing" (T175's `classifyRouteZoneType` was a pure geometry guess); turned into a real correction once the user pointed out real Lothian Country fares are zone-count-based, not distance-based — confirmed directly against `reference/Fares & Tickets - Lothian Countrry.pdf` (already in the repo), not approximated. **Zone letters**: new `computeZoneLetters(zones)` in `fare-journey.mts`, scoped per depot group, City/Country only (Island/Town never get one) — ordered by each zone's minimum distance from the City zone's own centroid (the smallest City zone is preferred as the anchor over any same-or-smaller Country zone; falls back to the smallest eligible zone if a depot group somehow has no City zone at all), not by polygon containment. The containment approach was tried first and is wrong for a real case the user raised live: Lothian zones aren't always a clean concentric ring — Zone B can be drawn as two separate pieces flanking Zone A rather than encircling it — and two rings sharing the same centre tie at distance 0 under plain centroid-to-centroid comparison, so the actual metric used is each zone's *nearest point* to the anchor (its own inner edge), which orders both a clean ring and flanking pieces correctly. Zones are grouped into "logical" identities by `(depotGroupId, zoneType, name)` before any of this runs — the same identity this codebase's own `mergeFareZone` (electron/db.mts) already uses for a zone drawn in multiple pieces — so a split zone's pieces always get the same letter and a journey touching both counts it once, not twice; caught live by the user's own flanking-zone question, not guessed. **Real zone-count fares**: new `countryZoneCountSingleFareGBPPence`/`countryZoneCountNightSingleFareGBPPence` in `fare-pricing-fixed.mts` reproduce the real table exactly (1 zone/2-or-3/4/5/6 zones: £2.40/£3.40/£4.40/£5.40/£6.40 Day, £3.50/£5.50/£8.50-flat Night), extrapolated beyond 6 by continuing the real +£1.00-per-zone Day step (Night's own top band is already flat, so it stays flat past 6 too — both confirmed with the user rather than invented). `quoteSingleFareGBPPence` (T175) rewritten: Island keeps the per-mile formula (no real zone-count precedent for it); a Country leg now counts the real zone-count via a new `legPointRange`/`countZonesForLeg` pair operating on the LEG's own board-to-alight stretch of the route (not the whole route — real Lothian prices per passenger journey), falling back to per-mile only when the leg touches no drawn zone at all; T175's Town "more expensive wins" override is dropped from this path as a flagged follow-up, not re-derived for the new pricing model. DESIGN.md §9 updated with dated correction notes (not silently rewritten) citing the real PDF directly, and the "up to two City zones" cap is lifted — any number of nested bands is now supported. **Zone boundaries while drawing**: new `showFareZonesOverlay`/`hideFareZonesOverlay` in `fare-zone-drawing.ts`, wired into `route-draw.ts`'s single `setDrawing()` function (every entry point funnels live request: "only the border in route mode, not the entire shaded area"); the Fare Zones panel's own `open()` defensively resets that visibility itself too, in case drawing mode is ever abandoned without `setDrawing(false)` running. **Verified**: extended `fare-journey.verify.mts` (new checks for nesting order, the flanking-zone case, split-zone dedup in both lettering and zone-counting, and zone-count figure plus the extrapolation) — a sort-direction bug in the first containment-based attempt (outermost zone computed as "A" instead of innermost) was caught by these tests before it pair and a real route spanning both through the actual `window.fareZones`/`window.routes` IPC, confirmed the Fare Zones panel's own rendered DOM showed "City → A" / "Country → B" badges, confirmed the map's own rendered zone labels read "City (A)"/"Country (B)", clicked through "+ New route" → "Start drawing" and confirmed `fare-zones-fill`/`fare-zones-label` layer visibility flipped to `"none"` while the outline stayed up, confirmed clicking Finish restored both to `"visible"`, confirmed the route's own list row read "outbound, 2 stops, Ordinary, Zone A, B", and confirmed `window.journeyPlanner`/`window.fareJourney.quoteJourney` against the real route returned 340p — exactly the real 2-zone Day Single figure — then deleted all seeded test data and confirmed the save | T175 | **Quoting a real fare for a planned journey, Phase 4 increment 2 — wires `fare-pricing-fixed.mts`'s own formulas to a real RAPTOR leg for the first time (2026-11-12).** T173's own entry named this as the next increment directly. New pure `fare-journey.mts`: `classifyRouteZoneType(route, zones)` reads DESIGN.md §9's "City-only vs Country buses" rule straight off a route's own drawn points (never leaves City zones anywhere along its length → city; otherwise country, or island if it ever touches an Island zone — geometrically exclusive from City/Country) — a ROUTE-level classification, not read off any one passenger's journey, exactly as that section specifies. `zoneForPoint` is a third deliberate copy of the same ray-cast point-in-ring test that already exists twice (`db.mts`'s `pointInFareZoneRing`, main-process-only; `fare-zone-drawing.ts`'s `pointInRing`, a large UI module this one must stay independent of) — confirmed the exact existing name via grep rather than trusting an earlier plan's note, which had it slightly wrong. `quoteSingleFareGBPPence(leg, route, zones, distanceMetres)` then branches on `routeType`: Airport Express flat; Night zone-classifies then applies the City/Country-Island night formulas; ordinary applies the flat City single or the base+per-mile Country/Island single (real distance, see below), with DESIGN.md's Town "more expensive wins" rule applied at the leg's board point (a `ride` leg only carries its two endpoints, not the path between, so there's no way yet to confirm the alight point also stays in the same town — a deliberate, narrower simplification than the full rule). Long-distance/Sightseeing throw a clear "not supported yet" error rather than a silently wrong fixed-figure price, since both are still player-set. **One correction made while implementing, not left as planned**: the approved plan had bundled "ordinary/staff" into the same zone-priced bucket, but `depot-groups.d.ts`'s own `RouteType` comment is explicit that staff routes are free with no fare-pricing section at all — quoting a fare for one would be exactly the kind of unrealistic figure CLAUDE.md's economy section rules out, so staff routes quote £0 instead. New `rust/game-wasm/src/lib.rs` `last_route_distance_m()`, mirroring `last_route_time_seconds()` exactly (same cached `last_edges`, summing each edge's real `length_m`) — nothing previously exposed a real routed distance at all. New thin `fare-journey-debug.ts` wires it in as `window.fareJourney.quoteJourney(journeyResult, depotGroupId)`, same debug-only-until-a-real-caller(the passenger sim)-exists treatment as T173's own `window.journeyPlanner`. **A real bug found and fixed only by live-checking, not caught by any unit test**: the debug entry point was first wired as `window.farePricing.quoteJourney` per the approved plan's own wording — but `window.farePricing` already exists, contextBridge-exposed by preload.ts for fare-pricing-panel.ts's own config IPC (`getConfig`/`listCountryZoneBands`/...), and a contextBridge-exposed property is non-writable from the renderer's main world. Reassigning it threw inside main.ts's own init chain with nothing to catch it — OPEN-ITEMS' own previously-logged "unhandled rejection silently kills boot chain" trap — silently halting every mount call after that line (fare zones, duty panel, fleet/duties/routes/timetables screens, all never mounted). Confirmed directly in a live session (reassigning the real property and reading its keys straight back showed the original IPC methods untouched, not the new one), then renamed to `window.fareJourney` instead, rebuilt, and reconfirmed every later mount call fires again. **Verified**: new Rust unit tests (`last_route_distance_m` against a known single-edge length and a 3-edge multi-hop chain with deliberately uneven lengths) plus the existing 40 all passing; new `fare-journey.verify.mts` (17 checks: city/country/island classification including a point matching no drawn zone at all, the city/night/country/island/town-override branches, a Country figure matching `fare-pricing-fixed.mts`'s own formula at a known distance, the staff-is-free correction, and the long-distance/sightseeing rejection); `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Live-verified end to end** via the session's own throwaway file-polling Playwright driver against the real WASM router: seeded a synthetic route+timetable through the real `window.routes`/`window.routeTimetables` IPC between two real Glasgow-area points, confirmed `router.find_route` + the new `last_route_distance_m()` returned a real figure (11,283.66 m ≈ 7.01 miles) for that exact road path, called `window.journeyPlanner.plan(...)` and got back a single real ride leg, then `window.fareJourney.quoteJourney(...)` returned 340p — exactly `countryOrIslandSingleFareGBPPence(7.01)` (£2.00 base + round(20p × 7.01) = £3.40) — then cleaned up the test route/timetable and confirmed the save was left empty again. `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded. Scope deliberately stops here, same as T173: no capping (needs the passenger sim's accumulated per-passenger spend), no Return/Child/NETWORK composition, no multi-leg journeys | Claude Code | Done, packaged, live-verified against the real WASM router and real IPC — no player-visible UI yet (debug entry point only, same as T173's own journey planner), so nothing for the user to check in the running game themselves; next Phase 4 increment is either the two-tier passenger simulation or demand generation from land use |
| T174 | **Fixed route 56's sibling variations disagreeing on inbound/outbound — T172's main-bus-station fix didn't actually reach this case (2026-11-12, live priority-1 bug report).** T172 gave Skye a real main bus station, but the user reported it made no difference: "Adding a bus station to Portree still hasn't fixed the 56... some are still wrong," "Nope that still hasn't fixed it. It's still completely broken." Root cause, found by reading `route-orientation.ts` directly rather than guessed: `computeRouteOrientation` checked `isCircular(points)` (`points[0] === points[last]`) **before** ever looking at the main bus station or the terminus index — and every one of route 56's variations is a terminus loop that returns to the exact same Portree stop it started from, so all of them hit that branch. Once there, the function used raw geometric winding direction (the shoelace formula) of that variation's *own* specific point sequence instead, completely ignoring the station — confirmed directly against a safe copy of the real save: 3 of the 4 variations share the literal same first/last point (Portree, osmId 562717341) and a terminus index, but each has a different number of points and a different exact detour shape (one explicitly "Excludes Castle"), so their signed areas don't agree even though they represent the same real-world direction. This explains the exact symptom — some variations happened to wind the "right" way by coincidence, others didn't, and no amount of setting the main bus station could ever fix it, since that branch never read it. Fixed in two lines: the circular/winding shortcut now only applies when no `outboundEndIndex` is given at all (a genuine circular tour route with no terminus loop concept) — a terminus-loop route always uses the existing station-distance comparison on `points[0]`/`points[terminusIndex]` instead, regardless of whether its full point list happens to loop back to the same stop. `route-draw.ts`'s one call site updated to pass `terminusIndex` through as `undefined` rather than pre-defaulting it to the last point, so `computeRouteOrientation` can tell "no terminus loop" apart from "terminus loop whose far end happens to be the last point" — only the former should ever fall back to the winding rule. Confirmed against the real save's actual route 56 data (not synthetic) that all 4 variations now agree ("outbound" for the Portree→west leg, correctly flipping to "inbound" for the return leg in the timetable grid). Since `routes.orientation` is computed once at save time and persisted, not recomputed on load, the code fix alone wouldn't repair rows already saved wrong — and the real station's coordinates aren't in the SQLite save at all for a backend migration to use (only its osmId; real position only exists once the renderer loads the actual stop/station data). So this ships with a renderer-side startup repair pass instead (`route-orientation-repair.ts`, runs once after `drawStops` every launch, cheap — a handful of routes per depot group): recomputes every route's orientation against its own depot group's real main bus station and corrects any row that disagrees, via a new dedicated `routes:setOrientation` IPC (mirroring `setColour`'s own "dedicated setter, not the general `update`" pattern — `update` unconditionally wipes `route_timetables`, which a pure orientation fix must never risk). **Verified**: new `route-orientation.verify.mts` (7 checks, including a direct repro of the bug — two differently-shaped terminus loops that used to disagree now agreeing, and confirming a genuine circular tour route with no terminus loop still uses the winding rule) and `db.verify.mts`'s full existing suite both pass; `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Live-verified against the real data**: loaded a safe copy of the real save in the dev build, confirmed the repair pass actually ran and corrected the two previously-wrong variations ("Portree - Lonmore" and "Portree - Lonmore (Excludes Castle)," both `inbound` → `outbound`) while leaving the two already-correct ones unchanged, with no user action needed beyond relaunching. `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged, live-verified against real data — ask the user to reinstall, relaunch (the repair runs automatically on load), and confirm route 56's timetable now shows Lonmore→Portree as Inbound and Portree→Lonmore as Outbound consistently across every variation |
| T173 | **RAPTOR journey planner, increment 1 — the first real Phase 4 piece, on CLAUDE.md's own "critical path for phase 4" (2026-11-12).** A phase survey showed every section built so far is Phase 1-3 scaffolding plus some Phase 6/9 work pulled forward — the game had never hit its own "first playable" milestone ("a bus carries a passenger and earns money"), and nothing downstream (demand, fare-per-journey calculation, ticket validity, the two-tier passenger sim) can be built correctly without a real "how would this person get from A to B" answer first; confirmed directly in code, not assumed — `fare-pricing.mts`/`fare-pricing-fixed.mts` already have every formula DESIGN.md §9 specifies, sitting unwired for exactly this reason. Planned in full via EnterPlanMode (three parallel Explore agents covering the Rust/WASM routing side, the TypeScript timetable/route/fare data model, and DESIGN.md's own demand/passenger spec) before any code, then implemented as a fork once approved. New `rust/game-wasm/src/journey_planner.rs`: a standard round-based RAPTOR (earliest arrival, transfer count as tiebreaker — DESIGN.md §2's own "generalised cost... waiting weighted more heavily" is deferred to a later increment, since it gives no coefficients to implement against), straight-line (haversine) walking for stop access/egress/transfers rather than real pedestrian road routing, transit data crossing the WASM boundary as a JSON string (`serde`/`serde_json`, new dependencies) rather than bincode, since — unlike the static pipeline-built road graph — this is a runtime-assembled structure built fresh from the live save, with no matching codegen on the TS side. New `src/renderer/journey-planner-data.mts` (pure, no WASM/DOM dependency, same convention as route-timetable.mts) reuses `route-timetable-grid.mts`'s own `buildRouteTimetableGrid` to turn every route+timetable component into real per-stop arrival/departure times — scoped to `direction: "both"` components only this increment, so a terminus-loop route's split outbound/inbound components contribute no trips yet. New thin `src/renderer/journey-planner.ts` wires it in (mirroring `router.ts`'s own construction pattern) as a `window.journeyPlanner` debug entry point only — no UI, no demand/passenger-sim caller yet, and no automatic rebuild-on-route-edit wiring (CLAUDE.md says RAPTOR "must be rebuilt whenever a route or timetable changes," but that's premature for a planner with no caller). A real bug was found and fixed while live-checking against a copy of the real save, not caught by the unit tests alone: footpath transfers only propagated from stops a *ride* had just improved that round, never from the stops marked by the very first origin-access walk — so a walk-then-footpath-walk journey with no bus involved at all (a real case in the save: two stops each within walking range of the origin/destination individually, where the triangle inequality means their *combined* distance can exceed what either single hop could cover) was only ever found by accident. Fixed by making footpath relaxation a shared, transitive-closure helper called both after initialisation and after every round's rides; a matching regression test added (`footpath_chained_straight_from_the_origin_with_no_ride_at_all`). A second, smaller bug from the same real-data check: `reconstruct` silently dropped the origin's own first walk leg when it was expected to be invisible (0m, the test-suite's only coverage until then) but was actually a genuine non-zero walk — fixed to emit it unless the origin sits exactly on the first stop. **Verified**: 6 new Rust unit tests (`cargo test`, direct single-trip, one transfer, walking beats a slow trip, infeasible returns none, a footpath transfer mid-journey, and the origin-side footpath-chain regression) plus the existing 33 all still passing; a throwaway `cargo run --example` (`journey_planner_against_real_save.rs`, mirroring `add_portree_bypass.rs`'s own convention) replayed real routes/timetables extracted from a copy of the real save, which is what surfaced both bugs above; new `journey-planner-data.verify.mts` (11 checks) for the TS-side trip assembly; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded. **Live-verified** end to end inside the actual running app via the session's own throwaway Playwright driver: seeded a synthetic route+timetable directly through the real `window.routes`/`window.routeTimetables` IPC (the dev-mode save has no real routes of its own), called `window.journeyPlanner.rebuild()` then `.plan(...)`, and confirmed a correct single-ride journey came back with the right route id, stop ids, and board/alight times — then cleaned up the test route. Scope deliberately stops here: no demand generation, no passenger simulation, no fare-per-journey wiring yet — each is its own next increment, and this one's output (per-leg route id, exact board/alight times) was shaped specifically so they can consume it later without a redesign, per CLAUDE.md's own warning that the two-tier split "is not optional... retrofitting it is painful" | Claude Code | Done, packaged, live-verified (with synthetic data — the real save's own routes mostly use split outbound/inbound timetables, out of this increment's scope, so nothing to meaningfully query there yet). Next Phase 4 increment: demand generation from land use (`rust/pipeline/src/landuse.rs`'s `LandUseData` is written but nothing downstream reads it yet) or wiring real fare calculation to a planned journey |
| T172 | **Players can now create a bus station by grouping existing stops — no map-placement tool, closing a real gap and fixing route 56's orientation bug at its root (2026-11-08, live request).** Trigger: route 56's sibling variations showed inconsistent inbound/outbound because their depot group (Skye) had no `main_bus_station_osm_id` to anchor orientation on, and there was no way to create one — a bus station's `kind` was 100% OSM-derived, with zero player-creation path, unlike an individual stop (`player_stops`) which has had one for a while. Confirmed with the user via multiple-choice first: group existing stops into a station, don't add a new map-placement tool. New `player_bus_stations` table (`electron/db.mts`, schema unchanged at v58 — a brand new `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, no migration needed) mints only an id, mirroring `stop_groups` exactly: name lives in the override layer under entity_type `bus_station` (the same one a real OSM station's own name override already uses), membership is the SAME `stop`/`bus_station` override field every stop's station assignment already uses — just pointing at one of these ids instead of a real OSM one — and position is always the live centroid of current members, recomputed exactly like a stop_group's own. The one thing this needed that stop_groups doesn't: an osmId space disjoint from `player_stops`' own negative ids (-1, -2, -3...) — `stops-layer.ts`'s `playerStationOsmId()` offsets by a billion rather than coordinating the two tables' autoincrement counters. New `window.playerBusStations.create()/list()` IPC, mirroring `window.stopGroups` exactly. `stops-layer.ts`'s `drawStops` now fetches player stations and their manual assignments before building the `stations`/`stopsById` consts, resolves each one's centroid from members already known at that point (real OSM stops plus `player_stops`, both already merged into `stops`), and pushes it into the SAME `stops` array everything else already treats uniformly — so a player station renders via the existing `stops-stations` map layer, flows through `displayName()`, and feeds `busStationsState` (depot groups panel's own main-bus-station picker, DESIGN.md §6 — the actual field that was null for Skye) with no new rendering path needed anywhere. The bus-station ASSIGNMENT dropdown on a stop's own popup (previously picker-only) gained a "+ Create new station here" option, mirroring the stop-GROUP dropdown directly below it almost exactly (same `newGroupRow`-style sub-form shape, same immediate-link behaviour — the stop whose popup this is gets linked to the station the moment it's created) — confirmed by reading that existing pattern first, not guessed. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; extended `electron/db.verify.mts` with real assertions for the new table (distinct ids, both listed, name round-trips through the override layer under `bus_station`, a stop's assignment round-trips through the same field a real OSM station's already uses, both survive close+reopen) — all pass alongside the existing 100+ checks. **Live-verified** end to end via the same throwaway Playwright driver this session's colour picker work used: opened a real stop's popup (Bath Street, Glasgow), confirmed the station dropdown already listed a real OSM station (Buchanan Bus Station) alongside the new "+ Create new station here" option, typed a name and created one, confirmed the dropdown collapsed back showing the new station selected at "(0 m)", confirmed it rendered immediately on the `stops-stations` map layer via `queryRenderedFeatures`, then opened a *different* nearby stop's own popup and confirmed its station dropdown already listed the brand-new station as a real candidate ("George Square Bus Station (81 m)") alongside Buchanan's, with no reload — selected it there too and confirmed no error. `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged and live-verified end to end — ask the user to open a stop's popup, pick "+ Create new station here" on the bus-station dropdown, confirm it works as described, and then (separately) set it as the Skye depot group's main bus station in the depot groups panel to confirm route 56's sibling variations finally agree on inbound/outbound |
| T171 | **Colour picker rebuilt as a real saturation/value gradient picker, replacing T169's palette grid (2026-11-08, live request).** T169 shipped a palette-grid-plus-hex popup, confirmed with the user at the time — but a follow-up live report was blunt: "I want a proper colour picker for route colours like what I had, I didn't want what I now have." `colour-picker.ts` rewritten from scratch: an SV gradient square (two layered CSS gradients — white-to-transparent for saturation, black-to-transparent for value — over a `hsl(hue, 100%, 50%)` background, not a canvas) plus a hue slider plus the existing hex field, matching the native picker's own feel. Pure HSV↔RGB↔hex math (`hsvToRgb`/`rgbToHsv`/`hexToRgb`/`rgbToHex`), verified via a standalone round-trip script against 8 real hex values before shipping. Kept the exact same `ColourPicker` interface (`el`/`value`/`disabled`) and the same portaled-popup open/close/position architecture as T169 (still the actual fix for the original multi-monitor bug this component exists for), so none of the 5 existing call sites needed any change. Pointer-capture drag (`setPointerCapture`) on both the SV box and the hue slider; unlike the old grid's discrete pick-then-close, dragging fires `onChange` continuously and does **not** close the popup — only outside-click, Escape, or the trigger itself closes it now. `theme.css`'s `.colour-picker-grid`/`.colour-picker-swatch` rules replaced with `.colour-picker-sv`/`.colour-picker-sv-cursor`/`.colour-picker-hue`/`.colour-picker-hue-cursor`. **Verified live** (not just a screenshot, learning from T169/T170's own miss): launched the dev build via a throwaway Playwright driver, opened a depot group's colour picker, simulated a real pointer drag on the SV box (confirmed the cursor moved and the hex updated while hue stayed fixed), simulated a drag on the hue slider (confirmed hue changed while the previously-set saturation/value were preserved — correct HSV independence), and confirmed clicking outside closed the popup cleanly and updated the trigger swatch. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged and live-verified via automated drag — ask the user to install and confirm the feel is right (drag the gradient square and the hue slider on a route's colour swatch, type a hex value, confirm it still opens in the right place on their multi-monitor setup) |
| T170 | **Fixed T169's colour picker shipping permanently stuck open on every row — exactly the regression "not live-checked" was flagging a risk of (2026-11-08).** Live bug report with a screenshot: every route row's colour-picker popup open simultaneously from the moment the app launched, covering the whole list, with no way to close any of them. Root cause, confirmed directly by reading the CSS rather than guessed: `theme.css`'s new `.colour-picker-popup` rule set `display: flex` directly on the class — since an author stylesheet's `display` declaration always overrides the browser's own default `[hidden] { display: none }` rule regardless of specificity, `colour-picker.ts`'s `popup.hidden = true` (set on creation and on every `close()`) had no visible effect at all; the popup rendered open unconditionally, for every instance, the instant its row did. `.dropdown-menu` (the component this one's architecture was deliberately copied from) never hit this because it happens to need no `display` override of its own — left to its element's plain block default, so the native `[hidden]` behaviour was never fought. Fixed by moving `display: flex` into a separate `.colour-picker-popup:not([hidden])` rule, so it only applies while the popup is actually meant to be visible. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded. Not live-checked for the same tooling-reliability reason T168/T169 both hit — flagged rather than claimed | Claude Code | Done, packaged; **not yet checked live** — ask the user to reinstall and confirm every colour picker (route list, route drawing, fare zones, depot groups) now stays closed until clicked, and closes again afterward |
| T169 | **A custom colour picker replaces every native `<input type="color">` in the game (2026-11-08, live request).** The native picker's own Chromium-rendered popup is a known weak spot on a multi-monitor setup with mixed DPI scaling — it can land on the wrong monitor or get clipped, and there's no config flag that fixes it; the only real fix is not using it. Confirmed with the user via multiple-choice: replace it everywhere (not just the one place they first noticed it), as a palette-grid-plus-hex-input popup (not a full HSV/gradient picker). New `src/renderer/colour-picker.ts`'s `createColourPicker(initial, onChange)` copies `dropdown.ts`'s own trigger + portaled-popup architecture exactly (document.body portal on open to escape ancestor stacking contexts, portal back on close for automatic cleanup, viewport-aware flip-above-if-no-room-below positioning, outside-click/Escape to close) rather than reinventing it — the preset grid reuses the existing `DISTINCT_DISPLAY_PALETTE` (`route-combine-variations.mts`, T164's own family-tab-collision palette, now exported since it was already a well-chosen distinct set), and the hex field commits on blur/Enter (same convention `route-timetable-panel.ts`'s own time/custom-departure inputs already use), reverting to the last real value on invalid input rather than passing garbage through. New theme.css block (`.colour-picker*`) matches `.dropdown*`'s own naming/visual language. | T168 | **Duties, Routes, and Timetables bottom-bar sections wired up — closes out the five-section conversion T162 started — plus a real colour-collision bug found and fixed in the timetable grid itself (2026-11-08).** Built as two parallel forks (Duties; Routes+Timetables together, since both touch route-panel.ts's mode system) scoped to never touch `main.ts`/`OPEN-ITEMS.md`/build output, wired together by hand afterward — confirmed with the user first that parallelising forks is only safe when they share no file, and that Routes/Timetables genuinely are one coupled change (both live in route-panel.ts), not two independent ones. **Duties**: `dutyPanelState` gained a `close()` (the existing full-screen overlay had no way to be closed externally); new `duties-screen.ts` picks a depot group first (skips the picker outright at exactly one group, shows a small top-center picker at 2+, a "create one first" note at zero) then opens the existing tool. **Routes**: new exported `routePanelState` (`route-panel.ts`) bridges "+ New route" (now also reachable as a one-shot action, not just the list's own button) and a new `timetablePickMode`; `long-distance-sections-panel.ts` converted to `open()`/`close()` like every other section this pass (lost its own standalone toggle); new `routes-screen.ts` is a two-tab bar ("+ New route" / "Long-distance sections") where the first tab is an action, not a panel. **Timetables**: new `timetables-screen.ts`, a small banner (not a tab bar — one toggle, not multiple sub-features) that flips `timetablePickMode` on, which makes every row in the always-visible route list jump straight to its own timetable instead of expanding — confirmed with the user as the intended replacement behaviour, not an addition alongside the normal row click. | T161 | **Driver roster backend, a "Staff bus" route type, and a depot's own staff-route stop — plus a real node:sqlite foreign-key bug found and fixed in two migrations (2026-11-08).** A long live-discussion session, picking the driver-duties thread back up: confirmed scope for a first driver increment (name + home depot + score, no wages yet, one driver permanently assigned per duty, no daily rotation) via AskUserQuestion, then the user asked, in sequence, for a "staff bus" route type connecting a depot and bus station for driver commuting, the map badge to show a person icon instead of a route number for it, "any vehicle except a minibus" (recorded as a design fact only — see below), and a depot's own placeable, staff-route-only, depot-named stop. Also discussed and fully confirmed, but **not yet built**, a detailed vehicle-duty time-rules spec (18h continuous-away cap, a 22:00–01:00 forced depot break for anything that would exceed it, an ordinary-to-night exception that skips the break and runs straight through to the end of the night block, a 06:00-earliest-start rule for a duty that will touch night work, a long-distance exception trading the 18h cap for a 60min inter-leg clean + 90min post-round-trip depot clean, and a flat 50min/day depot minimum standing in for a cleaner-shift gate that doesn't exist yet) — queued for its own increment, since building it blind without the driver/vehicle groundwork first would have been premature. **Schema v57→v58**: new `drivers` table (depot_group_id, depot_id, name, score — trainee/novice/competent/professional/fleet_elite, OPERATIONS.md §6's own 5-level driver score, player-set directly since the real hours-worked progression doesn't exist yet); `duties.driver_id` (nullable FK, one driver per duty); `route_type` gains `'staff'` — which forced a genuine rebuild of `routes` itself (a CHECK constraint can't be altered in place, and ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN only ever fixes a *new* column's own constraint), so the opportunity was taken to drop the CHECK entirely rather than just append a value, matching the precedent `variation_letter`'s own mutual-exclusivity rule already set (enforced by TypeScript typing at every real call site, not a CHECK); `player_stops.depot_id` (nullable FK) for a depot's own stop. **A real bug found running the full suite, not assumed safe**: `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync` defaults `PRAGMA foreign_keys` to **ON** (confirmed directly — a fresh in-memory DB reports `{foreign_keys: 1}`), contradicting this file's own much older comment claiming otherwise. SQLite's `DROP TABLE` performs an implicit `DELETE FROM` first when foreign keys are enforced, checked against every real dependent row — so the routes rebuild's `DROP TABLE routes` failed outright with "FOREIGN KEY constraint failed" against the real save's own `route_timetables`/`duty_pieces`/vehicle rows. Fixed with SQLite's own documented 12-step table-rebuild procedure (`PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` around the rebuild, `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` before turning it back `ON`, throwing if that check finds anything). The exact same latent bug was found in the existing T157 duties v55→v56 migration (never triggered before only because nothing had put a real row in `duty_pieces` at the moment that migration last ran against real data) and patched the same way, defensively, while in there. **Built**: `createDriver`/`listDriversForDepotGroup`/`renameDriver`/`setDriverScore`/`deleteDriver` (the last un-assigns from any duty first, no FK cascade in this DB) and `setDutyDriver` in `electron/db.mts`, wired through `main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` as `window.drivers.*` and `window.duties.setDriver`. A new `src/renderer/driver-names.mts` (plain UK first/surname lists, injectable rng, its own `.verify.mts`) for "+ Hire driver"'s random-but-editable name. `routeType: "staff"` added everywhere a `RouteType` is enumerated (`route-panel.ts`'s type picker, `livery-seed-data.mts`'s category mapping — falls through to "no restriction," any livery is fine, same treatment "sightseeing" already gets) and excluded from the fare-pricing panel by simply never gating a section on it. `vehicle-simulation.ts`'s map badge: a staff-route vehicle shows the existing (previously unwired, DESIGN.md §11-flagged) `support-vehicle-icons.ts` person icon in place of the route-number text, upright rather than bearing-rotated since it carries no directional meaning — the badge/circle underneath is untouched. A depot's own staff-route stop: `createDepotStop` (electron/db.mts) sits it at the depot's own `{lon, lat}` directly, no kerb-snap needed (every dead-mileage/pull-out/pull-in calculation already routes to/from a depot's raw point); `depot-placement.ts`'s own popup gained an "+ Add/Remove staff-route stop" button; visibility is enforced entirely in `stops-layer.ts`'s `visibleStops()` (invisible/unclickable unless `routeDrawState.isDrawing && routeDrawState.draftRouteType === "staff"` — a new field on the existing shared `routeDrawState`, set in `route-draw.ts`'s `setDrawing` at the one moment that matters, since no in-draft type toggle ever reaches "staff" mid-session) and its display name resolves live from the depot's own current name. New `stopsPanelState.registerDepotStop`/`unregisterDepotStop` hooks so a stop created from the depot popup (a different module from stops-layer.ts's own "Place stop" flow) shows up immediately rather than needing a reload. **Explicitly not built, recorded as a known gap**: "any vehicle except a minibus" for staff routes has nowhere to attach to at all yet — checked directly, not assumed: there is no vehicle-model-to-route-type assignment/lock mechanism anywhere in this codebase for *any* route type (OPERATIONS.md's "open-top double-deckers work sightseeing only" work-type-flag rule is itself only ever enforced indirectly, through livery compatibility, never as its own check) — building one was out of scope for this pass. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; new `db.verify.mts` assertions for every driver CRUD function, `setDutyDriver` (including the delete-unassigns-first case), `createDepotStop` (location, depot link, cascade-delete), and `'staff'` surviving `createRoute`/`listRoutes`; all 5 other affected `.verify.mts` suites re-run clean, including the real save migrating end-to-end to schema v58; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | **Backend done, packaged; UI not wired up yet** — hiring a driver, viewing the roster, and assigning one to a duty all need a `duty-panel.ts` UI pass before any of this is actually usable in-game (drivers can currently only be created via the IPC layer directly). Vehicle-duty time rules are fully specified above but entirely unbuilt. Once the UI exists: ask the user to hire a couple of drivers, assign one to a duty, draw a "Staff bus" route between a depot (with its own stop added via the depot popup) and a bus station, and confirm the map badge shows the person icon for it |
| T160 | **Timing points can now be flagged while drawing/editing a route, and copy across to a new variation (2026-11-08).** Two live bug reports after T159 shipped: "All stops are just numbers, also when making a route there is no way to set a timing point," followed by "Timing points should also be copied between variations." Investigated directly rather than guessed — drove the real packaged app via a throwaway Playwright driver (the project's own `.claude/skills/run-desktop/driver.mjs`, restarted as a file-polling variant since the stdin-REPL version died to a FIFO/job-control issue on Windows git-bash) against the real save, not a copy, closed cleanly with no Save/Clear ever pressed. Confirmed **"no way to set a timing point when making a route"** directly: the "Editing route" stop list (route-draw.ts) only ever had Edit/× per stop, no timing-point control at all — T159 only added the checkbox to route-panel.ts's separate locked stop list, reachable solely from an already-saved route's Timetable editor. Could **not** reproduce **"all stops are just numbers"** anywhere checked — the route list's own destinations, the timetable editor's stop list, and the route-drawing stop list all showed real names (e.g. "Waverley Bridge," "Shandwick Place (Stop SD)") correctly in the live screenshots; flagged back to the user rather than guessed at further, since a repro contradicted the report. **Built**: `routes.timing_point_indexes` (already a route-level column, T159) is now also set from route-draw.ts itself, not just route-panel.ts's `setRouteTimingPoint`. `createRoute`/`updateRoute` (electron/db.mts) gained a trailing `timingPointIndexes` parameter — `updateRoute`'s own column write changed from an unconditional `'[]'` reset (pickup_dropoff_overrides keeps that, unchanged, since route-draw.ts has no UI for it) to the caller's actual list. New `draftTimingPointIndexes` state in route-draw.ts, parallel to `hailRideSections`: seeded from `route.timingPointIndexes` in `startEditing`, and — directly answering the second report — from `parent.timingPointIndexes` in `startVariation`. A plain checkbox (no time inputs; those stay route-panel.ts-only, since route-draw.ts has no day-type context) added to each interior stop's row in the stop list, skipped for point 0 and the route's own last point (always-implicit timing points, same convention as everywhere else this is checked). Remapped correctly on every point-index-shifting operation already in the file — insertion (`addPoint`), single removal (`removePoint`), and "Diverge from here" (`draftPoints.splice(cutFrom)`) — matching exactly how `terminusIndex`/`startIndex`/`hailRideSections` are already kept in sync through the same three operations, not a new ad-hoc mechanism. `electron/main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` wiring extended for the new parameter on both `routes:create` and `routes:update`. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; new `db.verify.mts` assertions (`createRoute` round-trips an initial list, `updateRoute` replaces rather than resets); all 4 other affected `.verify.mts` suites re-run clean; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged; not yet checked live — ask the user to flag a timing point while drawing/editing a route and confirm it saves, create a variation from a route with timing points already flagged and confirm they carry over, and (separately) ask the user exactly where they're seeing stops shown as plain numbers, since it didn't reproduce in any of the screens checked |
| T159 | **Terminus-loop timetables lose the "Both directions (shared)" option; timing points move from the timetable to the route (2026-11-07).** Two live requests in the same session, both ahead of a planned driver-duties/relief discussion ("Before any of that can you remove both directions..." / "Also timing points should be set in the route and the times set in the timetable"). **Direction:** `route_timetables.direction` (schema, unchanged — still `'both'`/`'outbound'`/`'inbound'`, since every ordinary non-loop route still only ever has a `'both'` row and the UI never shows it a direction picker at all) keeps its type; the change is entirely in `route-timetable-panel.ts`'s direction dropdown, shown only for an actual terminus-loop route (`isTerminusLoop`): now offers only Outbound/Inbound, defaulting to Outbound, both for a brand-new component and for the panel's own initial-load guess. Checked the real save directly before deciding whether to migrate anything: route 51 (Airlink 100) and route 53 (AIR) both still carry old `'both'` rows for masks that already have full outbound+inbound rows alongside them — already-dead data per the schema's own documented "prefers leg-specific over 'both'" precedence, so left alone rather than migrated (same "shown as-is, player edits manually" precedent T157 used for pre-existing day-type components) — nothing reads them any differently than before, they just stop being reachable from this editor. **Timing points:** DESIGN.md §4 ("Timing point — set on the stop, overridable per route") and §6 ("timing points are per route") already specified this at the route level; the actual implementation had drifted into storing the full `{pointIndex, legMinutes, dwellMinutes}` list inside every separate `route_timetables` row instead, meaning every new day-type/direction component needed the same stops re-flagged from scratch — a correctness fix against the written spec, not new design. Schema v56→v57: new `routes.timing_point_indexes` column (plain JSON array of numbers, same "indexed against this route's own points, cleared on updateRoute" convention `pickup_dropoff_overrides` already uses), a plain `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` with no rename-rebuild risk (T141/T157's own FK gotcha doesn't apply to a column add). New `setRouteTimingPoint(db, routeId, pointIndex, isTimingPoint)` (electron/db.mts, mirroring `setRoutePickupDropoffOverride`'s exact shape) — flagging a point needs no further action; unflagging one also strips that pointIndex out of every existing component's own `timing_points` JSON, so no orphaned time entry for a point the route no longer recognises sits around. `route_timetables.timing_points` keeps the same on-disk shape (`{pointIndex, legMinutes, dwellMinutes}`) but now means "the times at this component for one of the route's own flagged points," not "which points are flagged." Also fixed in passing: `electron/db.mts`'s own `TimingPoint` interface said `dwellSeconds` while `route-timetable.mts`'s said `dwellMinutes` — a pre-existing, harmless (pure type-annotation, JSON.parse doesn't check it) but confusing mismatch, unified on `dwellMinutes` since that's what's actually stored. **UI**: `route-panel.ts`'s stop list (shown while a timetable is being edited) — the "Timing point (this route)" checkbox (already worded as route-level, now actually is) now calls `window.routes.setTimingPoint` and updates `route.timingPointIndexes` locally instead of writing into `timetableEditorState.timingPoints`; the leg-minutes/dwell inputs next to it still write to `timetableEditorState.timingPoints` as before, since those times still genuinely vary per component. `route-timetable-panel.ts`'s hint line now reads "N of M timing points timed for this day type" (M from the route, N from the component) instead of a single ambiguous count. New `electron/main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` wiring for `setTimingPoint`, same pattern as the pickup/dropoff override setter. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; new `db.verify.mts` assertions for `setRouteTimingPoint` (flag/unflag, sort order, and the cross-table cleanup into an existing component) plus the real save migrating end-to-end to schema v57; all 5 previously-affected `.verify.mts` suites re-run clean; `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged; not yet checked live — ask the user to open a terminus-loop route (Airlink 100/AIR) and confirm the direction dropdown only offers Outbound/Inbound, then flag a timing point from the stop list and confirm it now persists across switching day type/direction instead of needing to be re-flagged |
| T158 | **Private hire removed as a mechanic; Express 100 and Lothian Coaches liveries removed (2026-11-07).** Live request, given ahead of a planned driver-duties discussion: "I want to remove private hire work as there will be too much to do when playing, Express 100 [corrected from an initial "Express 200"] as that route no longer exists and I can't find reference for it and Lothian Coaches as they were only for private hire." Private hire was design-only — Phase 7+ in CLAUDE.md's build order, never actually built in code (confirmed by grep: no private-hire logic anywhere in `electron/db.mts`) — so this was a documentation-and-content removal, not a code/schema change. Grepped every one of CLAUDE.md/DESIGN.md/OPERATIONS.md/UK-EXPANSION.md/VEHICLE-SPECS.md for every "private hire" mention (over 25 across the 5 files) and removed or reworded each one in place: CLAUDE.md's phase 9 scope and economy-tuning paragraph; DESIGN.md's own `### Private hire` section deleted outright, plus its fare-cap exclusion list, the Fort William ticket-book exception (reworded to "tour work," the nearest surviving non-scheduled use), the school-run/private-hire-vehicle sentence, the Tour routes section's two cross-references to "private-hire-flagged vehicles" (reworded — tours now just draw on the fleet's ordinary coaches/Sprinters, since the flag itself no longer exists), the Skipping-time example, and the finance-categories table (moved "Marketing" to its own row rather than deleting the whole row, since it still needed a home); OPERATIONS.md's vehicle work-type-flag paragraph (now just open-top double-deckers/sightseeing), the accessibility table row, the casual-roles/pay-floor examples, the entire `### Private hire drivers` subsection, the overtime example (reworded to a multi-day tour), and the depot managers' duties sentence; UK-EXPANSION.md's STANDARD exemption pattern and the TfL exemption list — and, since Wales's TrawsCymru row only ever differed from "Standard" by its private-hire treatment, that row's Exemptions column is now just "Standard" too, flagged here as a real (not just textual) consequence rather than left silent; VEHICLE-SPECS.md's "+2 seats" rule (deleted outright, no surviving category to attach it to) and the toilet-requirement bullet. Code: `livery-seed-data.mts` — removed the `Express 100`/`Lothian Coaches` colour entries, their `OPERATOR_LIVERIES` membership (First Bus / Lothian), and `Express 100`'s `LIVERY_SPECIFIC_ROUTE` placeholder entry (was one of 4 "doesn't exist in the game yet" placeholders, now 3: Air/SkyLink/Oxford Tube), with the surrounding comments' counts corrected (8→7 route-locked liveries). `vehicle-catalogue.mts` — removed the two Mercedes-Benz Sprinter/EVM Cityline "stepped entrance" variants' "Used only for private hire work" notes (the vehicle configs themselves are untouched, just lost their now-meaningless purpose note) and the `GENERAL_RULES` comment's reference to private hire's +2-seat rule. Deleted `assets/liveries/Express 100.png` and `assets/liveries/Lothian Coaches.png` via `git rm`. Checked first, not guessed: grepped for a separate seeded depot group named "Lothian Coaches" (none — it was livery-only) and for any `.verify.mts` asserting on any of this removed text (none). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` + `npm run installer` both succeeded | Claude Code | Done, packaged; not yet checked live — ask the user to open the buy menu's livery picker for a Lothian or First Bus route and confirm Lothian Coaches/Express 100 no longer appear in the list |
| T157 | **Custom per-day timetable day-type picker, built end to end (2026-11-07).** Live request: "Can I also have the days timetables run be selectable per day, Airlink 100 runs the same service 7 days a week but I had to make the same timetable 3 times." The schema already stored exactly this — `route_timetables.weekday_mask` (a 7-bit bitmask, schema v19→v20) — but nothing past the 3 fixed presets (Mon-Fri/Sat/Sun) had ever been exposed; this increment is entirely UI/API-surface, no new schema needed for timetables. Confirmed through several rounds of direct questions: any number of day-type components per route (not capped at 3); overlapping days between components for the same direction are blocked by greying out already-taken days in the picker, not by a post-save error; term-time facet (`term_facet`, already in the schema too) stays out of scope, deferred; component labels are auto-generated from the day selection (e.g. "Mon-Fri", "Tue, Thu", "Every day"), not player-typed; the picker itself is 7 day-toggle buttons plus Mon-Fri/Weekend/Every day quick-preset buttons that pre-fill the toggles; an existing route's current (up to 3) components show as-is, no automatic merge-detection — the player manually deletes/widens if they want to consolidate; the full-screen printed-timetable grid's day-type bands (previously a fixed Mon-Fri/Sat/Sun loop) become dynamic, one band per the family's own actual distinct masks. **Scope was then widened live, against the initial recommendation**: duties (driver/vehicle rota grouping, `duties.weekday_mask`) are included in this same pass rather than deferred — but a duty's own day type is NOT a freely independent picker; it **tracks whatever distinct weekday patterns the depot group's own routes actually use** (confirmed: "Track the routes' own patterns"), so the duty panel's day-type bar is now dynamically rebuilt from the depot group's own routes' timetables instead of a fixed 3-button bar. **Built**: `route-timetable.mts` gains `WEEKDAY_BITS`/preset masks (`MONDAY_FRIDAY_MASK`/`SATURDAY_MASK`/`SUNDAY_MASK`/`WEEKEND_MASK`/`EVERY_DAY_MASK`)/`validateWeekdayMask`/`weekdayMasksOverlap`/`describeWeekdayMask` (the auto-label generator — groups consecutive selected days into ranges, Mon-first with no midnight-style wraparound, so Sun+Mon is two single-day entries not one range; 18 new assertions in its own `.verify.mts`, all passing). `db.mts`: `RouteTimetable.dayType`/`Duty.dayType` (the old fixed `DayType` 3-value union) replaced outright with `weekdayMask: number` throughout — `upsertRouteTimetable`/`createDuty`/`listDutiesForDepotGroup` all take a raw mask now; `term_facet` stays hardcoded `'any'` in `upsertRouteTimetable` (deferred, per above). **Duties migration (schema v55→v56)**: `duties.day_type` (the old 3-value TEXT enum) becomes `weekday_mask INTEGER CHECK (BETWEEN 1 AND 127)`, same scheme as route_timetables' own v19→v20 migration. **A real migration bug found and fixed while verifying this, not assumed safe**: following route_timetables' own precedent exactly (RENAME old table away, CREATE new with the old name, copy, DROP the renamed-away one) silently broke `duty_pieces.duty_id`'s foreign key — SQLite's `ALTER TABLE RENAME` automatically rewrites OTHER tables' FK text to follow a renamed table (confirmed directly, same underlying behaviour T141 already found and fixed once for `repaint_shop_entrances`/`repaint_shops`), so renaming `duties`→`duties_v55` silently rewrote `duty_pieces`' FK to `REFERENCES duties_v55(id)`, and dropping `duties_v55` at the end then left `duty_pieces` permanently referencing a table that no longer existed — `deleteRoute` threw `"no such table: main.duties_v55"` the moment a duty_piece existed, caught by actually running the full `db.verify.mts` suite (including its real-save migration test) rather than trusting the migration unseen. route_timetables was never exposed to this because nothing has an FK on it. Fixed by building the new table under a temporary name (`duties_new`) first, copying into it, dropping the OLD `duties` outright, then renaming `duties_new`→`duties` — `duty_pieces`' FK text already said "duties" the whole time and is never touched by a rename that only ever involves names it never referenced. Re-ran the full suite clean afterward, including the real save migrating end-to-end to v56 and a duty actually being created against it. `electron/main.ts`/`preload.ts`/`depot-groups.d.ts` (the ambient global types) all updated to match. **UI**: `route-timetable-panel.ts`'s old 3-value dropdown replaced with a pill list (one per existing component for the route's current direction, click to edit, × to delete) plus a "+ Add day type" button opening the toggle/preset picker described above; `stops-layer.ts`'s full-screen grid day bands and `duty-panel.ts`'s day-type bar both rebuilt dynamically as described; `vehicle-simulation.ts`'s manual day-of-week map-preview selector (top-right corner, a stand-in for a real calendar) also made dynamic, sourced from every distinct mask across the whole save's timetables. `tsc --noEmit` clean, every affected `.verify.mts` (`route-timetable`, `duty-journeys`, `stop-calling-services`, `route-timetable-grid`, `db`) re-run and passing, installer rebuilt | Claude Code | Done, packaged; not yet checked live by the user — ask them to open an existing route (e.g. Airlink 100, which prompted this), delete its 3 separate Mon-Fri/Sat/Sun components and replace with one "Every day" component, confirm the full-screen grid and the duty panel's day bar both reflect it; also worth checking the day-picker's overlap-greying actually disables already-taken days rather than just visually dimming them |
| T156 | **Two live bug reports fixed: route branding stuck on "First Training," timetable couldn't be saved with zero services (2026-11-07).** "I cant set a branding to none, it defaults to first training and if i set it to none and save it jumps back to first training. I also cant set a timetable to have no services on a day, it says I have to have at least one." (1) Route branding's auto-default logic (route-draw.ts, T153) ran on every refresh and treated `null` (the deliberate "none" choice) as "invalid, needs resetting" — no real livery has `id === null`, so it silently overwrote "none" back to `eligible[0]` immediately after every pick. Fixed by only falling back to `null` (not to some other eligible livery) when a previously-chosen one stops being eligible, and never auto-picking a default at all otherwise. (2) The actual "First Training" default was a second, compounding bug: `liveryEligibleForRoute` (T153/T155, livery-seed-data.mts) had no exclusion for link-only liveries (the 4 training ones, which deliberately have no entry in OPERATOR_LIVERIES — see T155) — nothing in that function's route-type/area checks applied to them, so they leaked through as "eligible" for any ordinary route's branding picker. Fixed with a guard at the top (`liveryOperator(liveryName) === null` → not eligible), which correctly excludes only the 4 training liveries while leaving Stagecoach Electric/First Electric selectable (they do have a real operator entry, despite also being linked). (3) The timetable "add at least one band or custom departure" check (route-timetable-panel.ts) was a pure UI restriction with nothing requiring it underneath — checked directly before removing it, not assumed: `generateDepartureMinutesForBands([])` already returns `[]` safely, and `validateNightWindow`/`validateNightFinishTime` both explicitly treat an empty minutes list as valid already. Removed outright; zero bands and zero custom departures is now a real, saveable "this day type runs no service" state. Verified directly (`liveryEligibleForRoute('First Training', ...)` now false, `'First Bus'`/`'Stagecoach Electric'` still true) rather than assumed. `tsc --noEmit` and the full 26-script verify suite both clean | Claude Code | Done. Not yet packaged into an installer or checked live |
| T155 | **Vehicle map badge: route number upside-down, sizing, zoom scaling — live bug report with screenshot (2026-11-07).** "I've noticed the route number on the buses is upside down, also the icon should face the direction the vehicle is traveling in. Can the route number be a bit bigger please and can the icons scale in size please as I zoom out." Checked the direction math first rather than assumed it was wrong: `bearingDegrees()` (vehicle-position.mts) is a correct forward-azimuth calculation, and `icon-rotate`/`icon-rotation-alignment: "map"` on the badge already uses it correctly — the badge genuinely does face the real travel direction. The real bug: `text-rotate` on the route-number layer used the same raw bearing, which reads upside-down for any heading between 90°-270° (south-ish) — correct literally (it does rotate with the badge, DESIGN.md/Q14's own confirmed choice) but unreadable in practice. Fixed with a normalizing expression (validated directly against `@maplibre/maplibre-gl-style-spec`'s own parser, not just eyeballed) that flips 180° past the readable boundary, so the digits always read left-to-right regardless of which way the bus faces — the badge's own rotation (true heading) is untouched. Also: `icon-size`/`circle-radius`/`text-size` all already scaled with zoom, but every one of them only had stops starting at zoom 10 — clamped to that same size below it, which on a whole-UK map reads as "doesn't scale when zoomed out further," exactly the live report. Added a zoom-4 stop to each (circle, badge icon, circle-number text, oneway-arrow) and bumped the badge's own number text-size range (6-14 → 8-18, live request: "a bit bigger"). `tsc --noEmit` and the full 26-script verify suite clean; this file (vehicle-simulation.ts) has no dedicated verify script since it's DOM/MapLibre-dependent, so this was checked by reading the exact logic plus the real style-spec parser rather than skipped | Claude Code | Done. Not yet packaged into an installer or checked live |
| T154 | **Depot group colour, data side only, confirmed narrower than first assumed (2026-11-07).** Deferred from the T153 discussion ("can each depot group have its own colour so it can be automatically added to certain liveries... saving the depot group colour for later"); picked back up and asked directly rather than guessed at scope. First guess — a general fallback tint for a depot group's whole fleet — was wrong: the real answer is **a second, separate branding-mask region, distinct from route colour (T153) and not overriding it, scoped for now to the Sightseeing livery specifically** (e.g. a shared Sightseeing base pattern with a different accent per depot group). New `depot_groups.colour` (schema v53→v54, plain ADD COLUMN, same `#3b82f6` default routes already use), a `setDepotGroupColour()` setter, and a plain colour-picker row in the Depot groups panel (same `<input type="color">` pattern already used for route colour in route-draw.ts). | T148 | **Timing points with separate arrival/departure times get two grid rows, "arr."/"dep." in italics (2026-11-07).** Live request: "At bus stops where the arrival and departure times are different can that stop have 2 lines in the timetable ending with 'arr.' and 'dep.' please with the added bits in italics." The harder part was the MERGED multi-route family grid (`buildMergedFamilyGrid`), since a stop can be split on one family member's own route and plain on another's (or on the same physical stop at a different point index) — `mergeStopSequences` (already shared with `route-combine-variations.mts`) was made generic over its element type rather than hardcoded to a plain osmId, so the merge can key on a composite `osmId:arr`/`osmId:dep` string instead; a non-splitting member's single time lands on the `:dep` bucket (today's existing "the one time is the departure" convention), and a merged row only actually gets labelled "arrival"/"departure" at all when some member in the family genuinely has a real split there — everyone-plain stops stay exactly as they always rendered, unlabelled. `merged-grid-table.ts` renders the suffix as a separate `<i>` element appended after the stop name (not baked into the text), so it's visibly distinct from the real name rather than just differently coloured text. 6 new assertions in `route-timetable-grid.verify.mts` (single-route split, merged-split-vs-plain alignment, merged-no-split-anywhere-stays-unlabelled), plus all 20 pre-existing assertions in that file still pass unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` and the full 26-script verify suite both clean | Claude Code | Done.
