| T147 | **Switched to a normal real calendar; the old 28-day/13-month one kept as the company's own financial period calendar instead (DESIGN.md §3, 2026-11-06).** While settling T146's festive-day timing, realised calendar-pinned dates (Christmas Day, 2 January, term dates, seasons) can't land on the real date under the existing 13-month/28-day/364-day game calendar — asked directly rather than reverted silently: "Can we have a normal calendar, we have previously discussed having a shorter one but I think a normal one is better. The company will run on a 4 week period calendar with 13 [periods] per year, this is what some real companies do." Resolved as two separate calendars, exactly the real-company pattern: **the game's own calendar is now the normal Gregorian one** (real month lengths, leap years, real dates), while **the company's financial/operational calendar keeps the old 28-day×13-period (364-day) structure**, now explicitly scoped to wages/maintenance/rent/contract-review timing only, not the whole game. Asked and confirmed it **floats freely against the real calendar rather than realigning to 1 January each year** — accepted drift, not a bug. "Anything annual" (MOT, annual test) now means one real year (365/366 days) instead of the old fixed 364. **Also resolved, same message**: the 6pm Christmas Eve/New Year's Eve early-finish figure (T146) was never actually in question — asked whether it should mean "last bus back by X" instead of "last departure at X" (since a 5-hour route would still finish at 11pm on a 6pm departure), and the user clarified they'd just been unsure how real operators handle it, not that it was wrong — **no change, 6pm last-departure stands**, confirmed as realistic. **Also corrected 2 January's scope (T146) a second time**: not "routes physically in Scotland" (which would have wrongly caught an English-based route that happens to cross the border) but **any service based out of a Scottish depot group**, ordinary or long-distance alike; an English-based service crossing into Scotland keeps running normally. **Not built at all yet** — `game-clock.mts`/`game-clock-engine.ts` (and its `.verify.mts`, asserting `DAYS_PER_MONTH===28`/`MONTHS_PER_YEAR===13`/`DAYS_PER_YEAR===364` as if that were the whole game's calendar) still implement only the old single-calendar model, with 9 files depending on it (`depot-groups-panel.ts`, `repaint-shop-panel.ts`, `repaint-booking.mts`/`.verify.mts`, `vehicle-simulation.ts`, `bottom-bar.ts`, `main.ts`). A real rework needs: a genuine Gregorian date engine (leap years, real month lengths) for the game's own calendar, the existing 28/13/364 engine re-scoped to be the company's own period calendar only (not realigned), and every current call site reviewed for which calendar it actually means. Design confirmed in DESIGN.md §3. **Built 2026-11-06, by direct request ("Built it now please")**: checked all 9 dependent files first — `vehicle-simulation.ts`/`repaint-booking.mts`/`depot-groups-panel.ts`/`repaint-booking.verify.mts` only ever use day-index or weekday arithmetic (unaffected either way, since the 7-day week cycle is independent of month/year structure), leaving only `bottom-bar.ts` and `repaint-shop-panel.ts` (both call `formatGameDate` for display) actually needing the real calendar. Rewrote `game-clock.mts`: `gameDateTimeFromSeconds`'s year/month/day are now real (using Howard Hinnant's public-domain days-since-epoch↔civil-calendar algorithm, O(1), no loops, correct leap years including the 100/400 exceptions) with game day 0 anchored to Year 1, 1 January; the old 28-day/13-month/364-day math is kept byte-for-byte but renamed and rescoped as the company's own period calendar (`companyPeriodFromDays`/`companyPeriodFromSeconds`/`formatCompanyPeriod`, confirmed floating freely, no realignment, per the user's own choice). No DB migration needed — the persisted save state is just a raw elapsed-seconds counter, calendar-agnostic; only the interpretation function changed, so an existing save's displayed date just updates automatically. `game-clock.verify.mts` rewritten (34 assertions: real month lengths, the 4 real leap-year cases including the century exceptions, a real non-leap year-1-to-year-2 boundary, the company period calendar's own old test cases carried over renamed, formatting). `tsc --noEmit` and the full verify suite (25 scripts) both clean | Claude Code | Done. Not yet packaged into an installer or checked live — the on-screen date display (`bottom-bar.ts`) and repaint booking dates (`repaint-shop-panel.ts`) are the two places that will visibly show real dates now instead of "Month N" |
| T146 | **Festive timetables and festive pay, confirmed design only (2026-11-06).** Live request: "Can we add festive timetables at Christmas and New Year please, staff will be paid extra for some days worked." Resolved through several rounds of direct questions rather than guessed, since this touches both the day-type model (DESIGN.md §7) and the pay model (OPERATIONS.md §7), neither of which had anything for it. **Five festive day types**, each calendar-pinned (a specific date every year, not a weekday pattern — a genuinely new axis the current day-type schema doesn't have yet, see T "underlying data model" note in DESIGN.md §7): Christmas Eve and New Year's Eve (default = normal timetable with an early finish, player can build a dedicated one instead); Boxing Day, New Year's Day and 2 January (default = Sunday-level, same override option; 2 January is Scotland's own second New Year bank holiday, not observed elsewhere in the UK — a factual addition, not separately asked). **Christmas Day is its own case**: no service at all anywhere in the UK, except Greater London and Greater Manchester, which run a reduced Sunday-level service, and only on routes where at least 80% of the route's mileage is inside the city — reusing the existing mileage-threshold mechanic UK-EXPANSION.md §7/§8 already has for livery requirements (Liverpool 40%, London Zone 6 40%), just at a much higher bar and deciding whether the route runs at all rather than which livery it needs. This narrowed down from an initial broader "any UK route running at least twice-hourly" answer, refined live to London+Manchester-only once the user reconsidered. **Festive pay**: Christmas Eve and Christmas Day both triple time (3x); Boxing Day, New Year's Eve, New Year's Day and 2 January all double time (2x, the same rate already used for remote-depot away-work and small-depot Saturday installs). Applies to any staff role actually working that day, not drivers only — the user's own wording was "staff," and the existing 39-hour pay floor's "drivers only" scope is a separate, unrelated rule. Written into DESIGN.md §7 and OPERATIONS.md §7 (new "Festive pay" subsection). **Not built at all** — blocked on the same two gaps as the rest of the day-type/pay systems: no UI lets a player create a calendar-pinned day type yet (only the weekday×term-time facets exist, and no custom-pattern picker exists for even those), and the whole pay/wages system is Phase 6, not yet built. Recorded as the confirmed design for when both exist. **Follow-up, same day**: three more real figures confirmed. (1) 2 January's scope corrected — not just routes physically in Scotland, but also any long-distance route *based* out of a Scottish depot group, even on legs running into England. (2) The Christmas Eve/New Year's Eve early finish is **6:00pm last departure**, and the Boxing Day/New Year's Day/2 January/Christmas-Day-in-London-and-Manchester next-day start is **9:00am**. (3) The driver base wage (OPERATIONS.md §6 "Wages," which every other role's pay is already a ratio of but which never had a real £ figure) is now **£16.30/hour** — the real ONS ASHE 2025 median for UK bus/coach drivers, the same "real published figure" approach the fare model used, offered alongside a real Stagecoach job-advert rate and a more conservative option; the user picked the ONS median. All three written into DESIGN.md §7 and OPERATIONS.md §6. **Second follow-up, same day**: the flat 6:00pm early-finish departure time turned out to need two cases, settled over a few rounds (one proposal — comparing the terminus's and a bus station's distance to the depot — was dropped as needless once the simpler fix was spotted). Final rule: **6:00pm if the last run is already heading toward wherever the route would normally end its day anyway** (its existing short-working, not necessarily its full daytime terminus) — a plain one-way run; **5:00pm if it genuinely has to run out to the terminus and back** (an out-and-back, because nowhere sensible exists to end the day further out), **and only where that combined out-and-back doesn't exceed 4 hours** — a route whose out-and-back would take longer isn't resolved, flagged rather than guessed. Written into DESIGN.md §7. **Third follow-up (2026-11-07)**: the flagged gap closed, asked and answered directly — "What's the gap?" / "Let's have it just not run and change the 4 hours to 6." The threshold is now **6 hours** (up from 4), and **a route whose out-and-back would exceed it simply doesn't run its last working at all that day**, rather than being left undefined. Written into DESIGN.md §7 | Claude Code | Design confirmed, written into DESIGN.md and OPERATIONS.md; nothing to build yet — waits on the day-type UI (custom calendar-pinned patterns) and Phase 6's pay system. No open gaps remaining in this rule |

Card data upload (Basic/Mid) is now specifically a location constraint (depot or bus station, not a fixed end-of-day time, and can happen more than once a day) rather than the old vaguer "end of day" wording; Advanced stays real-time over 4G regardless of location. Fixed by using a plain `ADD COLUMN` with no CHECK for `is_minor_variation`, with the mutual-exclusivity rule enforced in code instead. **Real bug #2, found while investigating #1 — already shipped, live on the real save right now**: that exact corruption had already happened once before, undetected. Fixed with a new v48→v49 migration rebuilding `repaint_shop_entrances` correctly (confirmed nothing else references that table, so safe to rebuild standalone), proven by actually adding a real entrance afterward, not just that the migration itself doesn't throw. A full schema audit of the real save (every table's own FK clauses checked against which tables actually still exist) found no third instance. **One separate gap found and flagged, not fixed here**: editing an EXISTING variation (major or minor) via the route list's "Edit" button silently drops its parent/letter, since `startEditing` always clears `variationParentRoute` — a pre-existing limitation this feature inherits unchanged, not a regression, genuinely out of scope for today (needs its own careful pass). `tsc --noEmit` clean, full verify suite green (every file run with its own documented runner), installer rebuilt | Claude Code | Done, packaged; the two FK-corruption fixes are proven against the real save directly, not just plausible — the "Edit on an existing variation" gap and the building-cluster/edge-offset items above are the real next steps |

Clicking the armed row again disarms it. **Follow-up same day**: "can the dotted lines be removed please" — the existing-saved-sections overlay line (`SAVED_SOURCE_ID`) was dashed (`line-dasharray: [2,1]`), unlike every other "existing/saved" map overlay in this codebase (`route-panel.ts`'s own saved-route-preview is plain solid, just distinguished by colour/opacity) — dropped the dasharray, now solid, same visual convention as everywhere else. Full verify suite and `tsc --noEmit` clean, installer rebuilt. **Follow-up report, same day**: "the dotted lines for the long distance are still there even though you said you removed them" — investigated directly rather than assumed wrong: grepped the actual compiled JS inside `release/win-unpacked/resources/app/dist/assets/` for every literal `line-dasharray` string and confirmed only the 4 unrelated ones remain (fare-zone-drawing.ts's own town-zone border and coastline-chain dash, route-draw.ts's hail-and-ride, basemap-style.json's admin-boundary layer) — the sections-panel fix genuinely is in the shipped exe. Told the user to fully close the app and re-run the latest installer, since rebuilding doesn't change an already-running or already-installed copy. **That turned out to be a dead end, caught properly rather than repeated**: re-checked directly against the real installed copy at `C:\Program Files\Bus Game\...` (not just the release staging folder) — same result, genuinely solid. Asked the user to clarify rather than guess a third time; they sent a screenshot. **The real cause, visible in the screenshot**: not a dash pattern at all — two distinct, near-identical, solid purple lines running almost exactly parallel, which reads as "dotted" from a distance. `updateSavedLayer()` never excluded whichever section is currently being redrawn, so starting "Redraw" left the OLD saved path rendering right alongside the new draft line (which starts as an exact copy of it) until the shape actually changed — a doubled line, not a dashed one. Fixed: the saved-layer filter now excludes `redrawingSectionId`, and `render()` (already called on every relevant state change) now also calls `updateSavedLayer()` so the exclusion can't drift out of sync. `tsc --noEmit` and full verify suite (19 scripts) clean, installer rebuilt | Claude Code | Done, packaged; not yet checked live by the user |
| T118 | **Building-cluster boundary snap for zone drawing, fully re-scoped** (2026-11-01, live correction after two screenshot rounds) — supersedes part of T116's own "not yet scoped" note. Confirmed, piece by piece, **nothing built yet**: (1) holding Ctrl replaces road-snapping entirely while held (not an additional candidate alongside roads). (2) The player clicks a **first** point and a **last** point on (or near) a building cluster's outer edge, and the game fills in the detailed boundary trace between them — corrected from an earlier "one click auto-traces the whole cluster" answer, which was wrong. (3) A genuinely large gap between two such traces (e.g. across open park land) is just a straight line, not a traced path around the empty space — confirmed correct as first proposed. (4) Transitioning off a building-trace onto a plain road click (no Ctrl) connects from whichever corner of the just-traced cluster is nearest to the new road point, not necessarily the trace's own last plotted point — confirmed correct. (5) The reverse (on a road, press Ctrl, click near a cluster) connects from the road point to the nearest corner of that cluster — confirmed correct. (6) **Ctrl+Z undoes the last click — confirmed to apply across every part of zone creation, not just this feature** (the existing "Undo point" button gets a keyboard-shortcut twin). **Still not scoped at all**: whether building footprint polygon data is even loaded in the renderer (unchecked — this blocks starting), and the actual boundary-tracing geometry itself (no geometry library in this project, flagged before for the simpler 10m/15m edge-offset case, and this is a harder problem again: computing a cluster's outer boundary from footprint polygons, finding the shortest/correct arc between two clicked points along it, and the gap-detection threshold). Recommended next step: check building-footprint data availability first, before any geometry design, since everything else depends on the answer. **Checked 2026-11-05**: building footprint data is **not available anywhere in the pipeline** — `rust/pipeline/src/landuse.rs`'s own doc comment confirms this was a deliberate scope decision from 2026-09-03 ("landuse zone polygons (centroid only, not full footprints) plus selected POI points, **not individual building extraction** — Scotland+border likely has millions of buildings"). So this feature is blocked on a genuinely new pipeline pass (parsing and storing real building polygon geometry at a scale the pipeline has so far deliberately avoided), on top of the still-unscoped boundary-tracing geometry itself — a bigger undertaking than the feature's own UI/interaction design suggested, not started since it's a real scope decision (new artefact, new pipeline cost, possible performance concerns at millions-of-buildings scale) rather than a quick addition. **Correction (2026-11-05, user caught it)**: the "checked 2026-11-05" conclusion above was wrong — it only checked the custom pipeline binaries (`landuse.bin` etc.), never the basemap's own vector tiles, which is a completely separate data source and already renders the real building footprints as the beige "coloured bit" visible on the map today. Verified directly against the real file this time (parsed `pipeline-data/tiles.pmtiles`'s actual binary header and metadata, not guessed): it has a real `building` vector layer, minzoom 13/maxzoom 16, with real per-building polygon geometry already loaded into MapLibre once those tiles render — retrievable client-side via `map.querySourceFeatures`/`queryRenderedFeatures` on that layer, with **no new pipeline pass needed at all**. So the real blocker is smaller than the first write-up said, but it isn't zero: (1) a "cluster" is several separate building polygons sitting next to each other, and tracing one continuous outer boundary between two clicks needs them UNIONED into one boundary first — genuine polygon-union geometry, structurally harder than the existing coastline-snap machinery (`coastline-clip.mts`'s `nearestPointOnCoastline`/`coastlineFollowBetween`, the closest real precedent in this codebase) gets for free, since real coastline data is already assembled into single open chains, never requiring a union of several separate shapes first. (2) Vector tiles clip polygons at tile edges, so one real building spanning two tiles can arrive as two separate partial features that would need stitching back together before any union/trace step. (3) No polygon-union library is installed (`package.json` has no turf/polygon-clipping/equivalent) — checked directly, not assumed. **Still not attempted, still a real scope decision, just a smaller and more precisely-scoped one now**: it's squarely a renderer/TypeScript task (no Rust/pipeline work at all), reusing the existing `coastline-clip.mts` click-and-trace pattern as its structural model once a building-polygon union step exists upstream of it | Claude Code | Scope fully clarified via live screenshots; the "needs a new pipeline pass" blocker was wrong and is now corrected — the real remaining gap is polygon-union geometry (no library installed) plus tile-boundary stitching, both renderer-side; nothing built, still a real scope decision, not attempted without asking |

Investigating (2) surfaced that LATE/NETWORK don't map cleanly onto zone-existence, which turned into a genuine redesign, confirmed piece by piece rather than guessed: **NETWORK is no longer its own typed-in price at all** — "a percentage more than city+country... rounded up to the nearest 5p... the same percentage on all types of ticket" — so `fare-pricing.mts` gained `networkFareGBPPence(combinedFareGBPPence, markupPercent)` (shares the Return formula's own round-up-to-5p helper) and the config stores one `networkMarkupPercent`, not three prices; shown once a depot group has both city and country zones (so Combined exists to compute from) and more than 3 total zones (the user's own stated threshold). **"Special service" renamed and narrowed to Airport Express** specifically the player-flaggable airport route type (DESIGN.md §10), with its own Single+Return — confirmed as genuinely independent figures, not the standard 1.6x Return multiplier, since AIR's own real fares (£6/£8.50) are a 1.4167x ratio. **Sightseeing** gained its own two fields per DESIGN.md's existing wording (hop-on hop-off day ticket, single trip round the loop) — shown unconditionally, same as Airport Express, since there's no "has this kind of route" signal to gate on. **A new real `runsNightServices` boolean per depot group** (the user's own choice over inferring it from anything) gates LATE and the two night-extra fields. Schema v36→v37 (`fare_configs`: dropped `special_service_single_gbp_pence`/`network_gbp_pence`, added the 6 new columns), `FareConfig` updated in `db.mts`/`depot-groups.d.ts`/`fare-pricing.mts` and the panel rewritten from scratch (`fare-pricing-panel.ts`): a shared `fareRequirements()`/`missingFields()` pair (one rule, read by both the field-visibility logic and the missing-indicator logic, so they can't drift apart) drives which sections show, a red border on the toggle (computed across every depot group, not just the selected one) when anything genuinely required is unset, and a 🔴 prefix on the affected group's own name in the dropdown (`createDropdown` only takes plain strings, so this is a label prefix rather than a drawn marker — kept to avoid touching the shared component other panels also use). Network's three prices are now read-only, computed live from the current Combined prices and the markup percentage, never stored. `fare-pricing.verify.mts` extended (Airport Express, Sightseeing, 3 NETWORK markup assertions incl. a 0% no-op). Caught and fixed two stale hardcoded `user_version` assertions in `db.verify.mts`/`fare-zones.verify.mts` left over from the v37 bump. Full verify suite (16 scripts) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Live-verified via CDP** against the usual isolated scratch-copy setup: confirmed against the real save's ground truth (only Glasgow has zones — 2 city, no country) that Edinburgh/Skye/Test Group (zero zones) show no City/Country/Combined/Network sections at all, Glasgow shows City-only fields (no Country/Combined/Network, correctly gated) with its own 🔴 marker in the dropdown and a red toggle border (nothing priced yet), and ticking "runs night services" live revealed LATE + the two night-extra fields immediately. Real save confirmed untouched throughout (still schema v36, no `runs_night_services` column). New installer built; **not yet relaunched for the user's own UAC approval, and the Combined/Network gating conditions (both zone types; both zone types AND >3 total zones) were stated back to the user as reasoning rather than individually confirmed with a direct yes/no** — worth a quick check next time. **A large new 31 October hand-off was pasted mid-session and deliberately not started yet** (route-number black-border styling, Shift-for-straight-line + sea-clipping in zone drawing, detached zone parts, ticket sections, a full zone-fares-counting rework with child-half-price and LATE split into City/Country/Combined, and joint day riders) — it directly contradicts part of what this entry just shipped (LATE as one flat field vs. the hand-off's own 3-way City/Country/Combined split), flagged to address once this entry's rework is confirmed working rather than redone twice | Claude Code | Code complete, verified live against the real save's ground truth; awaiting the user's own install + the two open confirmations above |
| T107 | **Island/Town zones and ferry tickets** — a large live design discussion (2026-10-31), following straight on from T106's fare-pricing rework, started by "Can we also add an Island Zone please for places like Skye. Also for places like Arran which is in the Glasgow depot group." Confirmed piece by piece via many rounds of `AskUserQuestion`, not guessed at (the user's own explicit request: "Please ask lots of questions as I don't think I've explained it that well") — **design only, nothing coded yet**. **Island zone**: a new type parallel to City/Country, banded by zone count like Country, with its own Single/Day/Weekly/28-day/LATE. Initially confirmed as entirely outside Combined/NETWORK, **then corrected**: Island access is covered by a **NETWORK ticket** as a coverage benefit, the same treatment NETWORK already gives Airport Express — a NETWORK holder can travel on Island zone services with no extra zone charge, but this doesn't touch the NETWORK price formula itself (still just the Combined price × markup%, `networkFareGBPPence`) — Island keeps its own separately-priced ticket family for anyone not holding NETWORK. **Town zone**: a new nested type, always inside exactly one parent Country or Island zone (never City), a parent can hold several towns (e.g. Shetland could have both Lerwick and Scalloway); renders with a dotted border rather than a solid line, to read as "inside" its parent. A trip staying entirely within the town prices its Single/Day/Weekly/28-day at 70% of the parent's own 1-zone figures; once a journey leaves the town the town is invisible to zone-counting — it always just counts as 1 zone of its parent, nothing added. **Replaces "ticket sections" (hand-off §4) entirely** — that mechanism (dotted-boundary auto-cut, player-drawn) is dropped in favour of Town zones. **Ferry vs. physical connection decides the mechanism, not the depot_group_id row**: two zones connected by road/bridge (even island↔mainland, e.g. Skye's own bridge) use the ordinary same-depot-group zone-crossing rule; two zones only connected by ferry always need the ferry-ticket/joint-ticket mechanism below, even if they're technically the same depot group in the database (confirmed directly: Arran, run by the Glasgow depot group, still needs it to reach Ardrossan, because the link is a ferry, not a road). Where an Island zone links to a Town zone and they don't directly touch, the link is to whichever real ferry connection is nearest. **Joint day rider rates revised** (hand-off §6's own formula — dearer group's day rider + rate × cheaper group's day rider, 50/50 split between the two groups — kept, only the rate numbers and rounding changed): the old 0.45x (a Town zone or an automatically-computed "most direct route" corridor through a Country zone, e.g. Skye + Fort William, replacing the earlier Skye+Mallaig-as-a-section idea) is now **0.6x**; the old 0.6x (linking two full Country/Island zones) is now **0.7x**; both **rounded up to the nearest 5p** (same convention as Return and NETWORK in `fare-pricing.mts`). The corridor itself is computed automatically from the road network, not player-drawn. **Ferry tickets**, a genuinely new mechanism introduced after the rates above were confirmed, which **replaces** the same-depot-group "extra ticket" idea floated earlier in this same discussion (a flat 0.3x of the Island zone's own day rider — superseded, never built, left here only so the dead end isn't silently lost): real **ferry operators** (CalMac, Western Ferries, etc. — a new concept, nothing like it exists in the codebase yet) each set their own per-mile rate; a crossing's mileage is computed automatically from real geography, not player-entered; a ferry ticket is sold as **Single or Return only** (no Day/Weekly/28-day of its own) and can be bundled into any dayrider (daily, weekly, presumably 28-day too) but **only one single-or-return per dayrider regardless of its own duration** — a 7-day dayrider still only gets 1 return, not 7. The full computed ferry fare goes to the player at time of sale; **95% of it is paid out to the real ferry operator at month-end**, the player keeping the other 5% — confirmed to document the formula now and implement the actual monthly payout once Phase 5's finance system exists to hang it on (same treatment this project already gives other Phase-gated gaps, e.g. T100's deliberately simplified Deliveries queue). The ferry fee is **always charged separately, on top of whatever zone ticket is held — including a NETWORK ticket** — confirmed explicitly: Island zone access is bundled into NETWORK, but the ferry crossing itself never is, since a different, real company (not the player) runs the ferry. For a **same-depot-group** ferry crossing (Arran + Ardrossan), the ferry ticket is the *only* extra cost beyond the Island zone's own fare — no joint-ticket rate applies at all, since it's one operator's own zones. For a **cross-depot-group** crossing (Skye + Mallaig/Fort William), the ferry ticket is **additional** on top of the joint day rider price — both charges apply together. **Not yet written into DESIGN.md/UK-EXPANSION.md §9** (flagged there as the next step once this entry's design is confirmed stable) and nothing in `fare-pricing.mts`/`db.mts`/the fare-zone drawing tool reflects any of it yet. **Agreed build order**: Island zone first, self-contained and already fully scoped; Town zones + the ferry-vs-physical-link rule + the revised joint-ticket rates + the whole ferry-ticket/operator system together as the next, considerably larger piece, since they're all tangled together. **Docs fold done (2026-11-01)**: the Island/Town zone pricing (city/country/island/town, 70% town rule) is now in DESIGN.md §9's own "Zone types" and "Single fares"; the ferry-vs-physical-link rule, joint day rider rates (0.6x/0.7x) and the full ferry-ticket mechanism (operator per-mile, real sailing mileage, 95%/5% split) are now in §9's new "Ferry and joint tickets" subsection. **One real inconsistency surfaced and flagged inline rather than silently resolved**: this whole mechanism assumes ordinary service buses cross by ferry, which directly contradicts DESIGN.md §1's own existing "buses do not use ferries on service or contract work" line — flagged with a ⚠ note in both places, not yet reconciled, needs the user's own call | Claude Code | Design confirmed through many rounds of questions; nothing built yet — Island zone is next; docs now synced, one flagged inconsistency awaiting the user |
| T108 | **Ferry-linked long-distance routes** — live design discussion (2026-10-31), started right after T107, with a real reference document supplied: Scottish Citylink's own 923 timetable (Glasgow–Ayr–Cairnryan–Belfast via Stena Line). Read directly rather than guessed at its structure (CLAUDE.md's standing "no shortcuts, follow source material" rule) — confirmed its real shape: the "923/UB" columns are a **Scotland-side coach** (Glasgow→Ayr→Cairnryan, continuing on to Stranraer by road for domestic-only passengers — Cairnryan is just an ordinary intermediate stop on that run, not a branch point); the "Stena/UB" columns are the **ferry crossing plus the Northern-Ireland-side coach** (Belfast ferry terminal→Europa Buscentre), bundled as one leg; the connection at Cairnryan is timed with real slack (the 0805 Glasgow coach reaches Cairnryan at 1015, the ferry it connects to doesn't sail until 1115), not a tight transfer. **Route representation**: no new "split" UI or route-drawing mechanism is needed at the ferry terminal itself — the player draws and times both the Scotland-side and Northern-Ireland-side coach legs as perfectly ordinary routes, same as any other; the only thing the game itself provides is the ferry crossing (real OSM ferry-route data, rendered as a dotted line "like OpenStreetMap already does," which also supplies the real distance needed for timing and ticket mileage) linking the two at a shared ferry-terminal stop. **However, the Northern-Ireland-side continuation (or more generally, whichever leg continues beyond the ferry) does need a real new mechanism**: it must keep the **same route number** as the Scotland-side leg (923, not a new number) — confirmed this is specifically a **route-numbering** requirement, not a ticketing one (the through-ticket/fare handoff is a separate concern, covered below) — which means it has to be built as a **variation** of the original route (the existing mechanism for "same route identity, different path"). This is a genuinely new kind of variation, confirmed directly: **every existing variation today assumes the same vehicle/duty could run it interchangeably with its parent or any other variation of that route; this is the first variation that must always be run by a completely separate vehicle**, physically based on the far side of the ferry crossing — duty/vehicle assignment (CLAUDE.md's own "duties with chaining, locking and lowest-cost auto-complete" hard part) must never try to chain this variation's duty with its parent route's own vehicles, since they can never physically reach each other. Not yet designed: exactly how this "runs as its own separate vehicle" property is represented on a variation, or how duty auto-complete is told to respect it. **Fare**: the whole Glasgow→Belfast journey is sold as **one through-fare**, using the existing long-distance formula (base + per-mile, DESIGN.md §9) — not the Island-zone per-mile/95%-5% ferry-ticket mechanism from T107, which is a separate, different mechanism. Of that one ticket price, **87.5% goes to the ferry company, 12.5% stays with the player** (corrected mid-discussion — the user's first phrasing had this backwards) — "due to the complexities of running the long distance ferry routes." A major route like this (Stena Line, Cairnryan–Belfast) will carry a **higher real per-operator ferry rate** than the smaller island ferry companies once T107's real ferry-operator pricing is actually filled in — noted for later, not acted on now. **Business rules for later phases, recorded now so they aren't lost**: once the commercial-team optimiser exists (OPERATIONS.md §13a, CLAUDE.md's own "optimiser's constraints" hard part), ferry connection times must be treated as a **fixed anchor** it builds retiming around rather than ever moves, consistent with CLAUDE.md's existing "ferry locks" language; and **all Skye services, plus Route 398, must never be touched by the commercial-team optimiser at all** — they always run on real, author-supplied timetables. **Open, not yet answered**: whether to source real ferry sailing times (Stena Line Cairnryan–Belfast, CalMac Ardrossan–Brodick, etc.) via a live lookup or wait for the user to supply them the way the 923 PDF was supplied — asked, not yet answered. **Scoped to long-distance services only** — confirmed directly: this whole mechanism (the ferry-terminal linking, the separate-vehicle variation, the through-fare split) applies to the long-distance route category only, not ordinary local/regional routes. The user also asked for ferry routes to be **rendered on the map, OpenStreetMap-style (a dotted line)**, which also supplies the real distance for timing/mileage — not yet scoped or started as a pipeline feature. **Real summer sailing data gathered (2026-10-31), confirmed live, not guessed**: CalMac's own official summer timetable PDFs for **Ardrossan–Brodick** (Arran; `stt-05-ardrossan-brodick.pdf`, last updated 10/12/2025, valid for the 2026 summer season) and **Mallaig–Armadale** (Skye; `stt-18-mallaig-armadale-31-july-27-september.pdf`, last updated 30/07/2026, explicitly "OPERATES 31 JULY – 27 SEPTEMBER") were downloaded and read directly — full day-by-day sailing times for both, plus real notes (Arran's 1.98m vehicle height restriction on specific weekend sailings; Skye's real non-service dates of 7 September/17 October and its tidal-variation date list). **Stena Line (Cairnryan–Belfast) could not be reliably sourced** — Stena's own site loads times only after a JS date-picker interaction, which a fetch can't drive, and the one detailed third-party page found (niferry.co.uk) is stale 2015 archived data; the real 923 Citylink timetable already in hand (T108's own source document) confirms 3 of the real 6 daily sailings each way (matching Stena's own published "2h15m crossing, 12 daily sailings" figures). **Update: the user then supplied every remaining ferry timetable directly over a long sequence of uploads (2026-10-31)**, per their own explicit instruction ("I will upload them as I find them, please don't comment... I will ask for a list of remaining ones every now and then") — real PDFs now in hand for the entire CalMac network (all ~26 real routes, Ardrossan–Brodick through Colintraive–Rhubodach), NorthLink (Aberdeen–Kirkwall–Lerwick passenger and freight, the latter flagged **freight-only, out-of-service bus movements only**), Pentland Ferries (Gills Bay–St Margaret's Hope), the full Isle of Wight set (Red Funnel, Wightlink ×3, Hovertravel ×2), Lundy Island (the Stayers/Oldenburg passenger timetable — a separate freight-only Gry Maritha timetable was also supplied and flagged the same **no-passengers, out-of-service-only** way as the NorthLink freight one), the Isle of Man Steam Packet combined UK timetable, Ballycastle–Rathlin Island, P&O Cairnryan–Larne, and Stena Liverpool–Belfast (with fares) — completing the full ferry list from the earlier research pass. **A new, separate real data source was then added**: Loganair's winter 2025/26 timetable plus real live departure/arrival board screenshots for every island airport (Kirkwall, Sumburgh, Stornoway, Benbecula, Barra, Tiree, Islay) and Donegal Airport — confirmed scoped down to **island airports and Donegal only**, every mainland-to-mainland leg in the same screenshots (Aberdeen/Edinburgh/Glasgow/Inverness/Birmingham/Bristol/Manchester/Southampton/Exeter/Jersey/Dublin/Belfast/Norwich) was only relevant as the other end of an island route, not wanted in its own right. **Jersey flagged as out of map scope** — the whole-UK data (still not swapped in, see above) covers Great Britain, Northern Ireland and the Isle of Man, not the Channel Islands, so any Jersey-touching flight data has nowhere real to connect to on the map. **A new fare mechanism confirmed for these flights**: when a passenger's journey uses a connecting bus (either an Airport Express service or a NETWORK ticket) at *both* the departure and arrival end of one of these island/Donegal Loganair flights, **the airline pays the player 7.5% of the flight's own ticket price**, settled at month-end — the same deferred-until-Phase-5-finance-exists treatment as T107's ferry 95%/5% split, confirmed via two rounds of direct questions after the user's own first phrasing read ambiguously ("these will pay 7.5%" could have meant either direction); note the direction here is the *opposite* of the ferry mechanism's own emphasis (there, the player collects the full fare and pays the large majority away; here, the airline is the one paying the player a minority commission for feeding passengers to/from its flights). **Exception confirmed**: the Orkney and Shetland inter-island hop networks (Kirkwall↔Stronsay/Sanday/North Ronaldsay/Westray/Papa Westray/Eday, and the equivalent Tingwall-hub Shetland routes — both real timetables now in hand, branded to the respective islands council, e.g. orkney.gov.uk/transport) pay the player **no commission at all**, since they're real lifeline services, not commercial routes — consistent with DESIGN.md's own "Island and remote-area review" table already flagging both Orkney and Shetland as lifeline/mixed-subsidy areas. The 7.5% only applies to the commercial mainland-hub routes (Kirkwall↔Aberdeen, Sumburgh↔Edinburgh, etc.), not these internal inter-island hops. **Flight mileage confirmed**: unlike the ferry mechanism (T107), which uses the real sailing-route distance, a flight's own ticket price is worked out per mile on the **straight-line (great-circle) distance between the two airports** — the natural equivalent for point-to-point air travel, no path to follow the way a ferry or bus route has. **Newquay Cornwall Airport dropped**: the user had added it (with its own real Loganair Manchester–Newquay timetable, both directions) specifically as a workaround for the long-distance router lag discussed in this same session — once the real cause was found and fixed (`vehicle-simulation.ts`'s `refresh()` was rebuilding every route's entire path from scratch every 30 seconds, not a one-off per-route cost; see the fix itself, same day), the workaround was no longer needed and the user asked to drop it. The "not yet scoped or started as a pipeline feature" line above is stale. **Docs fold done (2026-11-01)**: the route-structure/numbering/through-fare-split mechanism is now in DESIGN.md §1 "Islands and ferries" (flagged against that section's own older "buses do not use ferries on service work" line — see T107's own docs-fold note, same unresolved inconsistency); the "separate vehicle" variation requirement is now in §6 "Variations," explicitly marked as **still not designed** rather than guessed at; the flight-commission mechanism (7.5%, island/Donegal only, Orkney/Shetland exempt, great-circle mileage) is now in §9's new "Flight commission" subsection; the optimiser's ferry-lock and Skye/398-exemption guard rails are now in OPERATIONS.md §13a. **Two genuine open questions remain, not resolved — surfaced for the user rather than guessed at**: (1) the ferry-service-work inconsistency noted above, and (2) exactly how a variation's "must run as a separate vehicle" property should be represented and how duty auto-complete respects it | Claude Code | Design in progress via live discussion; nothing built for the route/duty mechanism itself (ferry map rendering now is); docs synced; 2 open questions awaiting the user |
| T109 | **Route deletion silently failing** — live bug report (2026-10-31): "I have also just found I can't delete routes the button lights up but does nothing." Investigated directly against a copy of the real save rather than guessed at the cause (project standing practice): **4 of the user's 9 real routes** have `duty_pieces` rows referencing them (97/67/265/24 pieces respectively), and `deleteRoute()` never cleaned those up before `DELETE FROM routes` — reproduced exactly, a plain `FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` (node:sqlite enforces FKs by default, same as the function's own existing comment already notes for its other two cleanup steps). The renderer's delete button (`route-panel.ts`) has no try/catch around the call, so the rejection just vanished as an unhandled promise rejection — no visible error, matching the report exactly. **Fixed**: `deleteRoute()` now also runs `DELETE FROM duty_pieces WHERE route_id = ?` before deleting the route itself, the same treatment already given to `route_timetables` and `parent_route_id` right above it in the same function — a route's own duty assignment doesn't survive the route disappearing, the rest of that duty's other pieces are untouched. New regression test in `db.verify.mts` (creates a route, adds a duty_piece against it, deletes the route, confirms no throw and the piece is gone). Full verify suite (16 scripts) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean. New installer built; not yet confirmed live by the user | Claude Code | Fixed, verified via a real-save reproduction and a new regression test; awaiting the user's own install + check |
| T130 | **Ridacard, Student passenger type, and a universal Child fare rule — confirmed 2026-11-05, design only, nothing coded yet.** Follow-up to T129's own flagged "Season tickets (not yet confirmed scope)" note, from a few days earlier. Found first that this wasn't really a new feature: DESIGN.md §8 already had an unspecified "season ticket purchase is decided per passenger" line, predating this whole fare-model discussion — Ridacard is now confirmed as the concrete answer to it, not a bolt-on. Four real decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`, not guessed at: (1) **Child fares apply to every ticket family in the game**, not just Ridacard — confirmed as exactly **50% of Adult, rounded up to the nearest 5p**, verified against every real Child figure gathered across this whole discussion (City/Country/Combined Day, City Single, NETWORK, Airport Express, and now all four Ridacard tiers) — every single one is an exact 50%, not an approximation. (2) **Student becomes a genuine fourth passenger type** (alongside the existing Adult/Child/Concession), with its own real place in the demand simulation — how student demand actually gets generated (university/college land use is the obvious candidate) isn't designed yet. (3) Ridacard's **4-Week tier is reintroduced, but only for Ridacard** — explicitly not the same decision as the general 28-day ticket dropped twice earlier; Ridacard also gets a Weekly and an Annual tier. (4) Ridacard **is confirmed as the real fulfilment of §8's own season-ticket line**, meaning both the individual and statistical passenger tiers need to agree on season-ticket revenue — the same cross-tier reconciliation problem capping already has (CLAUDE.md's own flagged hard part), not solved here, just correctly flagged as the same class of problem. Real Lothian figures recorded exactly: Adult/Child/Student at Week (£26.50/£13.25/£23.00), 4-Week (£80.00/£40.00/£68.00), Annual DD (£74.00+£25 fee/£37.00/£58.00 per month), Annual upfront (£760.00/£380.00/£660.00), plus a real Student-only 9-month Sept/Oct purchase window at £570.00 (recorded as real data, not yet decided whether this game adopts that specific fifth tier). Student's own discount doesn't follow Child's clean 50% rule — it ranges 78–87% of Adult across tiers, so recorded as real fixed figures rather than a derived formula. All written into DESIGN.md §8 and §9 | Claude Code | Design confirmed through real `AskUserQuestion` rounds; nothing built; Student's own demand-generation mechanism and the cross-tier season-ticket revenue problem are the next real design gaps, not yet started |
| T129 | **Fare model reworked, major live design discussion, 2026-11-01 — design only, nothing coded yet.** Started from "all routes should be based on a set fee then an amount per mile" and went through several real pivots before settling: Singles switch from zone-banded to base-fee-plus-per-mile, but **only beyond the city** — City zones keep a flat single fare. **Capping was removed, then reintroduced** with a new real-world restriction: caps only function on buses fitted with a tap-on tap-off ticket machine (TICKETING.md), directly mirroring a genuine discovery from Lothian's own real fares (supplied as a screenshot) — their City WEEK ticket has **no separate walk-up price at all**, only the TapTapCap automatic cap, which is now the exact model used in-game: Weekly is never bought up front, only reached automatically. **28-day tickets dropped a second time** (briefly reintroduced mid-discussion, then dropped again) — capping is Day/Weekly only, never 28-day (which no longer exists) or NETWORK. **Dayriders simplified**: the player only ever sets a Day price; Weekly is computed automatically via a real fixed ratio (Lothian's own City WEEK ÷ City DAY, £26.50/£6.00 ≈ 4.42x, rounded up to the nearest 5p) — not an independent per-depot-group setting any more. **Ordinary, Night and Airport Express fares are now fixed, company-wide game constants** (not player-set per depot group) — confirmed directly ("having this done by the player is too complex"), with Airport Express folded in later via its own real Lothian Airlink screenshot (Single £6.00, Return £8.50, matching this doc's pre-existing reference exactly). **Only Sightseeing stays player-set.** **City zones**: capped at 2 per city (inner **Zone A**, outer **Zone B** — London excluded, deferred), sharing one flat £2.40 single between them (confirmed from the real Lothian screenshot too) — the A/B split only matters once a journey leaves the city, not yet fully specified (pending the user's own Lothian Country fares next). **Town zones**: no longer 70% of the parent — now a flat, fixed **£2** single; Town's own Dayrider computed from the same real Lothian Day/Single ratio applied to that £2. **Island zones**: base+per-mile like Country, base fee confirmed at **£2.40**, per-mile rate explicitly deferred pending the same Country data. **NETWORK's own markup percentage is now a real fixed figure** (Lothian NETWORK ÷ City DAY, £14.00/£6.00 ≈ 2.333x) rather than player-set, and now explicitly covers all Ordinary/Night/Airport Express services in the depot group, plus Island. Rerun against the real data: 12,901 chains → 11,415 after bridging, 1,486 real gaps closed. Deployed directly to both dev `pipeline-data/` and the live installed game's map data (no reinstall needed, pure data). Full Rust suite green (73 tests). Not yet driven live (needs the installed game closed) Typechecks clean; not yet driven live (needs the installed game closed). **Update (2026-10-09/10): a long live-testing cycle, three real bugs found and fixed via direct CDP inspection, plus the Shandwick Place loop investigation.** User reported "the route from the exit is using one way roads the wrong way" — fixed by making each route carry a `direction` column (`'inbound' | 'outbound'`, schema v27→v28, `DROP TABLE`+re-`CREATE` since nothing real existed on the old shape yet), with a trunk (dealer→fork for inbound, fork→dealer for outbound) and one branch per depot entrance (fork→entrance inbound, entrance→fork outbound) — every router call made in the real direction of travel, the actual fix, not just a cosmetic relabel. After deploying, the user kept reporting "still broken"/"exact same problem" with no further detail; each time, read the live app's own state directly via CDP rather than re-guessing, and found two more real bugs this way, back to back: (1) **duplicate routes** — "+ New route" had no guard against a double-click, confirmed live via `window.deadMileage.listForDepot` showing two routes to the same dealer; fixed by filtering out dealer entrances that already have a route in that direction from the picker. (2) **a waypoint-click race condition** — the live map's own rendered GeoJSON showed duplicate waypoint dots and a route split into malformed fragments after a single click, while the database held exactly one correct waypoint — two overlapping async click handlers each reading the same stale array and overwriting each other's write; fixed with `processingClick`/`creatingRoute` boolean guards (try/finally) on the click handler and route creation. A closest-Volvo-by-distance default was added to the dealer picker in the same pass (mid-turn request). Distance changed from km to miles per the user's own correction. A working Close button was added to the panel (previously missing entirely). **The router turn-penalty fix (T67) was built out of this same investigation thread** — see its own entry. **A further "doesn't go through the waypoint" report led to a real router bug, found and fixed, that turned out not to be the cause of the specific visible symptom.** Investigated via `map.querySourceFeatures()` first, which showed the line passing 145m from the waypoint — a false alarm: that API returns MapLibre's zoom-simplified vector-tile geometry, not the real data; reading the GeoJSON source's own raw `_data` showed the true line passes within 37m (normal road-snap tolerance), matching a native Rust replay exactly. The actual visible problem was a tight loop/spiral near a real junction (Shandwick Place, Edinburgh), root-caused to a genuine router defect: an interior waypoint used as the goal of one leg and the start of the next was independently re-snapped for each leg (`nearest_node`'s `needs_outgoing`/`needs_incoming` flags, checked as if/else-if, never both at once), which at a one-way-heavy junction can resolve to different edges a street apart, splicing "arrive one way, leave a different way" into a loop. **Fixed in `game-wasm`**: `nearest_node` now has a real "needs both" branch (prefers a node satisfying both directions, only falling back to the single-direction heuristic if none do) and a new `Router::find_route_through(stops)` method routes a whole multi-stop journey in one call, snapping every interior stop exactly once instead of splicing independently-snapped legs in JS — `dead-mileage-preview.ts`'s `routeThrough` now calls this instead of looping `find_route` per leg. Two new Rust tests prove the mechanism directly: a synthetic one-way junction where the old independent-snap approach would pick different nodes for arriving vs. leaving, and an end-to-end check that the fixed route never revisits a coordinate. Rust 25/25. **Verified against the exact real route, honestly, not just claimed**: replaying it through the fix still showed the same loop — the waypoint already resolved to the identical node under every direction combination even before the fix, so the splice-mismatch mechanism was never actually what caused this specific case. Isolated the real cause instead: routing from that exact point to the entrance, completely alone with nothing before it, still loops — confirmed as Dijkstra's genuine shortest *legal* path given real one-way streets at that junction (a direct dealer→entrance route with no forced waypoint steers nearly 750m clear of the area entirely). **Conclusion, reported to the user as such**: the splice fix is real, tested, shipped, and will prevent this class of bug elsewhere — but this specific loop isn't a bug at all, it's the router correctly reflecting an awkward real one-way junction; recommended moving that particular waypoint rather than expecting a further code fix. Installed build rebuilt and reinstalled with all of the above (confirmed via the live exe's own bundle containing the fix). **Update (2026-10-10): the "genuine one-way junction, not a bug" conclusion above was wrong — see T69.** The user checked the real road on Google Street View and disputed it directly rather than accepting the explanation; both the Shandwick Place and a further Melville Crescent detour were actually caused by a real pipeline tag-parsing bug (`psv=designated` not recognised), not legitimate one-way geometry. Fixed and rebuilt in T69. **Confirmed by the user, live, on their own machine: "Works perfectly."** T65 — the dead mileage feature as a whole, including T55's live-reference combining variations ("The variations work correctly") — is now considered working end to end | Claude Code | Done, confirmed by the user |
| T66 | Ticketing technology (new `TICKETING.md`, supplied by the user 2026-10-09): ticket machine tiers (Basic/Mid/Advanced) per depot group with the uniformity and Advanced-ratchet rules, tap-on tap-off, install rules and timing (engineer vs. supplier, same-day switch-on), company-wide one-off upgrades (ticket app, mobile app live data, upgraded AVL), bus station info screens, and long-distance purchase rules (mobile app or travel centre staff only, 10-minute cutoff). Touches several systems not yet built: the passenger demand sim (QR/APC effects, Phase 4), finance (Phase 5), engineers and install scheduling (Phase 7), and the AVL/mobile app groundwork itself. **First increment built, see T143**: §1 (machine tiers) and §2 (tap-on tap-off), scoped to exactly what doesn't need those missing systems. §3 (install scheduling), §4 (company-wide upgrades), §5 (bus station screens) and §6 (long-distance purchase rules) still queued as their own future increments | Claude Code | §1/§2 done (T143); §3-§6 pending |
| T67 | Router turn penalty (DESIGN.md "Building a route"), found via a live dead-mileage bug report: "it is taking lots of weird turns instead of going along the main road." Confirmed directly by replaying the exact real route against the real road graph — a genuine sawtooth path cutting through residential streets near Ravelston Dykes, Edinburgh, instead of staying on the A90, because the router's time-cost model had nothing discouraging it from leaving a major road for a marginally shorter but genuinely worse shortcut. User decided: penalise every real turn, not just joining a bigger road. Fixed in `game-wasm`'s Dijkstra cost function: a flat 5s cost at any junction where the bearing changes more than 30 degrees, based on real node-to-node geometry, not OSM way id (a real road is routinely split into many separate ways with no actual turn, so a way-id check would have wrongly punished driving straight down an ordinary road). Stacks with the existing give-way delay. Two new tests: a pure unit test for the angle threshold, and an end-to-end test proving the router now prefers a straight road over a measurably shorter shortcut that needs one sharp turn. The real reported route was replayed post-fix and the sawtooth is gone. Rust: game-wasm 23/23 (2 new), pipeline 58/58, game-data 1/1 | Claude Code | Done, pending the user's own live check |
| T68 | Build hygiene: hundreds of orphaned build artefacts (198 old JS bundles, 42 old WASM files, 210MB) found sitting unused in the installed app's `resources/app/dist/assets`, discovered while investigating the T65 loop above — only one of each was actually referenced by the live `index.html`, the rest were dead weight from every earlier `npm run build` this session. Root cause, found directly in `npm run build`'s own output, not guessed: `vite.config.ts`'s `root` is `src/renderer` but `build.outDir` (`dist`) resolves *outside* that root, so Vite's own safety check silently refuses to empty it before each build (`outDir is not inside project root and will not be emptied`) — every single build this session had been adding its own content-hashed chunks on top of the last, never replacing them, and `npm run installer` had been faithfully copying that entire accumulated mess into both `release/win-unpacked` and, from there, Program Files on every reinstall. Fixed with one line, `build.emptyOutDir: true`. Verified directly: a rebuild went from 199 stale JS files down to 1; a fresh install (user ran the elevated installer themselves, UAC can't be approved from this session) brought the installed copy down from 210MB to 1.4MB, confirmed via direct inspection of the live Program Files copy. Also tried a full uninstall to force-clean the *pre-existing* clutter via `/S` silent mode — blocked by the harness's own safety check as an irreversible destructive action, correctly, since a silent uninstall of a system-level install is exactly that; left for the user rather than retried. Going forward every `npm run build` now starts from a clean `dist/`, so this shouldn't recur | Claude Code | Done |
| T69 | Real pipeline data bug, found via a live T65 dead-mileage report: user sent screenshots of a route leaving Princes Street for a large rectangular detour via George/Young/Hill/Rose Street, and a smaller detour into Melville Crescent, having independently checked both on Google Street View and confirmed buses are legally allowed down the real road the game was skipping — directly disputed two earlier "this is genuine one-way road geometry, not a bug" conclusions reached via router self-consistency checks (same path reproduced on an isolated re-query), which turned out to be the wrong kind of verification: it proves the router is deterministic, not that its input data is correct (see new memory `feedback_router_consistency_not_data_correctness`). Root-caused by dumping the real edges near the disputed location from `road_graph.bin` directly (a throwaway Rust example) and cross-checking the exact OSM way id against the real tags via the live OSM API: Princes Street/Queensferry Street are tagged `access=no` + `psv=designated` in the real data — a standard OSM convention for "general traffic banned, buses specifically intended here," stronger than `psv=yes`, not a different thing — but `rust/pipeline/src/road_graph.rs`'s tag parser only matched the literal string `"yes"` for both `psv` and `bus`, so the whole street was wrongly treated as closed to buses, sending every route near it on long back-street detours. Fixed with a 2-line change: `psv`/`bus` now also accept `"designated"`. Rebuilt the real Scotland+border road graph from the actual `.osm.pbf` extract (not a synthetic test) — backed up the old `road_graph.bin` first (`pipeline-data/.backup-before-psv-designated-fix/`). The rebuilt graph is a real, separate finding: 121MB vs the previous 922MB, a large drop that was itself verified rather than assumed safe — confirmed full coverage still holds at Portree, Glasgow and the Carlisle border strip, and that the Edinburgh depot/dealer test route still resolves correctly end to end (1250 points, non-zero). Both the dev `pipeline-data/` copy and the live installed game's own `%APPDATA%\bus-game\map-data\road_graph.bin` were updated (the latter backed up alongside it) so this is testable immediately without waiting on a new map-data release. Live-verified, screenshotted: the Princes Street detour is gone (route now runs directly along it), and the Melville Crescent detour is gone (route continues straight past it) — both on the user's own real saved route, not a synthetic one. All 58 pipeline tests still pass (no dedicated unit test added for this exact tag-parsing branch — it sits deep inside the real `osmpbf` `Way` iteration, not easily unit-testable in isolation; the real rebuild + live verification was judged the stronger proof here). Pipeline data is not currently published as a GitHub Release asset update — only the user's own local dev copy and installed copy were patched; a real map-data release republish (per CLAUDE.md's versioned-separately-from-code model) is a separate, bigger step not taken here. **Confirmed by the user, live, on their own machine: "Works perfectly."** | Claude Code | Done, confirmed by the user |

| T74 | Real router bug found live building route 57C: "The 57C has weird routing between a waypoint and Portree Dentist, it goes past the stop then back up the road." Root-caused by replaying the exact same leg (the real save's own waypoint and stop coordinates) against the real road_graph.bin outside the game, in a throwaway native example — reproduced identically on the completely original, unpatched road data, confirming it had nothing to do with any of this session's Portree bypass work. Real bug in `Router::find_route` (`game-wasm/src/lib.rs`): when a destination snaps onto a point *partway* along an edge (not at either endpoint), the old code pushed that edge's *entire* geometry — all the way to its far node — and only ever overwrote the final coordinate pair with the true snap position, leaving every point *past* the snap still in the output. The route visibly overshot the stop and jumped straight back to it. The same bug existed, mirrored, at the start of a route, and was duplicated again in `find_route_through` (used for dead mileage/multi-stop routing). **Fixed**: both functions now find exactly which segment of the matching edge's own geometry the true snap point falls on (`closest_segment_index`, a new shared helper) and truncate there instead of running the full edge length — plus a combined case for a single-edge route whose start and goal both land on the same edge. New regression test, `goal_mid_edge_does_not_overshoot_past_the_snap_point`, specifically reproduces the overshoot shape (asserts no point in the route lies past the true goal snap point). Full suite green: 26 game-wasm tests (25 + 1 new), 58 pipeline tests. WASM rebuilt (`wasm-pack build --release`), renderer rebuilt, a fresh installer built with both this and T75 together — **not yet installed by the user** | Claude Code | Fixed, tested, installer built, awaiting install |

Investigated the Add-button code path (`depot-groups-panel.ts` → `depotGroups:create` IPC → `createDepotGroup` in `db.mts`) directly — `depot_groups.name` has a real `UNIQUE NOT NULL` constraint, and neither the IPC handler nor the renderer's click handler had any error handling at all: a failed create (most plausibly a duplicate name) threw an unhandled rejection with zero visible feedback, so clicking Add just silently did nothing, exactly matching the report. **Fixed**: the Add button's handler now catches the real error and shows it inline (a specific "a depot group named X already exists" message for the known UNIQUE-constraint case, the raw error otherwise) instead of failing silently — this surfaces whatever the actual cause is on the next real attempt rather than guessing at it without live access to reproduce directly. The exact same root cause route-panel.ts already had to fix once before (its own "56px clears the bar's 42px height" comment). **Fixed**: moved the toggle to `top:56px` (and its panel to `top:88px` to keep the same relative offset), reusing the already-established value rather than re-deriving it. | T78 | Live user report (2026-10-11): route 57C's waypoint near the real (pre-existing, not the bypass's) mini-roundabout at Cearn Ròmasdail "is still weird"/"has a weird glitch." **First pass (incomplete, user called out as guessing)**: tried three different candidate waypoint coordinates in sequence, live, each checked only after the fact rather than the real topology being fully mapped first — one snapped onto the bypass's Woodpark Road connection instead of the Co-op arm, one landed close enough to the direct road that `snap_to_road` picked the wrong edge and skipped the loop entirely, the third produced a clean-looking native replay and was applied + saved via the UI — but the user deleted it, placed their own waypoint near the original spot, and reported the same glitch, correctly calling out the repeated guessing rather than root-causing ("You are guessing not actually fixing things... have you saved that anywhere like I asked"). Logged to the standing feedback memory (`feedback_no_shortcuts_follow_source_material.md`). **Second pass — two real, verified, distinct router bugs found and fixed in `game-wasm/src/lib.rs`'s `find_route`, both confirmed by replaying the user's own exact saved coordinates against the real `road_graph.bin`, not guessed:** (1) **the goal-snap gap**: when the cheapest path reaches the goal *node* via a different edge than the one the goal's own coordinate actually snapped onto (common at a cluttered mini-roundabout junction with several very short edges), the precise snap point was never appended at all — the leg silently stopped short at the node, and the next leg started fresh from the true point a few metres away, which is what rendered as the zigzag. Fixed by appending the true snap point (plus any real intervening geometry) whenever the arrival node is one of the matched edge's own two endpoints *and* the edge's oneway flag actually permits travelling from there towards the snap point — the existing regression test `goal_snap_point_not_used_when_its_edge_was_never_actually_reached` guards the illegal-direction case this must still refuse, and it does. A second guard was needed after this patch broke `dijkstra_prefers_the_faster_edge_over_the_shorter_one`: skip the patch entirely when the goal point exactly coincides with the arrival node's own position (a tie-break case where `nearest_node` can match a different parallel edge than the one Dijkstra actually used, even though no real gap exists). (2) **edge-chunk undercounting**: `last_route_edge_point_counts()` (what `route-draw.ts`'s `appendLeg` uses to slice the flat coordinate array back into one chunk per edge, for the chevron layer) is computed *before* the coordinate-building loop runs and was never updated to account for the extra points fix (1) adds — verified directly against the real data: a real leg decoded 47 points but the chunk counts only summed to 42, silently dropping the last 5 points from every edge chunk (present in the plain merged line, invisible to anything keyed off chunks) without erroring. Fixed by pushing a synthetic final chunk entry (edge id + point count) whenever fix (1)'s patch actually adds points; re-verified the same leg now sums to exactly 46 (= decoded points − 1, matching every other leg). Both fixes are specific to `find_route`, which is what `route-draw.ts` actually calls leg-by-leg — `find_route_through` (dead mileage routes) has the same `is_goal_step` shape and was **not** patched, since it's not what's rendering this report; worth checking if a similar symptom ever surfaces there. Full suite green throughout (26 game-wasm including the new tie-break case, 58 pipeline). WASM rebuilt, renderer rebuilt, new installer built — **awaiting the user's live confirmation this time actually holds** | Claude Code | Two real router bugs fixed and verified against real data; installer built, awaiting confirmation |

Root cause: the waypoint sits on a one-way edge (34580186→413289645, Forward) that can legally be *arrived at* right up to that precise point, but **cannot legally be departed from** at that same point without walking backward against the one-way flag — a real, physically-genuine asymmetry, not a bug in either of T78's two `find_route` patches (confirmed by writing and testing a direct start-side mirror of the goal-side patch, which correctly refused to fire here, exactly as it should given the one-way constraint). The actual architectural cause: `route-draw.ts`'s `rebuildRoute()` called `find_route` once per leg, independently re-resolving the *same* interior point as a goal for one leg and a start for the next — exactly the failure mode `find_route_through`'s own doc comment already describes ("two legs' endpoints independently snapped... land a street apart"), which that function exists specifically to prevent by resolving a shared interior point once. **Fixed properly, not patched around**: extended `find_route_through` (previously untouched, had no per-edge chunk tracking at all) with the same precision-extension patches as `find_route`, but gated to fire **only on the route's own true outer start/goal** (`i==0`/`i==n-2`) — applying them to interior points was tried first and correctly rejected by the existing `find_route_through_does_not_double_back_at_an_asymmetric_interior_waypoint` test, since extending past a shared interior node defeats the whole reason that node was chosen (it can reintroduce exactly the doubling-back bug the function exists to prevent). Also found and fixed a second latent gap while building this: the per-edge chunk-count tracking undercounted by not recording a leg's own first pushed point in the common case, corrupting the duplicate-point-drain's own count bookkeeping — switched to deriving each chunk's point count from the real, measured change in the coordinate array around each push rather than predicting it, which is self-correcting against the later drain. Verified against the real, full 5-point 57C route: the interior waypoint now has zero gap on either side, and per-edge chunk counts sum exactly to the real point count (154 of 154). **`route-draw.ts`'s `rebuildRoute()` switched to call `find_route_through` once for the whole point sequence** instead of looping `appendLeg` per leg — this is what's actually rendered whenever a route is opened for editing or rebuilt after an insert/remove (confirmed `startEditing` and every other `rebuildRoute()` call site). The incremental single-click-to-extend fast path (`addPoint`'s direct `appendLeg` call while actively drawing a brand new point) is **not yet migrated** — same latent bug class, lower urgency since it only affects a point being drawn for the first time, not reopening a saved route; `route-panel.ts`'s "Show" view and `vehicle-simulation.ts` also still use the old per-leg approach and would hit the identical interior-point gap if a route's actual driven path or the non-editor map view is ever reported broken the same way. Full suite green throughout (26 game-wasm, including catching and fixing a real regression against the existing double-back test before shipping; 58 pipeline). Traced to a real bug in `Router::find_route` (`game-wasm/src/lib.rs`, present since the goal/start precision patches added earlier this session, T78/T81): `last_edge_point_counts` was precomputed from every step's **full, untruncated** `geometry_len + 1` *before* the coordinate-building loop ran, but the loop itself truncates the start-step and goal-step to a **partial** slice of their edge's geometry whenever the snap point sits part-way along it (the precision fix from T78/T81) — the precomputed count was never adjusted down to match. A caller slicing the flat coordinate array by these counts (`route-panel.ts`'s `routeLineAndEdges`, building the saved-route chevron source) walks its running index **past the real array end** for every edge after the first truncated one, producing empty `.slice()` results — silently dropped by the chevron layer's own `coords.length >= 2` filter, with no error anywhere. This is exactly why it looked like "missing arrows" with everything else (filter, sprite, layer style) checking out clean. **Fixed** in `find_route`: removed the premature static precompute entirely and replaced it with the same delta-based tracking (`before = coords.len()` / `added = (coords.len() - before) / 2` around every push) that `find_route_through` already used correctly for the identical reason (T83). New regression test `find_route_edge_point_counts_never_overcount_a_truncated_start_or_goal_step`, reusing the existing `goal_mid_edge_does_not_overshoot_past_the_snap_point` graph (goal snaps 90% along an edge, the real truncation case), asserting `sum(counts) == decoded_points - 1`. Full suite green (28 game-wasm incl. the new test, 58 pipeline, 85 total). **Verified against the real route three ways**: (1) native replay of all 4 real legs — every leg's count-sum now matches exactly (was over by 1–6 points on 3 of 4 legs, now 0 on all 4); (2) live, via CDP against the freshly packaged app — opened 57C's "Show" view, queried the live `saved-route-preview-edges` source directly: all 52 rendered chevron features have `coordCount >= 2` (previously some would have silently vanished from this array entirely); (3) screenshot of the live map at the roundabout and the final waypoint→Dentist stretch shows continuous, unbroken chevrons the whole way, no gaps.
