DESIGN.md §11 updated to drop "with small chevrons indicating one-directional running" and record the 2026-10-17 decision and why. The Rust-side `last_route_edges()`/`last_route_edge_point_counts()` getters and their regression tests (T78/T81/T82/T83/T87) were deliberately left in place, unused by any TS caller now — general-purpose, already correct, removing them would only lose test coverage for a real bug class with no present benefit. **(2) "Cutting the corner before the roundabout" — investigated, found to be a false alarm, not a bug.** The screenshot showing a visible cut corner was 57C (blue) and 56 (purple) both shown simultaneously, diverging by a few metres near the Woodpark Road junction — confirmed directly by hiding 57C and re-screenshotting with only 56 shown: the ring renders as a clean, unbroken circle. 57C alone (checked separately, same zoom) is equally clean. Neither route has a real geometry defect here; the two routes simply don't resolve the junction identically, which only becomes visible when both are displayed over each other at once — not a realistic gameplay scenario, and not something fixed (there is nothing to fix: both routes are individually correct). **(3) Portree High School roads — real road data confirmed present and correctly positioned; found and fixed a genuine, separate contrast bug instead.** With real coordinates in hand, fetched the actual OSM ways within 150m directly from the OSM API (overpass was down, used `/api/0.6/map` instead) — found a real `highway=unclassified` way (1165433603) tagged `access=private` but explicitly `bus=yes` (the school's own bus turning loop), confirmed our router's existing access logic (`!access_restricted || psv_yes || bus_yes`, game-wasm/src/lib.rs:186) already handles this combination correctly — not a T69-style tag-parsing bug. Fetched this way's real node coordinates from the OSM API directly and overlaid them as markers on the live rendered map: they land exactly on the rendered white loop line, proving the road geometry is present and correctly positioned, not missing. The real cause: `landuse-education`'s fill colour (`#ffffe5`) was the single palest landuse class in the whole style — within 26 RGB units of pure white — rendered with **no casing at all** on `roads-service`/`roads-pedestrian-street`/`roads-minor` (pure `#ffffff`, only bridges get a casing layer), so a road crossing school grounds had almost no contrast against the fill beneath it. Confirmed every other landuse class is meaningfully darker (residential `#e0dfdf`, commercial `#f2dad9`, cemetery `#aacbaf`, etc.) — education was the outlier. Fixed by darkening `landuse-education` to `#f2ecb0` (`basemap-style.json`) — still a clearly "pale yellow/cream" school-grounds convention, but with real contrast against white roads now. This is a general fix (every school in the whole UK build benefits, not just Portree's), deliberately chosen over adding a general road casing (a much bigger, broader-impact style change) since the contrast gap was specifically and uniquely about this one landuse class, confirmed by checking all the others. Verified live via screenshot: the driveway loop is now clearly visible against a honey-khaki fill. No Rust changes this round (full workspace re-run clean, 85/85); `tsc --noEmit` clean; renderer rebuilt; new installer built and boot-tested clean via CDP | Claude Code | All three addressed: (1) chevrons removed per explicit user decision, 75% opacity everywhere, verified live; (2) investigated and found to be a false alarm (two routes overlaid), nothing to fix; (3) real contrast bug found and fixed, verified live with before/after screenshots — awaiting the user's own confirmation on all three |
| T91 | Direct continuation of T90 item (2): the user came back with "the roundabout is still broken" after T90 wrongly concluded it was a false alarm (two routes shown at once). Corrected: "Not on the 56, only the 57C" — a real, single-route defect, not an overlay artifact. User sent a screenshot with the bad stretch circled: the ring's own edge between the top of the curve and the Woodpark Road junction renders as a visible flat chord, not following the real curve. **Root-caused precisely, verified against the real deployed `road_graph.bin`**: the ring edge in question (edge 28227, 34580186→green-splice-node, the same edge T85 fixed the splice position on) genuinely has all 6 real intermediate curve points correctly stored (confirmed by decoding the live graph directly) — the data itself is NOT missing anything, ruling out a T80/T85-style pipeline truncation. The actual cause is in `find_route_through`'s own interior-waypoint handling: 57C's waypoint (forced mid-curve, ~27% along the segment between real nodes 413289643 and 34580189, confirmed by direct coordinate math) is correctly truncated when *arrived at* (the leg ending at the waypoint stops exactly at its own precise coordinate, confirmed in the decoded path) — but when the *next* leg *departs* from that same waypoint to continue around the ring, debug instrumentation added directly to `find_route_through` (temporary `eprintln!`s, not yet reverted) shows the `is_start_step` precision-truncation branch never fires for this leg at all, meaning `start_edge_matches` evaluates false even though both legs are using the same real edge — so the interior "plain node position only" branch fires instead, and the departure jumps straight to the next real graph node, skipping the real curve points between the waypoint and that node entirely. This is a new, distinct bug from everything T78–T87 already fixed — an asymmetry between how an interior point's *arrival* truncation and *departure* resumption handle the exact same shared coordinate. **Fixed.** All debug `eprintln!`s removed. The interior-point "else" branch (departure from a point whose own first real Dijkstra step doesn't match its own snap edge) now mirrors `find_route`'s existing goal-side gap patch instead of unconditionally using the plain node: it pushes the real snap coordinate first, then — only when that coordinate actually matches what the *previous* leg's own last pushed point already was (checked directly against `coords`, not assumed) **and** the oneway direction legally permits walking forward along the remaining stretch of the same edge — includes the real curve geometry between the snap point and the node before finally reaching it. The "only when it matches the previous leg's own last point" guard is the load-bearing part: a first attempt without it broke the existing `find_route_through_does_not_double_back_at_an_asymmetric_interior_waypoint` regression test immediately, because in that test's own scenario (an interior point's snap edge is a dead-end spur with no legal way out, so the real path arrives via a completely different edge) the previous leg never actually stopped at the precise point at all — inserting it unconditionally created a genuine, novel detour onto ground the route never reached, not a resumption of anything. New regression test `find_route_through_resumes_the_real_curve_after_an_interior_waypoint_mid_edge` (a synthetic edge with two real intermediate geometry points, an interior waypoint forced to land between them) proves both real curve points survive — the one before the waypoint (already correct, via the existing goal-side truncation) and the one after it (the actual fix). Full suite green throughout: 29 game-wasm (28 existing + 1 new), 88 full workspace. **Verified three ways**: (1) native replay of the real 57C route — both previously-missing real curve points (34580189, 34580190) now present, chunk-count accounting still exact, the already-confirmed-intentional out-and-back retrace pattern (T91b) untouched; (2) WASM and renderer rebuilt, new installer built, boot-tested clean via CDP; (3) live screenshot of the actual ring at tight zoom shows an even decagon — the segment that previously rendered as a visibly longer, flatter chord near the Woodpark Road junction now matches every other segment around the curve | Claude Code | Real router bug found, fixed, and verified three ways (native data, native regression test, live screenshot) — awaiting the user's own confirmation |
| T91b | Mid-investigation on T91, asked whether removing 57C's waypoint entirely (since a direct Coop→Dentist route, computed and verified natively, is 121 points, avoids the roundabout area completely, and has no retrace or sharp turns) would be the cleaner fix. **User corrected this directly**: "It needs the waypoint because thats where the route goes, it can't be removed" — the waypoint reflects where the real bus route actually goes (the roundabout loop is a real, intentional part of this route, not a router-bug workaround to delete). Settled: the waypoint stays; T91's actual router bug (departure-side truncation) is the thing that needs fixing, not the route's own design | Claude Code | Settled by the user — waypoint is real route design, not a workaround; T91's router bug is the only thing left to fix |
| T91c | Follow-up question from the same thread: does the game already auto-route via a roundabout/junction instead of letting a path imply an illegal U-turn, per DESIGN.md's "Turning round" section? **Checked directly, both the spec text and the router code**: DESIGN.md's own wording puts this on the player ("the route must be drawn to bring it back facing the right way"), and the router's cost function treats a 180° reversal as just another sharp turn (`turn_penalty_s`'s own test: "a 180 degree U-turn costs the flat penalty") — a cost, not a prohibition, so Dijkstra can still choose a path implying a literal U-turn if nothing else is cheaper. **Not implemented.** The user confirmed this should be a documented requirement regardless ("it should be in the specs as I have asked for it in the past") — added to DESIGN.md's "Turning round" section: the router should automatically detour via the nearest real turning point (roundabout/junction/turning circle) rather than finding a path that implies a U-turn, explicitly marked not-yet-implemented, with the manual-waypoint workaround noted as the current state until it's built. Not scoped or started beyond the spec addition — likely significant work (needs "can a bus physically turn around here" reasoning per node, not just the existing degree/width checks) | Claude Code | Spec requirement recorded in DESIGN.md, explicitly marked not-yet-implemented; no code started, not scoped into a phase yet |
| T92 | Live user request (2026-10-17), after confirming T91's roundabout fix works: "Can you also have it so each new route get a unique colour please." Every new route previously defaulted to the same fixed blue (`#3b82f6`) — confirmed live, the real routes list had accumulated many routes all sharing that one swatch. **Built**: a new colour picker (`nextRouteColour`, route-draw.ts) assigns each genuinely new route the next colour around the hue wheel at the golden angle (137.508°), indexed by how many routes already exist (`window.routes.list().length`) — the standard technique for a colour sequence that stays well-separated for a very long run, with no need to check existing routes' own colours for a collision at all. Wired into `clear()` as an async upgrade straight after its own synchronous default-colour reset (so clearing never has a visible colourless moment while the real count resolves), and `startDrawing()` extended to call `clear()` not just when resuming a stale edit/variation but also whenever `draftPoints` is already empty — covering the very first "+ New route" click after boot, which the original guard would have missed entirely (no stale state to clear, so it would have silently kept the static default). An in-progress, unsaved draft is left alone either way, unaffected — re-entering drawing mode still picks up where it left off, unchanged from before. `startEditing`/`startVariation` were already unaffected (they never call `clear()`, setting `colourInput.value` from the route's own saved colour directly). Verified live: with 10 real existing routes in the save, a fresh "+ New route" click produced `#c52dd2`, exactly matching the predicted 11th colour in the sequence computed independently. `tsc --noEmit` clean, renderer rebuilt, new installer built and boot-tested clean via CDP | Claude Code | Built and verified live against the real route count — awaiting the user's own confirmation |
| T94 | Duties (OPERATIONS.md §7, Phase 3) — vehicle work-pattern scheduling, built after "start on the next thing" (2026-10-17). Scoped first via extensive clarification with the user rather than guessed: deliberately **vehicle-only for this increment** — no driver assignment, no duty-length/hours validation (the user's own words: "Drivers will be swaping vehicles during the day so are slightly seperate from what we are doing now"), both explicitly deferred to Phase 6 once drivers and GB/EU hours rules actually exist. Manual chaining and lowest-cost auto-complete built together, not staged. Scope is per depot group, per day-type (Mon-Fri/Sat/Sun), matching the timetable grid's own split; cost for auto-complete is total dead mileage, not vehicle count; UI is a new full-screen view opened per depot group, like the timetable grid. **Journey data foundation**, previously missing entirely (departures were only ever computed fresh per-tick inside vehicle-simulation.ts, never persisted as discrete entities) — new `duty-journeys.mts`, pure and DOM/WASM-free, reuses the *exact* `generateDepartureMinutesForBands` → `applyExcludedDepartures` → `mergeCustomDepartures` pipeline route-timetable-grid.mts already uses, so a duty's journey list always matches the published timetable exactly; 16 unit assertions, all passing. **Data layer**: schema v30→v31, new `duties`/`duty_pieces` tables — a piece stores only `(route_id, departure_minutes)`, re-finding the real journey fresh each time rather than copying its timing/geometry, the same "merged view is a projection, not a copy" principle the timetable grid itself follows. Full CRUD (create/delete/rename/lock/add-piece/remove-piece) in `electron/db.mts`, wired through `electron/main.ts` IPC handlers and `electron/preload.ts`'s bridge. **UI** (`src/renderer/duty-panel.ts`): a duties column (card per duty: number, lock toggle, delete, its pieces each with a remove button and the real dead-run distance shown between consecutive pieces) alongside a journeys column (every unassigned journey for the active day type, clickable to append to whichever duty is selected, greyed out when infeasible — wrong order or no legal/timely road route between the two points, checked live via `router.find_route`). Dead running is computed live via the router and never persisted, the same convention the dead-mileage preview already uses. **Auto-complete**: greedy, processes every unassigned journey in time order, extending whichever open (unlocked) duty can legally reach it for the least dead mileage, starting a new duty only when nothing existing fits — a same-route return journey sharing its outbound's exact arrival point wins outright (zero cost), which gives OPERATIONS.md's "return journeys are always chained automatically" as an emergent property of the cost minimisation rather than a separate special-cased pass. **One real bug found and fixed during live verification**: the overlay's background was set to `var(--bg)`, a CSS variable that doesn't exist in this project's theme (confirmed via grep — only `--bg-page`/`--bg-surface-0/1/2`/`--bg-accent`/`--bg-warning` are real), so the overlay was effectively transparent — correctly stacked on top (z-index 10) but visually letting everything behind it show through. Fixed to `var(--bg-page)`, rebuilt, repackaged, reverified. **Verified live end-to-end via CDP against real data, not just claimed**: opened for Skye (confirmed correctly empty — `window.routeTimetables.listAll()` shows zero timetable rows for any Skye route, a genuine data gap, not a bug) and for Glasgow (452 real journeys across routes 398/398A/467/888, rendering correctly with real route colours and clock times). Manual chaining: created a duty, clicked two journeys onto it, confirmed both pieces appear with a correctly-computed `dead run: 0.0km` between them (a same-route consecutive departure sharing its start point). Lock: toggling correctly dropped every journey's addability to 0 while locked and restored it (449 addable) on unlock — confirmed functionally via the downstream addable-count change, since the lock button's own DOM node is replaced on every re-render (a test-script limitation, not an app bug). Piece removal and duty deletion both confirmed (2 pieces → 1 → duty gone, column empty). Auto-complete run against the full 452-journey Glasgow set: finished in 7.7s, created 6 duties, 0 journeys left unassigned, 515.8km total dead mileage across 446 dead-run segments, piece counts per duty `[96,103,107,67,57,22]` — correctly varying by route frequency (a tight-headway shuttle like 398 chains far more journeys per duty than a sparser route), confirming the greedy algorithm produces sensible, non-degenerate results on real data. **Noted, not fixed, out of scope**: route 900 (routeId 27) has three duplicate `monday_friday` timetable rows in the live save with different band configurations — a pre-existing data oddity unrelated to this feature; `journeysForDepotGroup`'s `.find()`-based lookup handles it gracefully (silently picks the first match) rather than crashing, left alone. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `duty-journeys.verify.mts` 16/16 passing, installer already rebuilt and boot-tested clean via CDP as part of the z-index fix above.

**Real incident, same day: live CDP testing against the real save file broke the installed game.** The CDP testing above ran against `release/win-unpacked` (already rebuilt to schema v31 for this feature) while the installed Program Files copy was still on v30 — both point at the same shared save file (`%APPDATA%\bus-game\save.sqlite`), so testing silently upgraded the user's real save, and the older installed app then refused to open it at all (an unhandled promise rejection in the main process — "Save file schema version 31 is not supported (expected 30)" — with no window ever created and no visible error, the exact `UnhandledPromiseRejectionWarning` failure class already on file elsewhere in this project's own memory). Diagnosed by checking running processes directly (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`): the installed app's main process was spawning a GPU and network process but never a renderer, proving it crashed before `BrowserWindow` creation; relaunching from a terminal to capture stderr (rather than the swallowed-by-double-click GUI launch) surfaced the real error immediately. **Fixed**: rebuilt a fresh installer from current code (`npm run installer`, a separate target from `npm run package`'s own `--dir`-only build — the two had silently drifted apart, confirmed by comparing each build's own compiled `SCHEMA_VERSION` constant directly) and launched it for the user to approve via the real UAC prompt (not something this environment can click through itself). The 6 test duties (452 pieces) the CDP auto-complete run had created in the real save were deleted directly via `node:sqlite` once the save was reachable again. **Process fix for next time**: all further live CDP testing in this same session switched to an isolated `--user-data-dir` pointing at a scratch copy of `save.sqlite` (so nothing real can be touched) plus a Windows directory **junction** onto the real `map-data` folder (6.8GB — too large to copy per test run, but read-only, so junctioning instead of copying is safe) rather than ever pointing a dev build at the real shared userData directory again.

**Update (2026-10-17), after the user's own first real test pass confirmed the feature working end-to-end ("Checked, all working") — three follow-up requests, all built and live-verified:**
1. **Start/end stop names on every journey.** `Journey` (`duty-journeys.mts`) gained `startStopOsmId`/`endStopOsmId` (null for a bare waypoint terminus) — kept as plain osmIds, not resolved names, so the module stays pure/DOM-free for its own unit tests; `duty-panel.ts` resolves the real name at render time via `stopsPanelState.displayNameFor`, the same lookup route-panel.ts's own `stopLabel` already uses. 18/18 `duty-journeys.verify.mts` assertions passing (2 new, covering both a real stop and a waypoint terminus).
2. **Duties tied to a specific depot, grouped by depot, numbered by depot rank.** A real scope correction from this file's own original "doesn't need to start/end at a depot" note: the user's own words — "Duties need to be tied to a certain depot, this is where drivers will start/end the day so will always need to start/end the dutie there." `duties` gained a required `depot_id` (schema v31→v32; DROP+recreate, same as the table's own original creation the same day — no real duty data existed on the depot-less shape to migrate). `duty_number` is now generated, not freely typed: 1xxx for a group's main depot (lowest id, i.e. first ever placed), 2xxx for the next, and so on (`nextDutyNumber`/`depotRankInGroup`, db.mts) — taken from the current highest number in that depot's own block, not a separate persisted counter, so deleting the current top duty frees its exact number for reuse but a gap left by deleting a lower one does not get backfilled (acceptable: the number is cosmetic and the player can always `renameDuty` it anyway). `listDutiesForDepotGroup` now orders by the numeric `duty_number` itself, which already groups by depot with no separate sort needed. 11 new `electron/db.verify.mts` assertions (depot-ranked numbering, reuse-on-delete-of-max, manual rename override, full CRUD), plus a migration check against a fresh copy of the real save confirming it lands on v32 and that a duty created on the migrated real data carries the right `depotId`.
3. **Layout rework**: services moved to the left column (now showing real stop names per item 1), duties moved to a CSS grid on the right, grouped under a section header per depot (the grid's own natural sort order already matches, from item 2). Each duty box is compact by default — number, lock, delete, and a coloured badge per unique route number it covers (looked up from `routes`, not `journeys`, so a badge survives even if that exact departure is later excluded from the timetable). Clicking a box expands it in place (the user's own preferred option from the clarifying question) to show the full ordered piece list, rename field, and — new — a **pull-out** row (real dead run from the duty's own depot to its first piece) and a **pull-in** row (last piece back to the depot), using the same `deadRun` helper as the existing inter-piece dead-run rows. Only one box is ever expanded at a time (reusing the existing single `activeDutyId`, which also still doubles as the add-journey target — clicking a different box moves both).
4. **Depot picker** in the header ("New duties at: ...", a themed dropdown, same component `depot-groups-panel.ts` already uses for region) — chooses which of the group's depots a new duty is created against; defaults to the first depot in rank order, rebuilt from the real depot list every refresh.
5. **Auto-complete made depot-aware**: for a brand-new duty the cost is now the real pull-out dead run from a candidate depot (not assumed zero as before), and when nothing open fits, a new duty is started at whichever depot in the *whole group* gives the cheapest pull-out to that journey — not always whatever the header dropdown currently has selected. A tie between reusing an already-empty duty and creating a fresh one at the same depot and same cost correctly favours reuse (strict `<` comparison, existing duties checked first), so this never spawns a redundant empty duty.
6. **A real, measured performance regression, found and fixed before shipping, not after.** Item 5's extra per-depot check, naively computing a fresh `router.find_route()` call for every (journey, depot) pair, took **280.2s** for 142 unassigned journeys against 2 depots (down from 7.7s for 452 journeys under the pre-depot-aware algorithm) — because many journeys on the same route share an identical start point (every 398 departs the same stop, every 900 the same stance), so the same road query was being asked for, and recomputed from scratch, dozens of times over. Fixed with an in-run `Map`-based memoisation (`cachedDeadRun`/`cachedCanFollow`, keyed on the two points' own coordinates to 6dp) wrapping every dead-run lookup inside the auto-complete handler specifically — the single manual add-one-journey path is untouched, since it's low-frequency enough not to need it. Re-measured on an equivalent fresh run (142 journeys, 2 depots, 0 pre-existing duties): **2.9s**, same correct result (0 journeys left unassigned, sensible depot-grouped duty counts).
All of this verified live via CDP against an isolated copy of the real save (not the shared one, per the incident above): real multi-depot Edinburgh data (two depots, "Portree" and "Ed") produced duty numbers 1001/1002 then 2001/2002/2003 exactly as specified, with correct section headers, correct badge de-duplication on collapse, and correct pull-out/pull-in distances (one real example: 75.3km pull-out, matching the real Edinburgh-Glasgow road distance for this depot group's own cross-city test route data). `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout; the isolated test instance and its scratch userData directory (including the map-data junction) were torn down afterward, and all throwaway `cdp_*.tmp.*` scripts removed | Claude Code | Built and verified live end-to-end, including the depot-anchored rework and a real perf fix.

**Update (2026-10-18): a run of live UI polish requests, several wrong on the first (and second, and third) attempt before landing right — kept here in full since the dead ends are as instructive as the fix.** After the user's own "Checked, all three fixes work" on item 3 above: (1) start/end stop names shown per journey everywhere (already covered above); (2) the expanded duty box should be "the same width as 3 duties plus the two gaps in between," not the full row; (3) a colour swatch per piece inside an expanded duty, matching the journeys list; (4) badge/chip text colour switches between black and white by the route colour's own real luminance (`readableTextColour`, the standard relative-luminance formula) — a live complaint that routes 467 (`#00ff2a`, bright green) and 888 (`#fcd6a4`, pale peach) were unreadable in fixed white text, confirmed directly against the real save's own colour values before fixing. Items 2–4 shipped clean first time. **Then real trouble, three failed layout attempts in a row**: the user reported "a massive gap next to the open duty." Attempt 1 (`grid-auto-flow: dense`) fixed a real horizontal packing gap but left a *vertical* one — a CSS grid row is always exactly as tall as its tallest item, and a shorter sibling's own dead space beneath it can never be backfilled by anything, which no reordering of placement (dense or otherwise) changes. Attempt 2 made the expanded duty own its entire grid row in isolation (`grid-column: 1 / -1` plus a measured explicit width) — this genuinely removed the vertical dead space, but traded it for a new *horizontal* one, since nothing else could ever share that now-exclusive row. At this point, stopped guessing a third time and asked directly whether a real masonry/packed reflow (duty order no longer strictly left-to-right, but zero gaps anywhere) was an acceptable trade-off — user: "Yes, pack it properly." **Built a genuine JS masonry layout** (`renderDuties`, two-pass: build everything in plain flow first since a fresh element's real height can't be known before layout; then absolutely position each one via the standard greedy per-column-height-tracking algorithm, a span-1 card going to whichever single column is currently shortest and a span-3 expanded card going wherever the worst of its 3 columns is shortest) — verified live against the real, by-now 268-duty Glasgow test data: zero overlapping card pairs, confirmed both programmatically and visually. Two more real, measured bugs followed, both caught and fixed before shipping rather than after: **(a)** setting the container's own explicit height to match its content exactly defeated its own `overflow-y: auto` (nothing left to overflow *within a box already sized to fit everything*) — user: "I can't scroll down." Fixed with a 1px absolutely-positioned "sizer" at the true content bottom instead, leaving the container at its normal flex-driven (viewport-bounded) height so real overflow exists to scroll. **(b)** the expanded duty's own bottom edge landing wherever its real content ended could leave its own column's next item starting lower than its neighbours' — user, after one clarifying round-trip confirming the read: "always extended, never shortened." Rounds the expanded card's occupied height up (never down) to the next whole multiple of a real measured collapsed-card row height. First version of this fix only used the rounded figure for the column-height *bookkeeping*, never applied it to the card's own rendered `style.height` — so the box still visually stopped at its shorter natural height, same dead space as ever, just now reserved further down instead of visible as a grid gap; user: "there shouldnt be a gap at the bottom, the box should extend to fill that gap." Fixed by actually setting `el.style.height` to the rounded figure. **(c)**, caught in the same round: cards were rendering out past the real scrollbar's own track — `getBoundingClientRect().width` is the element's border-box width, which does *not* shrink when a vertical scrollbar appears (the scrollbar eats into the content box, not the border box), so the masonry's own column math was sized against a width that didn't actually exist once scrolling kicked in. Switched to `clientWidth` (which does shrink correctly) — but `clientWidth` is measured right after clearing the previous render's own children, i.e. with nothing in the box yet to need a scrollbar, making the very same measurement wrong on every single render, not just the first; fixed properly with `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on the container, which reserves the scrollbar's own width unconditionally so the measurement is always correct regardless of what's currently rendered. **All three (scroll, row-snap height, scrollbar width) verified live via CDP against the real 268-duty Glasgow data before being shipped**, not just asserted: scrollHeight (4680px) genuinely exceeds clientHeight (767px) with `scrollTop` actually moving; the expanded card's own `getBoundingClientRect().height` exactly matches its `style.height`; every card's own right edge lands exactly at the scrollbar-excluded boundary, not past it. **Confirmed by the user on their own real install: "Both working."** Throughout this whole round, every change was additionally boot-tested first against an *isolated* `--user-data-dir` (a scratch copy of `save.sqlite` plus a Windows directory junction onto the real, 6.8GB `map-data` folder — too large to copy per test run, but read-only, so junctioning is safe) rather than ever pointed at the real shared userData directory again, specifically to not repeat the T94-incident mistake recorded above | Claude Code | Confirmed working by the user, including a genuine masonry reflow and three further real bugs found and fixed before shipping |
| T95 | Real-world policy update supplied by the user (2026-10-18): "with the £2 fare cap, that has been extended to Aberdeen and Aberdeenshire so can that be done in the game please, same rules as all the others." The £2 fare cap (DESIGN.md §9) isn't built yet (fares are Phase 4, not yet reached), so this is a spec update only, for whenever that phase is built — matching the project's own established practice of recording real-world data in advance rather than losing it (see T64's own livery-data precedent). DESIGN.md §9's "£2 fare cap" section extended from two zones to three: Highland, SPT, and the new **Aberdeen** zone (Aberdeen City and Aberdeenshire, full council areas — a single combined zone, confirmed directly with the user after an initial pass: "Aberdeen and Aberdeenshire count as one zone not 2, going from Aberdeen to Aberdeenshire will be £2 not £4"). Crossing *between* any two of the three zones is £4, same rule regardless of which two (no three-zone "through fare" case, since Highland geographically sits between the other two). Explicitly recorded, per a further user clarification: a journey leaving a capped zone, crossing real *uncapped* territory, and later entering a different capped zone is **not** a £4 crossing — each capped portion gets its own £2 cap and the uncapped middle is charged normally, "exactly as if it were two separate journeys" (the user's own words: "if traveling through an area not in a fare cap then the regular fare will apply in the bit not covered by fare caps" — this was already implicit in the existing "leaving the zone, normal fare applies" sentence, but made fully explicit since the new three-zone wording could otherwise read ambiguously). | T96 | Fleet numbers and plates (OPERATIONS.md §3, UK-EXPANSION.md §14) — picked as the next Phase 3 gap (2026-10-18), then scoped down twice via live clarification before building: fleet numbers/plates are only ever assigned "once bought," but there was no owned-vehicle concept anywhere in the save at all, only the static catalogue — so this also had to build the minimal purchase flow underneath it (the user's own choice, after confirming the other Phase 3 options — basket buying, vehicle condition/delivery — would hit the exact same missing-ownership blocker). **Deliberately minimal**: new vehicles only, buy-outright only, no basket/bulk discount, no leasing, no condition decay over time, no delivery lead time — a vehicle is bought and appears at its depot immediately, with condition starting at 100 (stored, not yet simulated changing). **A real stale-catalogue bug found and fixed on the way**: `vehicle-catalogue.mts` still had the ADL Enviro500 in it, even though UK-EXPANSION.md §14a already recorded it as replaced by the ADL Enviro400 XLB (13.4m, 100 seated/25 standing/125 total, £270,000) — fixed by swapping the entry for the real XLB spec and removing the now-obsolete "Airlink 100 requires the 13m Volvo 9700DD" note (Airlink 100's own requirement moved to the XLB per that same section). **Fleet numbering** (`fleet-numbering.mts`, pure/DOM-free): UK-EXPANSION.md §14's finalised scheme, `[category digit][3+ digit sequential][e/h suffix]` — every one of the catalogue's 22 real models mapped explicitly to its category digit by id (not inferred from the `category` string, which doesn't even say "double-decker" for the open-top Enviro400s), with the digit itself derived from broad vehicle class plus length (10.8m single-deck midibus/full-size cutoff, 12.9m twin/tri-axle cutoff, reusing the catalogue's own existing `axleCountForLength`). Sequential numbering is MAX-based per category digit, same convention as duty numbering (T94) — deleting the current top vehicle in a category frees its exact number, a gap left by deleting a lower one does not. **Registration plates**: real current-style GB format (2-letter DVLA area tag + 2-digit age identifier + 3 random letters), verified against the real DVLA table (gov.uk's own INF104, cross-checked against a reproduction of it) rather than guessed, since the map now spans the whole UK and OPERATIONS.md's own plate section only ever named 5 Scottish cities — confirmed with the user to extend this UK-wide with real area codes rather than a placeholder or a short fixed list. One real bug caught by its own unit test: the age-identifier's September-February rule used the date's own calendar year instead of the year the period actually started, so a January 2027 purchase computed 77 instead of the correct 76 (the same period as the preceding September) — fixed and re-verified. Northern Ireland (never adopted the 2001 GB scheme) gets a distinct, simplified NI-style plate instead of a nonsense GB-format one. **Data layer**: schema v32→v33, new `vehicles` table — `category_digit`/`sequential_number` kept as their own columns rather than parsed back out of the formatted `fleet_number` string, so numbering stays exact however many digits the sequential part grows to. `buyVehicle` (db.mts) computes the category digit, next sequential number, formatted fleet number and a real plate (using the depot's own coordinates as "the area it was bought in," since there's no dealer-selection step yet) in one atomic call. **UI** (`vehicle-catalogue-panel.ts`, extending the existing read-only browser): a "Your fleet" list at the top showing every owned vehicle with its fleet number, plate, model and depot, with delete; a "Buy" section in the catalogue detail view with a length-variant dropdown (where a model has more than one), a depot-group/depot cascading picker, and a Buy button — disabled with an explanatory note for donor-priced vehicles (open-top conversions) that can't be bought directly yet, and for a depot group with no depot placed. **Verified live via CDP against an isolated copy of the real save** (the established workaround from T94's own incident, never pointed at the shared save again): bought an ADL Enviro400 MMC at the real Edinburgh depot, got fleet number 4001 and plate "SK76 PKD" (SK — the real Edinburgh DVLA area — correctly matching that depot's own location), shown correctly in "Your fleet"; delete confirmed removing it both from the list and the count. 31 new unit assertions across `fleet-numbering.verify.mts` and `db.verify.mts` (including a real-save migration + purchase check), all passing; every other verify script across the project re-run clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean | Claude Code | Built and verified live end-to-end (buy, fleet number, real plate, delete) — awaiting the user's own confirmation |

| T99 | Length-based vehicle pricing (confirmed 2026-10-18, same pasted batch as T98's buy-menu spec): base price is the shortest length under a given power; each 10cm over that adds 0.5% of the base price, rounded to the nearest £100 with round-half-to-even (verified against every one of the user's own worked examples via a throwaway Node script, which caught a real floating-point bug — `0.005 * 350000` computed as `12250.000000000002` instead of exactly `12250`, breaking the tie-detection for the Kite 11.6m case, which should round to £12,200 not £12,300 — fixed with integer-safe arithmetic). Coaches crossing twin→tri-axle (over 12.9m) also add a flat £15,000, confirmed coach-only, computed dynamically in `priceFor()` (vehicle-catalogue.mts) by comparing the selected length's axle class against that power's own shortest length — never baked into a stored delta, so it can't silently double up if a length's own raw delta is later changed. Applied to every length across the whole catalogue: ADL Enviro400 diesel (10.9m +£4,700, 11.5m +£11,800), Wrightbus StreetDeck diesel (11.0m +£6,200, 11.5m +£14,000), ADL Enviro200 diesel (9.7m +£6,600, 10.8m +£15,700, 11.5m +£21,400 — the round-half-to-even tie case, 11.8m +£23,900) and electric (10.9m +£14,500, 11.7m +£26,100), MCV Evora diesel (12.9m +£16,300), Wrightbus GB Kite electric (11.6m +£12,200 — the tie the float bug affected, 12.5m +£28,000), Volvo 9700 diesel (13.1m +£10,200, 13.9m +£21,800, 15m +£37,700, each plus the dynamic axle charge), Volvo 9700DD diesel (14.8m +£37,800, no axle charge since both its lengths are already tri-axle). **Two real data conflicts resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before coding, not guessed past:** (1) the Yutong E10 and E12 were two separate flat-priced catalogue entries; the user confirmed merging them into one `yutong-e10-e12` entry (same pattern as the GB Kite's own merged electric/hydrogen), E10's 10.9m as the base (no delta) and E12's 12.2m priced via the formula at +£19,500 — `fleet-numbering.mts`'s `BROAD_CLASS_BY_MODEL_ID` updated to the merged id, confirmed via grep that no reference to the old separate ids survived anywhere. (2) the pricing note's own figure for "T15E" at 15.0m was for the wrong length — the real catalogue entry is the Yutong GTe14 at 14m (confirmed by the user: "14m is correct, the 15.0m reference was approximate, not a real correction"), so that given £61,500 figure doesn't apply; the GTe14's own delta was computed fresh from the same verified formula against the real 2m difference (20 × 10cm × 0.5% × £310,000 base = £31,000 raw, no rounding tie), with the +£15,000 axle charge applying automatically via `priceFor()` since 12m is twin-axle and 14m is tri-axle — flagged here as the assistant's own consistent application of the already-confirmed formula to the correct length, not a figure the user gave directly for this specific case. 20 new unit assertions added to `vehicle-catalogue.verify.mts` covering every worked example above (including both round-half-to-even ties and both axle-charge cases), plus a merged-entry check for `yutong-e10-e12`; full project verify suite (10 renderer + 3 electron scripts) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean. **Live-verified via CDP** against an isolated copy of the real save (scratch save + a Windows directory junction onto the real, unmodified `map-data` folder, a unique debug port, killed and cleaned up afterward — the real save was never touched): the Enviro400's 11.5m length showed £246,800, the GB Kite's 11.6m tie-case showed £362,200, and the Yutong coach's GTe14 showed £356,000 (base + £31,000 raw delta + £15,000 axle charge) — all exact matches. | T104 | **Resolves T103's one flagged discrepancy**: the user confirmed "D&E is in the files" — the hand-off's claim that D&E's badge was still pending was simply wrong, no action needed, nothing to fix. **Fare pricing, increment 2 of the fares build** (DESIGN.md §9): before coding, two rounds of `AskUserQuestion` to pin down the exact data model rather than guess — confirmed 28-day tickets get the same city/country/combined 3-way split as Day/Weekly (DESIGN.md's own Lothian reference gives 3 separate Day prices and 3 separate Weekly prices, so this was a real structural question, not a stylistic one); confirmed "beyond the depot group" (plain per-mile) and "long distance" (base + per-mile) are two genuinely separate player-set rates, not one rule read two ways; and — after the user's own correction mid-flow, "Return is 1.6x a single rounded up to the nearest 5p" — Return turned out NOT to need its own flat price or its own per-mile rate at all, just a fixed multiplier applied to whatever the journey's own Single fare already computed to. New `fare-pricing.verify.mts`, 15 assertions including exact Lothian worked-example matches (country bands) and the Return formula's own rounding-up behaviour (not rounding to nearest). The "journey crosses between city and country zones" rule is explicitly NOT implemented — DESIGN.md's own wording doesn't pin down a precise formula and guessing one risked baking in a wrong interpretation. **Fare zone follow-up round, several live user reports in quick succession, each investigated rather than guessed at**: (1) "can I have it so zones snap to zones and so each one can have a colour" — both built (zone-to-zone vertex/edge snapping in screen-space pixels, a per-zone colour column + picker), live-verified via CDP (a custom colour round-tripped, a click 5px from an existing vertex snapped exactly onto it). (2) "I've checked the colour and it can only be set when the zone is made" + "the city/country drop-down is visually wrong" — investigated by measuring real rendered widths live rather than assuming: the per-row type dropdown was rendering at **2.17px wide** (effectively invisible and unclickable) because `.dropdown`'s own `min-width: 0` plus the row's default `flex-shrink: 1` let it get squeezed to nothing next to the name input's `flex: 1` — fixed with a protected `flex: "0 0 110px"`; the colour swatch was a native 12px square, easy to miss entirely — sized up to 32px with a border; both the per-row and draft-form name inputs were missing the `.field` theme class outright, rendering as plain unstyled white boxes, not the app's own dark theme — fixed; the draft form's own type dropdown had no width set at all, shrinking to fit just "city"/"country" next to the full-width name field above it — given `width: 100%` to match. Fixing the dropdown/colour to their real protected widths then clipped the Rename/Delete buttons off the panel's own right edge (confirmed visually) — panel widened 360px→440px. (3) "I meant snapping to roads" + "when snapping to a road it should follow the road until the next point if it is also on a road" — the real architectural gap surfaced and flagged before building rather than assumed away: the only existing pathfinding (`find_route`) is bus-legal-only, with no "any road" routing at all (only a single-point "nearest road" snap already existed, `snap_to_any_road`, already used by depot/dealer placement). Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: build real any-road pathfinding (not restrict to bus-legal roads as a shortcut) and ship road-following without the requested 10m outside-edge offset for now (that's a real polygon-buffering problem — mitred corners, no geometry library in this project, genuine self-intersection risk — deferred as a separate follow-up rather than rushed). **New Rust/WASM work** (`rust/game-wasm/src/lib.rs`): a second CSR adjacency buffer and spatial grid built from *every* edge regardless of bus legality, with every edge traversable in both directions regardless of its real `oneway` tag (a drawn zone boundary isn't a vehicle obeying traffic law, just tracing a road's physical shape) — `nearest_node_any_road`/`dijkstra_any_road` (plain shortest-distance, no turn penalties or junction delays, since those model real driving behaviour that doesn't apply here) and the exposed `find_any_road_route`, deliberately simpler than `find_route`'s own intricate mid-edge-truncation precision engineering (snaps to the nearest *node*, an acceptable small overshoot for a feature whose own click tolerance is already approximate). 3 new Rust tests (an access-restricted road `find_route` refuses but `find_any_road_route` follows; oneway ignored in both directions; two genuinely disconnected road components correctly produce no path) — caught two real bugs in the TESTS themselves before they shipped (float exact-equality on a `0.1` round-trip, and a wrong assumption about `find_route`'s own behaviour in a single-edge graph), not in the new Rust code. Rust: 33/33 (30 + 3 new). WASM rebuilt (`wasm-pack build --target web --release`) and copied into `src/renderer/wasm/`. Wired into the click handler: zone-to-zone snapping still wins outright when it applies; otherwise snaps to the nearest road unconditionally (matching the existing `snap_to_any_road` precedent elsewhere — no distance cutoff, same as depot/dealer placement already have); road-following triggers only when *both* the new click and the immediately preceding point landed on a road, inserting the real intermediate road-geometry points; "Undo point" now pops a whole click's worth (via a new `draftClickPointCounts` stack) rather than one array entry, so undoing a road-followed stretch removes the whole stretch in one go. (4) "when a zone completely surrounds another zone, the bit in the middle should be left blank so the 2 zones dont overlap" — a from-scratch ray-casting point-in-polygon test plus a "every one of B's own vertices sits inside A" containment check (an accepted approximation — doesn't also check for edge-crossing between vertices — judged sufficient for "one zone drawn entirely inside another," the real case asked for); `zonesToGeojson` now gives each zone an interior ring (GeoJSON hole) for every other zone it directly contains, which — as a property of layered rendering, not extra code — also renders deeper nesting (A around B around C) correctly for free. **Live-verified end to end via CDP** against the usual isolated scratch-copy setup (real save never touched, confirmed before and after): road-following produced a real 15-point path from 3 clicks near real Edinburgh streets (not just straight lines between snap points); a drawn "outer" zone around a smaller "inner" one rendered with 2 rings (hole) vs. the inner zone's own 1 (no hole), confirming containment and rendering both work, including on a complex many-vertex outer shape (unintentionally produced by road-snapping always being active, regardless of zoom level — not a bug, a correct consequence of `snap_to_any_road` working in map space, not screen space); the dropdown/colour fixes measured directly (88-110px and 32px respectively, not 2px/12px). `tsc --noEmit` and the full verify suite (12 renderer + 6 electron scripts, including the new `fare-pricing.verify.mts`) clean throughout. | T93 | Picked back up a loose end from earlier in this session, never logged at the time: "in Sconser the bus isn't going into the bus stop and stays on the main road" (route 52's "Ferry Road End" stop, Sconser — the real Raasay ferry village). Investigated fresh with real data rather than trusting the old half-finished notes. **Root-caused precisely**: the real stop sits ~1m from a genuine one-way service-road loop (OSM way 985620800, `access=private`+`bus=yes`, correctly routable per T92's own access logic) that peels off the main A87 trunk road and rejoins it a little further on. The loop's own legal direction only lets a bus *enter* from one end and *exit* the other. The main trunk road happens to reach the loop's own *exit* node directly, more cheaply than detouring through the loop at all — and since that exit node is also *geometrically closer* to the stop's raw coordinate than the loop's entry node is, `nearest_node`'s own plain-distance tie-break (used whenever both candidate endpoints are otherwise equally valid) picked the exit node as the route's own goal. Dijkstra then correctly found the cheapest way to reach *that* node — straight along the trunk road, never touching the loop — and the goal-side extension correctly refused to fabricate an illegal reverse move back into a one-way road to reach the real stop. The bus stopped dead on the main road, 24.2m short of the real stop, with no error anywhere. **Fixed at the source, not patched around**: `nearest_node`'s `needs_outgoing`-only (route start) and `needs_incoming`-only (route goal) tie-breaks now prefer whichever endpoint the edge's own oneway direction actually permits a legal approach into/out of, when plain reachability doesn't already decide it — mirroring the existing "prefer a node that can do both" preference already used for interior waypoints (T78), just applied to the route's own true start/goal for the first time. Guarded with a new `ALREADY_AT_NODE_M` (5m) threshold so this never overrides a genuine exact match at the "wrong" endpoint — caught directly by a first version breaking this session's own T91 regression test, which had an exact match at one endpoint and a wildly distant point at the other; without the guard, the extendability preference wrongly chose the distant-but-legally-forward endpoint over the trivially-correct exact one. New regression test (`goal_mid_edge_on_a_one_way_loop_prefers_the_endpoint_that_can_legally_reach_it`) proves the fix directly: a synthetic one-way loop with a cheaper bypass edge reaching its exit node first, same shape as the real Sconser case. Full suite green (89 total: 30 game-wasm incl. the new test, 58 pipeline, 1 game-data). **Verified against the real route**: native replay of route 52's actual 7 points now ends exactly 1.1m from the real stop (was 24.2m), correctly walking into the loop's own real curve geometry first; a live screenshot shows the route visibly leaving the main road and detouring into the layby, not running straight past it. WASM rebuilt, renderer rebuilt, `tsc --noEmit` clean, new installer built and boot-tested clean via CDP | Claude Code | Real router bug found and fixed at the root, verified natively (1.1m vs 24.2m) and live with a screenshot — awaiting the user's own confirmation |
| T62 | Live user request (2026-09-28), mid-turn while T61 was being finished: "Can you also place a temporary marker where all dealerships are and a temporary way for me to add all the dealers and there entrances/exits please that once done will then fix there places into the game, I think the code should be left in for this one so I can add more in future If I need to." Explicitly NOT a one-off/throwaway tool like T55's Portree bypass injection — the user wants the placement UI kept permanently so more dealers can be added later. Real-world dealer network is CLAUDE.md's own reference: "the dealer network (Volvo, Alexander Dennis, Western Commercial, and the Wrightbus/Yutong nearest-depot collection rule)," scoped to Phase 9 in the build order and detailed further in UK-EXPANSION.md §2. **Built 2026-10-02, both halves.** Checked UK-EXPANSION.md §2 before designing rather than assuming the depot-placement pattern transferred directly, then confirmed three real design questions with the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessing: (1) dealer entrances use the same entry/exit/both mode as depot entrances (not a simpler mode-less access point), (2) manufacturer is a fixed dropdown (volvo/adl/western_commercial/wrightbus/yutong), not free text — needed now since Phase 9's collection rule is manufacturer-specific, (3) a dealer belongs to no depot group (independent, since any operator can buy from any dealer) unlike a depot. **Permanent tool**: `dealer-placement.ts`, a close structural mirror of `depot-placement.ts` (site marker, draft panel, junction-hint dots, per-entrance mode dropdown, "+ Add entrance", delete) with a manufacturer dropdown replacing the depot-group dropdown and a violet site marker (`#8b5cf6`) distinct from the depot's amber. New `dealers`/`dealer_entrances` tables (schema v22→v23, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` — no ALTER needed, brand new tables) with the identical bearing/road_width_m/size_scale columns and manual-nudge mechanism as depot_entrances. Backend (`db.mts`), IPC (`main.ts`), and preload bridge (`window.dealers`) all mirror the depots API 1:1. Before writing dealer-placement.ts, pulled the shared, bug-prone entrance-marker logic (icon registration, mode colours, the nudge-button row and its bearing-rotation maths) out of depot-placement.ts into a new `entrance-marker.ts` module — CLAUDE.md's own "build it once as a shared component, not three times" principle, since repaint shops (T63) are a likely third user of the identical mechanic. depot-placement.ts refactored to import from it too, re-verified clean (`tsc --noEmit`, full `.verify.mts` suite) after the refactor before building on top of it. The two tools' map-click handlers check each other's placement state one-directionally (dealer-placement checks `placeDepotState`, matching the existing precedent where each newer tool defers to earlier ones — depot-placement already checks `routeDrawState`/`placeStopState` the same way) rather than adding a circular mutual import. **Temporary reference-pin layer** (the literal, repeated, most urgent part of the request — "place rough temporary locations/dots on the map"): a separate `dealer-reference-pins.ts`, explicitly marked for deletion once every real dealer is placed, toggled via its own "Show dealer reference pins (temporary)" button. All ~66 real UK-EXPANSION.md §2 locations (Volvo/Yutong/Wrightbus plus the Loughborough repaint shop) resolved to real coordinates — NOT guessed — via a throwaway Rust example querying the whole-UK settlements data built during T55-map-boundary (`pipeline-data-uk-expansion-verify/settlements.bin`, never swapped into the live Scotland-only pipeline-data/), deleted after its output was embedded as a static TS array. 64 of 66 resolved by locality name; the remaining two (Hayes, Deeside — not in the settlements data, likely tagged as an OSM "suburb" which that dataset doesn't decode) were decoded directly from their own real Plus Codes via a from-scratch Open Location Code decoder (no local package available, Nominatim doesn't resolve plus codes either — confirmed by trying it directly, not assumed), **validated against a real known case before being trusted**: decoding Castleford's own Plus Code landed 1.8km from its already-verified real town centre, well within the town, confirming the decoder's correctness before using it on Hayes/Deeside. Each reference pin is coloured by manufacturer and clickable for a label popup; real dot positions confirmed via live CDP screenshots across Scotland, Northern Ireland and northern England (pins render correctly even where the basemap itself has no tile detail, e.g. outside the Scotland-only PMTiles coverage, since they're independent lon/lat markers). **Both halves live-verified via CDP against the real packaged app**: created a throwaway test dealer with a real road-snapped entrance, opened its popup in the live app, confirmed all UI elements render (manufacturer dropdown, entrance mode dropdown, 6 nudge buttons, Add entrance, Delete dealer), clicked a nudge button and changed the manufacturer dropdown live, confirmed both persisted correctly via a direct data query, then deleted the test dealer — the real save's own pre-existing Portree depot was confirmed untouched throughout. Rust (game-data crate, used only for the one-off lookup, no game-wasm changes needed this round — snap_entrance_to_junction/nearby_junctions were reused unchanged), `tsc --noEmit` clean, full TS suite green (9 verify files), built, packaged, exe rebuilt 2026-10-02. **Update (2026-10-02): reference pins corrected to the real Plus Code position for all 68 entries, not just the 2 with no settlement-name match.** Direct user correction: "The reference pins should be on the plus code locations" — the first version used a town-centre settlement-name lookup for 64 of 66 entries, which was a rougher approximation than the real data actually supports. Re-decoded every single dealer's own Plus Code (not just Hayes/Deeside) with the same validated Open Location Code decoder, using each one's already-resolved settlement coordinate purely as the short-code recovery reference point (not as the final position). Checked the result wasn't silently wrong before trusting it on all 68: every decoded point landed within 0.4–12.3km of its own reference town — plausible real industrial-estate-from-town-centre distances, nowhere near the ~100km+ a recovery bug would produce. `locality` field replaced with `plusCode` (shown in each pin's popup) since settlement-name matching is no longer part of the positioning logic at all. Live-verified via CDP: Glasgow's pin now sits visibly several km southwest of the city-centre label, consistent with a real industrial dealer site rather than the town centre. `tsc --noEmit` clean, built, packaged, exe rebuilt 2026-10-02. **Not yet checked by the user** — ask them to open "Place dealer," place one real dealer using a reference pin as a guide, and confirm the manufacturer dropdown/entrances all behave as expected before relying on it for the rest.

**Update (2026-10-02): entrance/exit markers simplified to plain dots, the whole oriented-marker mechanism removed.** Direct user instruction: "I think entrances and exits should not be shown on the map, only dots on the road to show where entrances and exits are when placing depots and dealerships as they are constantly breaking. We should remove all code that isnt needed other than for the basic functionality." This was the entire T61 saga's accumulated complexity (bearing-rotated semicircle icon, road-width-based icon scaling, the manual nudge mechanism built specifically to compensate for both) — all removed outright rather than left dormant. **Rust**: `snap_entrance_to_junction` shrunk from returning `[lon, lat, bearing, road_width_m]` to just `[lon, lat]` — the junction's own raw position, no pull-back, no bearing computation at all (a plain dot has no orientation to get wrong, so the whole "kerb offset vs. pixel-width zoom mismatch" problem that drove T61's many rounds simply doesn't apply anymore). `ENTRANCE_PULL_BACK_M`/`move_point_along_bearing` deleted. `nearby_junctions` (the hint-dot layer, shown while choosing where to click) simplified from `[lon, lat, max_width_m, ...]` triples to plain `[lon, lat, ...]` pairs — its own road-width-based sizing (a separate, working T61 fix, "scale don't filter ambiguous candidates") was removed too, consistent with the same "basic functionality only" instruction, since the literal basic need is just a clickable candidate dot. `node_max_width_m` field deleted entirely (nothing left to use it). Rust 22/22 (3 obsolete tests rewritten to check plain `[lon, lat]`, 1 width-reporting test deleted outright since the feature it tested no longer exists). **Schema v23→v24**: `bearing`/`road_width_m`/`size_scale` columns dropped from both `depot_entrances` and `dealer_entrances` via `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN` (confirmed node:sqlite's bundled SQLite supports this before relying on it). `moveDepotEntrance`/`setDepotEntranceSizeScale`/`moveDealerEntrance`/`setDealerEntranceSizeScale` and their IPC/preload bridges deleted outright. **Renderer**: the SDF semicircle icon, `buildEntranceIconWidthScale`, and the 6-button nudge row all deleted from `entrance-marker.ts` (now just the mode labels/colours, ~20 lines down from ~170) — `depot-placement.ts`/`dealer-placement.ts`'s entrance layers changed from `symbol` (rotated icon) to plain `circle` (fixed 6px radius, coloured by mode), matching the site markers' own existing style. `db.verify.mts`'s now-invalid nudge assertions removed. A broad repo-wide grep confirmed zero leftover references to any removed symbol before shipping. Screenshot confirms the entrance renders as a plain blue dot, not a semicircle. exe rebuilt 2026-10-02. **Ask the user to confirm this looks and feels right** — this closes out the fragile part of T61/T62 for good; if dots still look wrong, it would be a very different, much simpler kind of bug than the orientation/scaling issues chased before.

Two changes: (1) the `depot-entrances`/`dealer-entrances` map sources are now owned exclusively by new `showEntrancesForDepot`/`showEntrancesForDealer` (populate) and `hideEntrances` (clear) functions, called from `openDepotPopup`/`openDealerPopup` and the popup's own "close" handler respectively — the entrance dots are empty by default and only populate while a given depot/dealer's popup is actually open, confirmed live via `queryRenderedFeatures` (0 before click, 1 while the popup is open, 0 again after closing). (2) The junction-hint dot layer (`depot-junction-hints`/`dealer-junction-hints`, `refreshJunctionHints`/`clearJunctionHints`/`nearestHintToClick`) removed entirely from both placement files — a click now calls `router.snap_entrance_to_junction` directly with the raw click coordinates, no hint-dot re-aiming. **Update (2026-10-02): the exact Plus Codes arrived, decoded, and the whole feature levelled up — real dealers are now auto-seeded, the reference layer is a live checklist.** User supplied real data directly: "Alexander Dennis - 254F+F2 Falkirk" and "Western Commercial - VM4Q+J8 Glasgow, WHR2+86 Broxburn, RXH4+58 Bellshill, & F4M5+PW Dundee." Decoded all 5 with the same validated Open Location Code decoder (drift from each town reference: 1.4–5.5km, consistent with every other entry in the file) and added them to `REFERENCE_PINS` — all 5 manufacturers now have at least one real position. In the same exchange the user asked for two related upgrades: (1) "if a dealer is placed within the dealer reference then the dealer reference should turn green," then refined moments later to (2) "add all dealers exactly where there plus codes place them but make the reference show on the map until I have added entrance and/or exits" — read as (2) superseding (1)'s manual-placement model entirely, not adding to it. Built: new `seedRealDealers()` (idempotent, matched by name — safe to call on every launch) creates every real dealer in `REFERENCE_PINS` directly in the `dealers` table at its own precise decoded position, skipping the Loughborough "repaint" entry (not a real dealer, no manufacturer the table's CHECK constraint accepts — T63's concern). This is NOT a bug in this change — it's the already-known, already-documented T55-map-boundary situation (the whole-UK map data was built and proven working back in September but deliberately never swapped into the live game pending the user's own sign-off, since it's a large, hard-to-reverse jump in data size/memory/load time).
